| DEC-146 | **The cash comparison's two tiers: a cash difference pauses on the second consecutive run out of the band** ([#205](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/205), the coordinator's ruling on round 1). Trading-domain spec §11's cash row is "alert above threshold; pause if persistent" and does not say what persistent is. `mandate-executor` counts consecutive reconciliation runs whose broker cash falls outside the band (`0.01 × fill notional since the last broker cash snapshot + accrued unposted fees`, around a model of the last broker cash plus the fills' cash since): the first alerts the owner and pauses nobody, and the **second** consecutive one pauses every agent under `reconciliation:cash` until the owner acknowledges `cash`. A run inside the band starts the count again. The count is folded from each recorded `AccountSnapshotRecorded` (`cash_in_band`), so it survives the re-anchor that snapshot makes and a restart | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; reversible) | Two is the most conservative count that still tells one run's drift from a persistent one: one would pause on every drift, which the spec's two tiers rule out, and a single false positive would stop every exit on the account (#205 review, round 1, finding 2) | Pause at once (the spec's two tiers collapsed into one); a count kept in memory (lost at each re-anchor and restart); a time window rather than a run count (runs are the unit §11 compares at) |
| DEC-147 | **An asset-denominated crypto fee is journaled as `FeesCharged` with `family: "crypto_asset"`, held in the asset, never as USD** ([#205](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/205), the coordinator's ruling on round 1; settles the backlog's "Decide before E7-3's cash slice" row from #197). The event keeps the shape the pending tests and RC-07 fold: `family: "crypto_asset"`, an `instrument`, `accrued` and `charged` in the asset's quantity, and no `day`. `mandate-executor` reduces the position by the accrued fee, holds the part not yet posted per instrument in `asset_fees`, and adds it back to the model quantity when §11 compares the crypto position; it never enters the USD unposted fees, so it is not an accrued liability (§6.3) and is outside the cash band and the fee comparison (§11's crypto row) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; reversible) | It matches §6.3 (asset-denominated fees are not accrued USD liabilities) and §11's crypto row (unposted asset fees explain a position difference), and it is what the two pending tests already journal, so no tests correction is needed | No `FeesCharged` at all, recording the fee only in the position (loses the record of what was charged and when it posted); accruing it as USD (what §6.3 forbids) |
| DEC-148 | **E10-6 is pulled forward, and stays optional and never the main path** ([DEC-141](#decisions), [strategy options](../product/10-strategy-options.md#7-recommendation)). (1) **Placement:** E10-6 moves to M8 as the first story after the M8 work it needs, ahead of M8's other Should stories (for example E9-5), and it does not wait for M9's web app or M10's channels. It starts once these have landed: the owner-input API (M8's workspace services that take the owner's input: the mandate registry, the approval service, and the deployment manager's owner controls), E9-1 sign-in, E9-2 roles (the client's scoped, revocable token), E9-4 step-up authentication, and E10-3 mandate versions with step-up. Its earlier dependencies land before the Phase 1 gate: the journal (E5), the order builder and autonomy rules (E6-2), the risk gate (E6-3), the executor and account ledger (E7-2, E7-3, E7-5), and the tracer bullet (E7-7), where they first run end to end. (2) **It cannot come earlier.** Phase 1 (M5 to M7) has no owner-input API beyond the founder's CLI control (M7), and the token and step-up that E10-6's acceptance requires are M8 work; bringing them into Phase 1 would change milestone order, which this decision does not do. (3) **Its role is unchanged.** It remains an optional channel and an adoption on-ramp, never the only or the main path. DEC-141's order stands: the complete product leads, and the platform's research agent (DEC-97) brings the ideas. (4) Everything else in E10-6's acceptance and DEC-141's boundaries (1) to (6) is unchanged | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27); amended by DEC-183 (a primary distribution path, no longer "never the main path"), 2026-09-30 | The founder: "we can make it forward as long as we are saying it is not the only or main path". Research on 2026-09-27: brokers now open order entry to third-party agents while disclaiming supervision. Per the broker's announcement, Robinhood's agentic trading (beta from 2026-05-27) "does not control, supervise, monitor, recommend, or audit these AI agents" [RH1]; per the broker's announcement as reported, Coinbase for Agents (2026-06-11) will add custom limits such as maximum trade size and spend later [CB1]. Owners who already run agents need enforced limits now, so the on-ramp is worth having early. Sources are in the [competitive landscape](../product/03-competitive-landscape.md#sources) | Keeping E10-6 behind the other Should stories (loses the on-ramp while brokers leave limits to others); moving it into Phase 1 (needs M8's identity and step-up first, which would change milestone order); making it the lead product (weakens DEC-97, as DEC-141 already rejected) |
| DEC-149 | **Mandate has two layers that evolve together: the harness, sold to enterprises, and the retail platform, which runs through it** ([harness engineering research](../product/11-harness-engineering.md), [strategy options](../product/10-strategy-options.md#positioning-harness-and-platform-dec-149)). (1) **The harness** is the gate, the autonomy rules, the journal, the executor, the connectors, the conformance suite, and the MCP channel. It is sold to enterprises: brokers, fintechs, and teams building agents. (2) **The retail platform** runs through that same harness and has no private path around it: every platform order passes the same builder, autonomy rules, gate, account ledger, and journal an enterprise customer's would. (3) **Revenue expectation.** Retail brings some revenue and proves the harness on real owners' accounts; enterprise is expected to be the main revenue. The founder expects retail to be hard to win. (4) **DEC-141 is amended, not reversed.** Its product decisions still hold: Mandate stays the complete product, the platform's research agent (DEC-97) brings the ideas inside the owner's envelope, and the owner's own agent stays an optional channel (E10-6, whose placement DEC-148 sets). What changes is that the harness is a first-class enterprise product, not only the platform's engine. (5) **Consequence.** Harness interfaces (the gate's inputs and verdicts, the autonomy rules, the journal format and verifier, the executor's intents, the connector contract, the conformance suite, and the MCP channel) are designed as product surfaces for third parties: stable, versioned, documented, and conformance-tested. (6) **No plan change here.** Milestone order does not change. Enterprise stories go into the backlog as proposals (epic E18, [enterprise harness](06-backlog-v1.md#enterprise-harness-proposed-dec-149)), not scheduled. (7) **Build for all three segments** (the founder: "could we not build for all?"; "it is just you and me … there is no cost to you running other than what I already pay"). The shared core is built once for brokers, fintechs, and agent builders, and each segment's packaging is built alongside it, not deferred until a buyer asks, because build capacity is agents under the founder's existing subscription; pace is limited by review and merge throughput, the plan's usage limits, and the founder's decisions. A self-serve entry for agent builders is the demand test. Which enterprise customer buys first stays open for discovery; customer outreach, and anything that costs money or needs counsel (a SOC 2 audit, broker agreements, legal text), needs the founder's authorization (DEC-79) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27) | The founder: "I want it both to evolve along side each other, harness is for enterprise and platform runs through the harness. Retail would bring some money, real money would be in the enterprise, retail is hard to crack." Research on 2026-09-27 found no well-adopted open-source guardrail harness for trading agents, and generic harnesses and MCP gateways enforce stateless per-call rules with no model of positions, P&L, drawdown, settlement, or budgets (inference, [harness engineering §3 and §4](../product/11-harness-engineering.md#3-trading-specific-findings)). Running the platform through the harness means every retail order exercises the enterprise product, so the platform's record is the harness's evidence | Retail platform only, with the harness as an internal engine (DEC-141 as it stood); harness only, dropping retail (loses the proving ground on real owners' accounts); a separate platform path around the harness (two routes to the gate to secure and review) |
| DEC-150 | **Stream G's `mandate-risk` readings for E6-6 and its tests correction** ([#217](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/217), the coordinator's ruling on round 1; claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123)). (1) **The `kind: gate` harness drives the full `evaluate`, amending [DEC-129](#decisions) item 19.** Item 19 had the harness run only `reference/mandate/ref.py`'s `gate` subset (the working universe, concentration, order size, the cooldown, orders per day, gross exposure); `crates/mandate-risk/tests/refcases.rs` has always driven the full gate instead, and that is wanted, because a mandate case then also proves the gate's other checks let its allow through. Where the full gate is rightly stricter than the case, the case goes on `FULL_GATE_ONLY`, a list named case by case, never by rule: each entry names the one check outside the subset that decides it and the `computed` keys that check stops the gate before reaching, and the harness asserts that checks 1 and 2 pass, that the named check is the only failure, that the reached figures match and the unreached ones are absent, and that the entry has expired once the full verdict matches the case. The one entry is `MC-G02`, whose increase beside a working opening order in the same instrument trading-domain §5.3 rule 6 denies `working_order_limit` at check 4, so it gives up its own `gross` and `gross_limit`. **The window this opens:** `MC-G04` and `MC-G06` still pin gross exposure, but on a denial; the only other allow case that states `gross`, `MC-G13`, stays pending on E6-8's fail-closed stub, so until E6-8 lands no live reference case pins `gross` on an allow path, and the in-module tests of `gate.rs` (`the_account_bound_counts_other_agents_working_orders`, `the_agent_gross_limit_is_on_agent_equity`) are what hold it meanwhile. **E6-6's readings** (E6-6, [#221](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/221), [task brief](tasks/E6-3-risk-gate.md), DEC-129), where trading-domain spec §5.3, §9.2, §9.4 and §9.5 leave the gate's shape open. (2) **§5.3 rules 5 and 6 (`working_order_limit`) deny openings and increases only, never an exit, at either pass.** §9.6's control table lists "one side at a time; one working non-protective order per instrument per account" as §5.3 rules 5–6, §9.6 says "these controls deny opening and increasing orders" and exempts risk exits and protective orders, and `AGENTS.md` rule 13 says risk reduction is never denied by conduct controls; §5.3's closing paragraph ("the gate re-run immediately before submission applies every rule in full") is read for rules 4 and 8 and for an opening, since applying rules 5 and 6 to an exit at the re-run would deny what rule 13 forbids denying. Rule 4 (`sell_exceeds_available`) is not a conduct control and binds every reduction, excluding the agent's own protective orders at the first pass only; rule 8 (`add_blocked_by_protective_order`, §5.4) binds a plain or IOC US-equity opening beside any resting protective order in the instrument, a bracket being its own tranche and crypto following DEC-36's sequence. (3) **A US-equity opening that asks for `extended_hours` is `extended_hours_opening_not_allowed` in every session**, the regular one included: the broker keeps such an order working into after-hours, where v1 opens nothing (§4.3, DEC-37). (4) **§5.3 rule 2's increment half and rule 7 fail closed like its minimum size** (DEC-129 item 27): an opening below `min_order_size`, a fractional quantity in an instrument that is not fractionable, and a fractional equity opening that is not TIF day or is a bracket (§5.2) are refused with `GateError::Unimplemented` naming DEC-129 item 27, since none has a registered code and a denial without one is unrepresentable; an exit is never refused for them, the broker judging its size and a whole-position sell being always valid. (5) **A market-order exit outside the regular session or in an auction window is re-priced, never denied**: §5.1 allows market orders only in the regular session outside the auction windows, and the gate treats the rest of the day and both windows as it treats a halt under DEC-129 items 28 and 31, allowing the exit with `Pacing::marketable_limit_required`; `auction_window` denies a market opening only ([DEC-159](#decisions), amending DEC-129 item 18). (6) **A negative fee reservation counts as zero** at check 7, since it could only loosen the bound; the reservation stays the caller's input (DEC-129 item 16), computed through `mandate-accounting`'s fee rules by the executor. (7) **The `legacy_pdt` budget applies to a margin account only** (§9.2: a day trade is "in a margin account"), and `required`'s "working same-day opening orders in other instruments" counts every agent's on the account, since the budget is the account's. (8) **A US-equity instant outside every calendar session (a weekend, a holiday) is `Overnight`**, the session in which v1 opens nothing, rather than a guess or an error; a date outside the committed calendar is `ConfigOutOfRange` (DEC-129 item 9). | Item 1 Accepted (coordinator, under DEC-79); items 2 to 8 Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Item 1: The full gate is the one production runs, so a reference case that passes only through a subset would prove less; a named, self-expiring exception keeps every other case at full strength. Items 2 to 8: Each reading takes the side of `AGENTS.md` rules 3 and 13: an opening is denied or refused wherever the spec is silent, and no exit is denied by a rule the spec lists as a conduct or session control | A `mandate_limits` entry point for the subset (public API beyond #136, and a second path the production gate never takes); a founder re-scope of `MC-G02` (founder-owned case file, and it would lose the "cap met exactly" boundary beside a working order); a rule such as "skip the verdict when check 2 passes" (would also exempt `MC-G04`, `MC-G06` and `MC-G07`, whose own denials sit at checks 6 and 7); items 2 to 8: Apply rules 5 and 6 to exits at the re-run (denies a risk exit by a conduct control, against rule 13); mint codes for rules 2 and 7 (breaks ES-09 until the founder registers them); deny a market exit after hours (a session rule denying an exit, against MI-1) |
| DEC-151 | **How `Mandate::parse` reads the schema where the schema, `jsonschema`, and the Rust types part ways** (E10-1 implementation, slice S; [task brief](tasks/M5-F-mandate-spec.md), DEC-128 item 3). (1) **Condition depth.** `$defs/condition` is recursive with no bound, so `jsonschema` accepts any depth. The parse reads to depth 5, one level past V-017's limit of 4, so V-017 still reports the first level too deep (MC-V24), and refuses depth 6 as `too_deep` without recursing further. A document that deep breaks V-017 anyway, so no valid mandate is refused; only the stage and the code of the rejection differ, and no MC-S case nests. (2) **Pattern anchors follow ECMA-262**, the regular-expression dialect JSON Schema specifies: `$` is the end of the text. `jsonschema` 4.26 uses Python's `re.search`, where `$` also matches before a final newline, so it accepts `"0.08\n"` in a decimal field; the Rust parse refuses it, and must, since that text is not canonical and its round trip would break. A differential fuzz of 51,000 mutated fixture documents found no other disagreement. **Recommendation:** the reference harness validates patterns with a full-match (for example a `jsonschema` validator whose `pattern` keyword uses `re.fullmatch`), in its own ES-22 change. (3) **Dates.** The schema's `date` is a pattern, not a calendar, so `2026-02-30` (MC-V22) and `0000-01-01` parse; the typed goal reads such an `end_date` as no date, and its text stays in the document for V-015. (4) **The canonical form is the value parsed**, kept whole in a private field, not the typed fields written back: those hold set-like arrays as sorted sets (V-009 must see the order the owner wrote) and have no value for an invalid date. Because the fields are public, `canonical`, `canonical_bytes`, and `version` re-parse the kept value and refuse with `diverged` when the fields no longer match it, so a version never names anything but the mandate the gate enforces. (5) **Two parse codes** join ES-09's list: `not_unique` (a `uniqueItems` array repeats an item; the pointer names the repeat) and `diverged`. (6) **Strings on a `decimal` branch.** A signal-model parameter string in the `decimal` grammar is `ParamValue::Decimal` and any other string, `-0` and `0.020` among them, is `ParamValue::Text`, as the schema's `anyOf` allows; a condition value string on a decimal field in the `decimal` grammar is `ConditionValue::Decimal`, and any other string is text for V-023 to judge, as stream H's harness reads it. (7) **ES-13.** The parse is one unit: every family-S test parses a whole document, so no smaller slice turns a test live without dead code. It lands at about 660 non-blank, non-doc src lines (DEC-140 is the precedent). **The coordinator ruled on #225 in round 1 to accept it as one change**, because the review confirmed the parse does not split without dead code | Items 1 to 6 Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79); item 7 Accepted (coordinator, #225 round 1); item 2's recommendation proposed to the coordinator | Each item keeps the Rust parse and `jsonschema` in agreement wherever the schema is unambiguous, keeps the version hash equal to the bytes the owner confirmed, and never lets a document past the parse that a V-rule could not then see | An unbounded recursive parse; matching Python's `$`; rejecting calendar-invalid dates at the parse (breaks MC-V22); re-serialising the typed fields (loses order and invalid dates); private fields with accessors (the tests PR made them public) |
| DEC-152 | **How the E6-2 autonomy slice reads §6.2 where the spec and DEC-130 leave a choice** (stream H, claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125)). (1) **The whole rule set is re-checked before any rule is read:** unique ids, then per comparison in tree order V-017's depth, V-018's reserved field and V-023's type rules, the first failure reported; a malformed rule behind a matching one still refuses the opening action, and the re-check never runs for a reducing purpose (MI-1, `AGENTS.md` rule 13). (2) **`decide` classifies by side:** a sell is the built-in AUTO whatever its label, because long only (DEC-32) it can only reduce and the gate re-derives its purpose (§6.1); a buy labelled with a reducing purpose, or whose two labels disagree, is the new refusal `mislabelled_buy` rather than a built-in AUTO. (3) **A `two_approver_above_usd` wider than `Usd` refuses the action** rather than rounding it across the order value (DEC-130 item 8). (4) **`Condition::matches` evaluates every child of `all` and `any`**, so an unevaluatable comparison is an error wherever it sits, and a missing fact or an operator the value's type does not admit is `invalid_input` naming the field; this is the implementation DEC-128 items 18 and 21 assign to stream H's implementation PR | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each reading errs toward refusing an opening action, which adds no risk (`AGENTS.md` rule 3), and none can refuse, pace or ask a reducing one | Walking only the rules the first match reaches; a sell labelled `open` classified by the rules; rounding the threshold; short-circuit `all` and `any`, which let a sibling hide an unevaluatable comparison |
| DEC-154 | **How the family-N harness reads its cases until stream F's parser and stream H's `classify` land** (claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), DEC-77 stage 4, DEC-132's case-loading design). (1) **The envelope** is projected from the case's patched base JSON onto `mandate_research::MandateEnvelope` field by field, because `mandate_spec::Mandate::parse` is still a stub; it moves to F's parse with DEC-132 item 24. (2) **The output envelope's model identity** is the base's admitting signal model, the first model when none admits; no check reads it. The source allowlist's version is `AllowlistVersion(1)`, which no case states and no check reads. (3) **No family-N case states a policy**, so the overlay is `PolicyOverlay::permissive()`, as `ref.py`'s `admit` applies none. (4) **An input `lineage_instruments`**, which `ref.py`'s fold reads but no case states, is rejected by the sweep rather than read. (5) **Comparison:** `working_universe` compares as a set with a repeat failing (MI-15), because the crate holds a `BTreeSet` and `ref.py` returns a refusal's input in authored order; `removed`, `offsets`, and every journal compare in order, rows whole. (6) **`first_order_autonomy`:** where it is stated, the case compares everything else and then fails naming E6-2; where it is `null`, admission must report no first-order facts. `admission_action` is swept, but no passing case reads its values, so E6-2's `classify` must be wired to them. (7) **Delivery:** three stacked slices for ES-13 (`thesis_expiry` and `stagger`, `admission`, `lineage`), and the one assertion in `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/mandate_harness.rs` that expected family N to fail moves out in its own tests-correction PR, since DEC-77 item 2 bars the harness PR from `tests/` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Every choice reads the fixture as the reference does or more strictly, and none adds a key nobody compares (DEC-85); the in-module oracle doctors every expected member and requires the case to fail | Wait for F's parser and H's `classify` before any family-N case runs; compare `working_universe` in order (fails on refusals, whose order is authored) |
| DEC-157 | **How the E7-7 tests PR builds `mandate-shell`** ([task brief](tasks/E7-7-tracer-bullet.md), DEC-138, stream L). (1) **The harness is real and the adapters are stubs.** The effect runner, the stage traits, the total mappings, the journal envelope, the deterministic ids, the host controls and the command line are code in the tests PR and under the mutation gate (DEC-137 item 3), because the brief's keystone case — every stage a permissive double — must place exactly one order, and a stubbed runner cannot; every production adapter (`adapters.rs`) is a stub answering `Cause::Unimplemented`. This amends the brief's stage-2 row, which listed the effect runner among the stubs. (2) **`Stage::Reconcile` runs at startup**, after the signal and before sizing, because the runtime holds every opening until a clean `ReconciliationRun` is folded (DEC-131 item 13); its failure is `reconciliation_unavailable` and a mismatch is `reconciliation_mismatch`. (3) **Each per-stage case asserts at the furthest boundary its stage could reach** (TI-2): zero submissions for every stage, zero sink hands for stages up to `Sink`, zero `IntentProposed` up to `Journal`, zero `OrderSubmitted` up to `Executor`, that the stub was reached, and that no stage downstream of it was. The brief's loop asserted zero hands and zero `IntentProposed` for every stage, which cannot hold downstream of the stages that produce them. (4) **A `Long` signal is one model output of conviction 1 at confidence 1**, expiring after the model's `max_output_age_s`; `Flat` and `Undecided` produce none (`no_long_signal`). The model id is `quant.ma_crossover`: the brief's `ma-crossover` does not match the schema's `^(fast|llm|quant)\.[a-z0-9_]{1,48}$`. (5) **Deterministic ids are 26 decimal digits** — the id space, then the epoch, head and ordinal zero-padded to 5, 14 and 6 places — which is the ULID alphabet; a part too large makes the id longer than 26, which the journal refuses, never a truncation into another id. (6) **A planning run keeps no journal**: the CLI refuses `--journal` without `--place-one-order`, and `--place-one-order` without `--journal`. A planning run's `IntentProposed` would otherwise be re-handed by the next start and could be submitted then. This amends the brief's manual-run command, which passed a DSN to a planning run. (7) **The in-module journal double checks head and epoch only**, and the committed-draft ledger parses bytes with `mandate_canon::parse`: `mandate_journal::Draft::parse` registers no payload schema for any agent-stream event, and its `StreamOpened` accepts only an account stream, so no agent draft can be appended to a real journal yet (a finding for the journal's owners, reported on the PR). (8) **No golden journal file in this PR**: its bytes depend on every stub. TI-10 is held by a byte-identity check across two independent runs, and the shadow order book rebuilds the intended order from the committed bytes; a golden file comes with a tests-correction PR once the path is real. (9) **The advisory mapping of `Defer` and `Hold`**: allowed for a reducing purpose, because the dry run can only narrow and narrowing an exit away would make an advisory pass the reason an exit did not go out (`AGENTS.md` rule 13); denied for an opening. (10) **The shell implements no `TradingTransport` anywhere, tests included**: the in-module submission counter sits at the `Connector` stage and the end-to-end one in `tests/`, so the source scan forbids the impl across `src/` rather than exempting `#[cfg(test)]` (stronger than the brief's scanner). (11) **The binary passes no transport in this PR**, so it reads no credential; the implementation PR constructs `AlpacaPaperHttp` from `Credentials::from_env`. The optional key-prefix check (the brief's control 4) is left out. (12) **One order per run**: a second submission in one run refuses with `one_order_only`. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each keeps the brief's invariants and oracles and closes a gap its text left; none touches live money, spending, legal text, or a safety invariant | Stub the runner (the keystone cannot place an order, which the brief's own stop conditions forbid); keep `ma-crossover` (fails schema validation); let a planning run keep a journal |
| DEC-159 | **An auction window re-prices a market-order exit; it denies only a market opening** (the coordinator's ruling on [#221](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/221), round 1, blocker 1; claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123)). Amends [DEC-129](#decisions) item 18. Trading-domain §4.3 says "no opening orders in either [window]; exits in them use limit orders, never market orders". Item 18 read the second clause as a check-3 denial of every market order, a risk exit's included, so the gate would deny risk reduction by a session rule, against MI-1 and `AGENTS.md` rule 13. Now `auction_window` denies a market **opening** (open or increase) in the opening auction or the closing ten minutes, and a market-order **exit** of any purpose in either window is allowed with `Pacing::marketable_limit_required`, sent as a marketable limit at its proposed quantity and price, exactly as items 28 and 31 treat a halt. Check 3 still reads the session before the window (§9.1), so a market opening in the opening auction (pre-market) is `session_not_allowed`; the reachable `auction_window` denial is a market opening in the closing ten minutes, and a limit opening there stays check 6's `close_window` (item 18's other half, unchanged). `tests/hand.rs::an_auction_window_denies_a_market_opening_and_reprices_a_market_exit` pins both halves. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | It only narrows what may be denied, in the direction rules 3 and 13 require, so it is not a weakening of a safety rule; the exit still reaches the market as a limit order, which is what §4.3 asks of it | Keep the denial (a session rule denying a risk exit, contradicting MI-1's own words); re-price only risk exits (a discretionary or owner market exit would still be denied by a session rule, which rule 13 forbids for every exit) |
| DEC-160 | **E7-4 takes DEC-140's slice 7 and runs in six slices; a protective leg the broker created belongs to the position's single holder, and fails closed otherwise** (the coordinator's rulings on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174), comments 5860360498 and 5860925375; amends DEC-140). (1) **Scope.** The `ProtectionChanged` fold, the confirmed cancel (`CancelAccepted` makes the order `Canceled` with `cancel_confirmed`, releasing its reservation and the unconfirmed cancel) and the agent kill switch leave DEC-140's slice 7 and join E7-4, which is one story in six slices, each about 400 non-test lines or less, tests first, with zero missed mutants: **1** protection state and the confirmed cancel; **2** brackets and the partial-fill OCO; **3** the marketable and passive exit sequences, the bounded unprotected interval, reducing sells and exits-only; **4** the exit price ladder, sessions and the triggered-stop watchdog; **5** crypto, fractions, the trading day and corporate actions; **6** the kill switch, agent and account scope. DEC-140's slice 7 is not built on its own. (2) **`BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS` stays shrink-only.** No slice adds a row (amended for slice 6 by [DEC-260](#decisions) (19): three rows, where a stub on the park's live path fails 4 live tests). A pending test that a slice lets past its old stub, and that would then fail on a later slice's missing behaviour, stops instead at a loud `Unimplemented { story: "E7-4" }` at that behaviour's entry point; slice 1's are `protection::sequenced` (an order other than a protective one in an instrument whose protection rests) and `protection::watched` (a quote in such an instrument). Each is replaced by the slice that implements what it stands for. (3) **The leg-agent rule** ([trading-domain spec](../specs/trading-domain.md) §2.3, §5.4, §9.1, v0.11; the coordinator's round-1 ruling on [#243](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/243), comment 5861072494). A protective order that `ProtectionChanged placed` names and no `OrderSubmitted` recorded (a bracket's or OCO's leg, which the broker creates) joins the order set as a live sell of the covered quantity with a zero reservation. (a) **The ID grammar.** A protective order is named `{entry}-p{protection}` for the entry it protects, its legs `…-tp` and `…-sl` (§2.3); this supersedes `ClientOrderId::for_protection`'s `md-p-<origin>`, which names no entry. Until E7-4 slice 1 aligns `ids.rs` to it and `mandate-alpaca`'s `wire.rs` keeps each leg's `client_order_id` (it reads `legs[].id` only today; backlog), the ID branch receives no input and is dead: every leg is attributed by the single holder or is unattributed. (b) **The owner** is the entry's agent when the leg's ID names the entry; otherwise the position's **single holder**, the agent whose attributed lots (§8.1) make up the whole currently open quantity, so an agent that has gone flat holds nothing. (c) **Unattributed.** With no such agent (a position no attributed order built, several agents, or quantity no order accounts for) the leg is attributed to no one: it joins the order set like any leg, with no agent (`Order.agent` is `None`), so a reconciliation finds it present rather than external and its fills, expiry and confirmed cancel fold, while it counts toward no agent's sub-ledger ([#258](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/258) round 1, comment 5862359097); and the instrument **holds** (never denies) every new opening with `protection_unattributed` until the leg is attributed or done (terminal or wholly filled); a reconciliation establishes presence, not ownership, so it does not end the hold. The zero-holder case was ruled (a) in comment 5860925375, and the kill-switch fixtures attribute their position through an `OrderSubmitted` accordingly ([#242](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/242)). (d) **Rule 13.** An unattributed leg never holds an exit: the instrument's exit sequences and its protection re-placement cancel it as step 1, and an agent-scoped kill switch in the instrument cancels it by its own `client_order_id`, never by cancel-all (§5.5). (e) **The reason code.** §9.1 places `protection_unattributed` at check 4, before `add_blocked_by_protective_order`, as the more specific cause. Registering it in the reference-case `reason_codes` registry is **Proposed (founder)** by DEC-129 item 27's path, the YAML being founder-owned (ES-22); until then the executor journals it as the partial gate's hold code, as it does `startup_reconciliation_pending`. (4) **Rule 13 and the slice order.** `sequenced` answers a stub for an exit in a protected instrument, which would be a denied exit once reachable. It is reachable only in an instrument whose protection rests, and nothing creates protection on an unprotected position until slice 2 (brackets and the partial-fill OCO). So, **binding** (the coordinator's rulings on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174), comments 5861010591 and 5862180909): slice 3 runs as **3a** (the marketable exit sequence, its re-placement and the bounded unprotected interval) and **3b** (the passive exit sequence, reducing sells and exits-only), and the slices land in the order **1, 1b, 3a, 3b, 4, 2, 5, 6**: **slice 2 lands no earlier than 3a, 3b and 4**, and no path may create protected instruments before exits through them work. Once sessions and auction windows moved to slice 5 (item (9)), **slice 2 also lands no earlier than slice 5's session and auction part** (the coordinator's ruling, comment 5864074639, option (a)), so no protected instrument exists before an exit through it outside the regular session or in an auction window works. From 3a, `sequenced` answers its stub only for an add or a passive exit where protection rests (a marketable exit runs the sequence instead), and `watched` only for the triggered-stop watchdog's input, a sane mark at or below a resting stop (slice 4); every other quote is kept as the latest observation, and a refused quote is refused alone, never blocking or delaying an exit, a cancel or a reconciliation. `protection::stub_tests::no_protection_is_created_on_an_unprotected_position` pins the order by scanning every production source in the workspace (the connectors, the journal, the shell, the runtime, the Python tools): no source builds a bracketed `SubmitOrder`, the one OCO built is a running sequence's re-placement, and only `protection.rs` writes the protection event, with the two registries that name it pinned by count. Slice 2 deletes the pin. (5) **Adds where protection rests** (ruling (b) on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174), comment 5862934909). A plain add in an instrument whose protection rests is **denied** `add_blocked_by_protective_order` (§5.4), checked just after `protection_unattributed` (§9.1 check 4). A bracketed add there is a new bracket, slice 2's, and answers its stub, `bracketed_add`, which replaces `sequenced`. `IntentReceived` journals the intent's stop and take-profit, so the fold tells a bracket from a plain add. (6) **A passive exit of part of the position** (ruling (c), same comment; rule 3). §5.4 does not say, and an OCO's legs share one quantity. So the new OCO covers the exit's own quantity: the take-profit at the exit's limit and the kept stop. The remainder gets a second OCO at the recorded prices. Nothing sells beyond the intent, and the stop covers exactly the position, before and after the take-profit fills (`a_passive_exit_keeps_the_stop_on_exactly_the_position`). (7) **Rule 5's wait** (the coordinator's ruling, comment 5863046153). Before any reducing sell, whatever its purpose (risk exits included), the executor cancels the agent's own resting opening in the instrument and waits for the confirmation (§5.3 rule 5). This is a sequencing step like §5.4's cancel and confirm, and it never denies. The wait is bounded (rule 3). §5.4 names no cancel-confirmation timeout, so the bound is `unknown_absent_window_s`, the Unknown machinery's, at its conservative Proposed 15 s. A cancel unconfirmed past the bound, or refused by the broker, is journaled overdue (`cancel_overdue`), and the opening is queried by id as an Unknown is. Once the query answers, the exit goes, whether the opening is terminal or still resting; a self-cross is left to the broker. An exit never waits a second time on the same opening. Each step is journaled, and each is tested: a cancel never confirmed, and a cancel refused because the buy filled. The case that `coverage::a_held_exit_is_released_at_the_first_tick_its_hold_has_cleared` used to hide moved in-module, and its tests correction is [#273](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/273). (8) **The gate before the sequence** (the coordinator's rulings, comments 5863814949 and 5863867565). §5.4's sequence starts only for an exit the gate allows. A held exit (rule 13's Unknown, paused or stopped, or the broker) or a denied one leaves protection resting and opens no interval. When the tick releases a held exit, its sequence starts then. The executor reads the verdict first without journaling it (`decide`) and journals `GateDecided` after the protective cancels, so the journal order stays cancel then gate, which the live hand test pins. For an exit, the shares resting protection holds count as available (§5.3's note on the first gate decision), since the sequence cancels that protection first. An exit for the whole protected quantity is therefore allowed, never `sell_exceeds_available`. Slice 5's crypto-add stub runs before the gate; an add may always be refused (rule 2). Tests: a denied exit starts no sequence; a held exit leaves protection resting until released; an exit for the whole protected quantity is allowed. (9) **Slice 4's split** (the coordinator's ruling on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174), comment 5864074639). Slice 4 runs as **4a** (the exit price ladder, its rungs and the floor, and [#264](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/264)'s follow-ups) then **4b** (the triggered-stop watchdog, replacing `watched`'s stub). Sessions and auction windows move to slice 5, beside the trading day they read; item (4)'s order is extended accordingly. **What slice 5's session part carries** (the coordinator's ruling D3 on #174, 5926142854): the calendar's sessions, extended-hours exits, the ladder outside a sequence, closed-market holds, the watchdog's session condition, §8.2's in-session trade, and held exits never abandoned for age (DEC-260 (13) to (17)). RC-24 ×2 stay pending with the trading day, slice 5's trading-day part: the reference-case harness needs `TradingDayStarted` folded, and folding it carries the other pending cases past their stubs (DEC-260 (13)). The four kill-switch session tests (`an_automated_flatten_defers_equity_sells_to_the_session`, `an_automated_flatten_sells_crypto_at_once`, `an_owner_exit_outside_the_session_prices_from_the_confirmed_bid`, `an_unconfirmed_owner_exit_waits_for_the_session`), the owner-confirmed extended-hours path, and the owner floor's wiring ([#373](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/373) round 1, minor 1) move to slice 6, which owns the kill switch; the owner exit's 154.23 tests correction landed first ([#392](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/392)). (10) **The rung ID** (same comment; [trading-domain spec](../specs/trading-domain.md) §2.3, v0.12). Each ladder rung after the first is a new order named for the exit's `client_order_id` plus `-l{n}`, n derived from the journaled rung count, so a restart derives the same ID; the first rung keeps the exit's ID. An agent decision: it neither weakens a safety rule nor touches money. (11) **The watchdog exit's identity** (same comment; §2.3, §5.5, v0.12). The watchdog's exit is the executor's own journaled intent, `md-w-<event>` from the watchdog record (like `for_replacement`), purpose `risk_exit`, its agent the position's single holder (item (3)(b)) or none (`*`). A `*` exit is cancellable by every scope covering its instrument, by its own ID, and no agent-scoped kill switch treats it as its own; each has a test. It is never refused (rule 13). (12) **An exit with nothing to price from** (same comment; §5.6 step 4, v0.12; rules 3 and 13). With no fresh sane bid, no sane bid within 5 minutes and no last trade: a risk exit, protective order, kill-switch flatten or owner exit falls back to its intent's own limit, never below an owner exit's floor, journaled with an owner alert; a discretionary exit is held and re-evaluated every tick, the hold journaled once, never dropped, with the same alert. The ladder resumes stepping at the next priceable quote or trade; a fallback price is never where it ends. `exit_limit` returns no error for any purpose. Tests: a risk exit with no bid and no trade goes at its own limit; an owner exit's fallback respects the floor; a discretionary exit is held, then goes when a quote arrives; `exit_limit` never errs, as a property over every purpose. (13) **Rule 5's wait, bounded in every state** ([#286](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/286) round 1, B1; the coordinator's ruling). The wait starts at the `GateDecided` that allows the exit, its latest one (a held exit has not started waiting, item (8)), folded as the intent's `allowed_at`. The bound covers every opening of the agent in the instrument that the exit waits on, whatever its state: `Submitting`, `Unknown`, `Accepted`, `PartiallyFilled` or `PendingCancel`. A `Submitting` opening is cancelled as soon as the broker accepts it. At the bound it becomes `Unknown`, which a submission unanswered that long is (§5.7), and is queried; an answer that the broker never had it holds the exit on that `Unknown` (rule 13's hold) until §5.7's absence rule resolves it. After every step, every tick and every order-state change, each waiting exit asks the cancel of any opening accepted since, and once nothing holds it back is re-gated and submitted, whatever ended the wait: a confirmed cancel, the broker reporting the opening canceled or filled with no confirmation, an expiry, a reject, a fill, or a query answered past the bound. A cancel the broker refuses while the opening's cancel is outstanding, in any state, is overdue at once and queried, never read as the opening's own reject; a refusal after the opening filled changes nothing. §5.7's table has no edge from `PendingCancel` to `Expired` or `Rejected`, so such a report is journaled and ignored and the bound ends that wait; the table is not changed here. Tests: the reviewer's three paths (a `Submitting` opening cancelled once accepted, or queried at the bound; an opening reported gone with no confirmation; an opening filled while its cancel is outstanding), a refusal after the opening moved on, a wait that starts at the allow ending a hold, and a property over random scripts: no exit stays neither submitted nor denied past the bound plus one tick except under rule 13's holds, and none goes while its agent's opening is live and unanswered. (14) **A passive exit over a stop-only placement** (#286 round 1, M1). Where the resting protection has no take-profit (crypto's one stop-limit, DEC-36), a passive exit answers slice 5's `Unimplemented { story: "E7-4" }` in `begin_exit` before anything is cancelled or journaled, so the stop keeps resting: never an OCO in a crypto instrument (§5.4 allows it simple orders only) and never a remainder left unprotected. `passive_exit` answers the same stub for such a sequence found on the journal. Like `replace` and `crypto_add`, it is unreachable before slice 2 (item (4)'s pin). Tests: a stop-only placement with the exit passive and marketable, and a journaled passive sequence with no take-profit. (15) **The release path's journal order** (#286 round 1, minor 1). A held exit that a tick releases journals its sequence's `ProtectionChanged` and protective cancels before its `GateDecided`, as on arrival (item (8)); `a_held_exit_leaves_protection_resting_until_released` pins the order. (16) **Modes other than `normal`** (#286 round 1, minor 4). A copied `AgentModeApplied` whose `to` is anything but `normal`, one of mandate spec §7.4's three stricter modes or a string that names no mode, cancels the agent's openings: cancelling only reduces risk (rule 3), so an unrecognised mode fails in that direction. No behaviour change; `any_mode_but_normal_is_read_as_stricter` pins it. (17) **A passive exit that ends without its OCO** (rule 3; found by #286 round 1's mutation run, outside the ruling). A passive exit denied or abandoned once its OCO's cancel is confirmed, for instance too old at a slow confirmation, ends its sequence as a marketable sequence's finished exit does: the recorded OCO is re-placed for the position held and the sequence ends, where before the position was left with no protection, no interval and a stale sequence. Test: an abandoned passive exit re-places the protection. A passive exit held, or waiting on its opening, after that confirmation opens an interval the bound ends (item (19)). (18) **Rule 5's wait ends when the opening goes overdue** (#286 round 2, B1′, the coordinator's ruling; amends (13)). An exit's wait on its agent's opening ends when the opening is marked overdue, at rule 5's bound or when the broker refuses its cancel, not when the query is answered. The query still goes. A broker that never answers the query, or 404s an opening it acknowledged earlier, therefore holds no exit by its silence. An opening the broker may still hold is `Unknown`, and the gate holds the exit on it (`unknown_order_in_flight`, rule 13's hold); one still resting is a self-cross for the broker to refuse. `OrderDetail::answered` is removed. The overdue mark applies to one submission attempt (nit 2): a resubmission after a confirmed absence is a new attempt with a new detail, and an exit waits on it afresh. The property's venue can now lose an opening it acknowledged, so every later query 404s, and drop query answers; restoring the old condition fails it (minimal script: an exit waiting on two openings, the acknowledged one lost, the exit still waiting at 15 s). Tests: the rule-5 tests updated to the new timing, and the widened property. (19) **A passive exit's wait is bounded** (#286 round 2, M2′; closes (17)'s gap). Once nothing rests and a passive exit is neither placed nor finished, because it waits on its opening or the gate holds it, `settle` journals `unprotected_start` once for its sequence; `passive_exit`'s placement or `replace` ends it. At `max_unprotected_s` the bound ends every exit in the instrument, in any sequence: a working one is cancelled, and one with no order yet is abandoned with `unprotected_interval_limit`, so `settle` re-places the recorded OCO in the same step (item (17)). It ends them all, not the sequence's exit alone, because `settle` re-places protection only once no exit is working, and one left working would keep the position unprotected past the bound. The old OCO is never re-placed while the exit waits. Tests: the bound ending two working exits; a passive exit waiting on an opening the broker never had (the query 404s and the gate holds the exit on the `Unknown`) and one held on another agent's `Unknown`, each bounded at 30 s with the OCO re-placed for the whole position, and a placement ending the interval its wait opened. An opening acknowledged and then lost releases the exit at rule 5's bound (item (18)), so the interval's bound does not fire on that path. (20) **A passive exit beside other exits** (#286 round 2, M1′). The remainder OCO covers the position less the exit and less what every other exit in the instrument may still sell: each live non-protective sell's unfilled quantity (the set `gate::available` walks, plus an order back in `Intent` for resubmission) and each exit received with no order yet. The passive exit is never refused for them (rule 13). Where anything was netted, the placement hands the sequence on: `unprotected_start` records it again, its interval is bounded like any other, and once the other exits end `replace` covers the held quantity less what protective orders still cover, so the stop never covers more than the position before or after they fill. An exit the placement was sized around goes without cancelling it (`fits`: what protection covers plus what exits may still sell is at most the long position). Any other exit cancels the whole placement, the passive exit's own OCO included, and waits for the confirmations; `replace` then re-places what is left at the smaller size through the same interval. That is the ruling's conservative interim, taken because slice 2 owns the replace and because cancelling protection before a risk-reducing sell keeps §5.3's reading of `gate::available` unchanged, so no exit is ever denied for the shares a passive OCO holds. At the bound (item (19)) the exits beside the placement are ended and the placement, being protection, keeps resting. Tests: `a_passive_exit_keeps_the_stop_on_exactly_the_position` extended to every split of ten shares between a passive and a marketable exit, in both release orders, filled or cancelled (stop ≤ position before and after the fill, and = position once the other exit ends); an exit waiting on its opening that the placement was sized around; an exit it was not sized around; and the handed-on bound's cancel and abandon. Ten seeded bugs are each caught. (21) **Rule 13's hold codes in the property** (#286 round 2, minor 1). The property exempts an exit only while the gate holds it with `unknown_order_in_flight`, `agent_paused`, `agent_stopped` or `broker`. The ruling named `account_trading_blocked`, which is the executor gate's deny code for an add in a blocked account; it holds an exit there with `broker` (`gate::account_failure`), so the set uses the code the gate emits. (22) **An ES-13 size exception for #286** (#286 round 3, minor 1). Outside `#[cfg(test)]`, excluding blank and doc lines, the slice is +528 −124, net 404 non-generated lines against the merge base, over ES-13's 400 for a safety-critical crate. Round 2's three fixes added about 146 of them, so the earlier +379 −91 figure is stale. The slice is not split, because those three fixes depend on each other. The sizing fit (M1) feeds the protection check that both the settle step and the cancel wait read. The unprotected interval (M2) is what bounds a handed-on passive exit. The overdue release (B1) is what lets that bound be reached when the broker reports a lost opening. Landing any subset would ship an intermediate state a review has already found unsound. (23) **How slice 4a carries out items (10) and (12)** (the coordinator's rulings on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174), comments 5864074639 and 5864659765; trading-domain spec §2.3 and §5.6, v0.12). (a) **Rungs.** A tick at which the current rung has been working `exit_step_s` cancels it to step, journaled `ladder_step` on its `OrderStateChanged`; only that cancel's confirmation submits the next rung, `-l{n}`, for what the last rung left unsold, one offset step lower and repriced from the current reference. Each rung's `OrderSubmitted` journals `rung` and `at_floor`, so a restart resumes the ladder. At `max_exit_offset` the rung rests, journaled `ProtectionChanged ladder_floor` with one `exit_ladder_floor` alert, and never steps again. The interval's bound (item (19)) overtakes a step: a step's cancel confirmed after the bound re-protects instead of stepping. (b) **Nothing to price from.** The fallback is journaled `ProtectionChanged exit_unpriced` with an `exit_unpriced` alert. The discretionary hold is the gate's, `hold exit_unpriced` at check `exit_priceable`, taken before §5.4's sequence, so protection stays resting, and `release_held` re-evaluates it at every tick without journaling again. A discretionary exit whose price vanishes after its sequence cancelled the protection never waits unprotected for the bound: the sequence ends, protection is re-placed at once for the held quantity, and the exit stays held until a priceable quote starts a fresh sequence. (c) **The later-rung reading** (comment 5864659765, item 2): once an exit is working, a later rung with nothing to price from goes at the intent's own limit for every purpose, a discretionary exit's included, journaled `exit_unpriced` with an owner alert. The hold in (b) applies before submission only, because a working order resting at its own limit adds no risk and prices no worse than the intent asked. `properties::the_only_holds_on_an_exit_are_the_four_the_rule_names` judges risk and owner exits only, so the discretionary hold is outside it. Tests: `a_later_rung_with_nothing_to_price_from_goes_at_its_own_limit` (discretionary, risk and owner exits), `the_bound_overtakes_a_step_in_flight`, `a_step_on_a_rung_a_late_fill_completed_ends_the_sequence`, `an_unpriced_exit_beside_another_sequence_re_places_nothing`, the rung, floor, bound and restart tests and item (12)'s four in `protection::sequence_tests`, and the nine hand and fault tests the slice makes live. Slice 4a's own readings where the rulings are silent are [DEC-260](#decisions). (24) **How slice 4b carries out item (11), the triggered-stop watchdog** (trading-domain spec §2.3, §5.4, v0.12; rules 3 and 13). A sane mark at or below the instrument's resting stop starts a breach, kept process-local beside the quotes (`breaches`); a sane mark above the stop ends it, and an insane quote or one with no mark changes nothing. On a quote or a tick, where a breach has lasted `stop_watchdog_s`, every resting protective order there is unfilled ("with no fill"), no sequence runs there and no watchdog intent there still waits, the executor journals the watchdog record (`ProtectionChanged watchdog`, with its agent and quantity), alerts the owner (`stop_watchdog`), and hands itself the intent `w-<record>`: a `risk_exit` of what the resting protection covers, never more than the long position, its agent the position's single holder (item (3)(b), now `fold::single_holder`, shared with the leg-agent rule) or none (`*`). The intent runs like any exit: the gate, then the marketable sequence (cancel, confirm, re-gate) and the ladder, under `md-w-<record>`, so a restart derives the same id. A watchdog exit the gate holds (its agent paused, an `Unknown` in the instrument) keeps the stop resting and is journaled once; the tick that releases it starts its sequence. A `*` exit is no agent's own: it is ended by the instrument-wide bound by its own id like any exit working there, and the kill switches' side of it is slice 6's (backlog). `watched`'s stub is gone. Tests: hand `a_stop_at_its_trigger_price_without_a_fill_is_watchdogged` and `the_watchdog_exit_is_a_risk_exit_through_the_ladder` (live, the latter after its tests correction to the cent), and in `protection::sequence_tests` `a_breached_stop_is_watchdogged_into_the_holders_risk_exit` (with a restart), `a_breach_must_last_stop_watchdog_s`, `a_stop_that_has_filled_is_not_watchdogged`, `a_held_watchdog_exit_fires_once_and_goes_when_released`, `a_watchdog_exit_with_no_single_holder_is_nobodys_and_the_bound_ends_it`, and the random-script oracle, whose marks now reach the stop. Slice 4b's own readings are DEC-260 (9) to (12). | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Reversible engineering and process: it moves work between slices of one crate and names the conservative owner of a leg. It weakens no safety rule: an unattributed leg holds openings and never an exit, it is cancelled by its own id by every exit and kill switch in its instrument, and the stubs are unreachable while the pin holds | Land slice 7 on its own with about 24 temporary `BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS` rows (reverses the shrink-only list); keep the fold stubbed and land only the confirmed cancel (no live test reaches it but the kill switch); give an unattributed position to the mandate's only agent (guesses an owner, which rule 3 forbids); have `ProtectionChanged` carry `agent` (a journal-spec change for a fact the entry already records) |
| DEC-161 | **How E10-1's semantic slice reads §4.1 and §4.2 where the spec, the reference, and the merged types leave a choice** (E10-1 implementation, slice V; [task brief](tasks/M5-F-mandate-spec.md), claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). (1) **Two slices, for ES-13.** Family V is about 555 non-blank, non-doc src lines in `mandate-spec`. This slice carries every V-rule except E17-1's field split, V-038 included, the four worst-case figures, W-001 to W-005, `Mandate::at`, and the harness's `semantic` arm (about 485 lines in `mandate-spec`, about 160 in `mandate-refcases`, a tool crate); the next carries V-003, V-034 to V-037, V-039, W-006, and `worst_case_stop_distance` (about 70). V-038 cannot wait: its test in `tests/validate.rs` is `pending E17-1`, and once `validate` is not a stub that test would fail on an assertion rather than at a stub, which the pending gate refuses (DEC-137). Both `pending E17-1` markers in the file therefore go here; `the_platform_proposes_five_instruments_and_three_revisions` passes on this slice's V-020. **The coordinator ruled on #238 in round 1 to accept this slice at about 508 src lines as a one-off, setting no precedent for other slices:** it reviews in one sitting, it is tabular rule-per-row, and splitting it further would separate the rules from their shared figures; part 2 stays a separate PR. (2) **A diverged mandate is refused.** V-009 (the order the owner wrote), V-015 (a date with no calendar position), and V-020 (a listed default's value) read the document the mandate was parsed from, so `validate` first calls `canonical` and returns `diverged` if the public fields no longer match it: a report describes only the mandate that is hashed and enforced. (3) **The figures are exact.** Each is `Usd × Fraction` with no rounding; a fraction past `Fraction`'s nine places, or a product `Usd` cannot hold, makes the document unevaluable, `out_of_range` naming the field (DEC-128 item 4), never a V-code and never a rounded figure. V-002 and V-032 compare exactly. Display rounding stays the open founder item. (4) **Provenance entries cover their subtree.** An entry at a path speaks for everything under it. V-020 judges each entry: a `platform_default` is valid only where a §7 path covers it and, where §7 fixes a value, the document holds that value at the entry's own path, so an entry under a fixed-value field (`/autonomy/default/x`) names nothing and is refused; `approvers` only with one workspace user; system fields are skipped. V-022 reads every entry on, under, or **above** an `auto`'s path (`/autonomy/default`, `/autonomy/admission`, `/autonomy/rules/<i>/then`), so `/autonomy` stated by the compiler cannot carry an `auto` past it; `ref.py` reads the exact path only, and no case tells the two apart. (5) **V-023 reads §6.3's table:** `thesis_confidence` is "decimal in [0, 1]" there, so it is bounded like `combined_score` and `drawdown`, although `ConditionField::is_unit_bounded` omits it; and a condition decimal wider than the `Ratio` the order path compares it as is V-023 at load, which is stream F's half of #216's backlog row. (6) **V-006 claims are on a group or on an ungrouped instrument,** two variants rather than one string, so a group id that spells an asset id never claims that instrument (the `_group_claimed` reading #216's review found in `ref.py`). (7) **`ConnectionId::parse`,** the schema's `$defs/id` and nothing wider, joins `ModelId::parse` under DEC-128 item 22: V-031's `PreviousVersion` cannot be built without it. (8) **`ref.py` accepts any `platform_default` on `leveraged_etp_disclosure_version`;** §7 and the merged `platform_defaultable` table say `null` only, and the crate follows them. A 60,000-document differential against `ref.py`'s `semantic` (three seeds) found this and DEC-151 item 1's `too_deep` as its only disagreements. (9) **An end date `Date` cannot hold is V-015.** `mandate_time::Date` spans 1970 to 9999, so a pattern-valid `end_date` before 1970 is read as no date (DEC-151 item 3) and reported as V-015 rather than V-030. The document is refused either way, and any such date is before every validation date. (10) **Part 2's readings** (E17-1's field split). W-006's "a research agent configured" is a signal model with `admits_instruments`, as `ref.py` reads it; a research envelope with no admitting model is V-036 either way. V-003 compares the pinned list to exactly the goal's one instrument, in order and count. V-035 counts the pinned instruments exactly, and a count `u32` cannot hold is V-035 rather than a wrapped number. `worst_case_stop_distance` is the stop plus the offset when the universe admits crypto, summed exactly as a `Ratio` and returned in the `positive_decimal` grammar (the sum of two open fractions can reach past one), while without the offset it is the document's own stop in its `open_fraction` grammar; `None` when there is no stop, and a distance past a `Ratio`'s 24 places is `out_of_range` naming its field. | DEC-163 | **How E6-8's implementation reads trading-domain §8.2 and §9.6: check 5, check 6, the pacing of an allowed exit, the cancel rule and the surveillance report** ([task brief](tasks/E6-3-risk-gate.md), DEC-129, DEC-77 stage 3; stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123)). Every control compares against `GateInput::now` and an instant or count the executor folded; `mandate-risk` reads no clock. Only an opening or an increase is ever denied by checks 5 and 6 (`AGENTS.md` rule 13): a risk exit, a protective order and a kill switch's order pass them untouched, an owner exit is paced by the participation caps only, and a discretionary exit is paced by all of them and never denied. (1) **Freshness.** A quote's age is judged upstream: §4.2 leaves the staleness threshold to the data profile's configuration and DEC-168 item 5 makes the bound the reader's, so a quote past it reaches the gate as `MarketSnapshot::quote: None`. No quote, a quote stamped after `now`, or a `stale_mark` instrument restriction (DEC-129 item 23) is `stale_mark` for an opening at check 5. A crossed quote (`bid > ask`) refuses to decide an opening (`GateError::QuoteUnsane`); a locked one (`bid = ask`) is sane. A reducing purpose never reaches check 5 (§8.2: "any source"), a crossed or missing quote prices no exit, and no usable quote bars market orders as the stale-quote arm of a presumed halt (DEC-129 item 28), so a market exit is sent as a marketable limit. (2) **The collar is a closed interval.** For a buy, `[ask × (1 − band), ask × (1 + x)]`; for a sell, `[bid × (1 − x), bid × (1 + band)]`. The passive band is measured from the same side of the quote as the aggressive bound, the stricter of the two readings of "a wider passive band". Both ends round inward (`Price::collar_bound`), both ends pass, and a price past either is `price_outside_collar`. `x` is `collar_other_x` when the 20-day median dollar volume is known and below the threshold, and `collar_liquid_x` when it is at least the threshold or unknown: an unknown median takes the narrower collar, so the ambiguous case admits fewer prices (round-1 review m5). An opening with an unknown median is denied by the eligibility floor before check 5, so this tier binds the pricing of a discretionary exit. (3) **Check 6's order** is §9.6's table order after the collar: order size against trailing 5-minute volume, daily participation, order-to-fill, the opposite-fill interval, the close window, self-trade prevention, then the mandate's `max_orders_per_day`. Both participation caps and order-to-fill report `conduct_limit_breached`, as does self-trade (the registry has no self-trade code). The opposite-fill interval includes its last instant: §9.6's "within 60 seconds after" an opposite-side fill still covers an opening exactly 60 s after it, which is denied, and one a nanosecond later is allowed; only a fill on the other side counts. This is the reading that never allows an opening the text could deny, and it differs on purpose from the re-entry cooldown's "until `reentry_cooldown_s` after" (mandate §5.3), which lifts at the boundary. It amends this item's first draft (strict), after CI's mutation gate on #311 found nothing pinning the boundary; the tests PR's `properties::only_an_opposite_side_fill_starts_the_interval` oracle (`elapsed < 60`) is corrected to `elapsed <= 60` in its own tests-correction PR. Order-to-fill is compared without dividing (`orders > max × max(fills, 1)`), counted from `order_to_fill_min_orders` orders, the proposal not among them. (4) **A missing volume.** An opening whose `trailing_5m_volume` or `adv_20d` the executor could not supply is `conduct_limit_breached`, failing closed as the eligibility floor does for an unknown median. An exit with a missing volume is not sliced by that cap, since a cap with no volume cannot say how much to hold back and rule 13 forbids refusing the exit. A cap that works out to zero (an order cap of zero or truncated to zero, or a daily cap used up or overdrawn) is treated exactly like a missing volume: it slices nothing and leaves `applied` unset. A cap above zero but below `min_order_size` slices at `min_order_size`, or sends the whole exit if that is smaller, never below it. No new defer or verdict code. This is the round-1 review's ruling on its blocker B2: before it, a zero cap allowed an owner or discretionary exit with `qty` 0, an order the broker refuses, which would deny the exit rule 13 protects; risk exits and protective orders were never sliced and are untouched. (5) **The collar prices a discretionary exit.** A limit past the aggressive end is raised (a sell) or lowered (a buy) to it; a limit past the passive end is brought to it; a market order becomes a marketable limit at the aggressive end, since "the collar prices them" leaves no exit unpriced. An equity price then goes on the Reg NMS grid inward (`Price::on_tick`); a crypto price stays at the bound, because the snapshot carries no venue increment and the executor's rounding against the order (§2.1) moves it inward. `Pacing::applied` names `Collar`. With no usable quote there is nothing to price by and the exit goes as proposed. In the close window a discretionary exit is sent as a marketable limit (`CloseWindow`); an owner exit is not, because §9.6 paces owner exits "by the participation caps only". (6) **Slices.** A discretionary or owner exit is sliced to the smaller of `order_size_participation × trailing_5m_volume` and what is left of `daily_participation × adv_20d` today, each truncated to the instrument's increment (whole shares unless fractionable). Each cap that binds is named in `applied`, where a cap binds when its slice (item 4) is below the proposed quantity; a cap equal to it slices nothing. Item 4 leaves no zero slice, so no defer code for one is needed: the PR's question whether to add one is answered no, and its backlog row is withdrawn. (7) **Cancels.** `evaluate_cancel` denies `min_resting_time` for a non-marketable opening order canceled strictly inside `min_resting_time_s` of `resting_since`, unless the cancel precedes a risk-reducing order. That flag is how every exit sequence and kill switch cancels are exempt: `CancelInput` has no cause field and the executor sets it. The two booleans stay in #311; a required `CancelCause { RiskReducing, Replace { marketable }, Discretionary }` is a follow-up story with its own tests correction (backlog, round-1 review n2). The decision's purpose is the canceled order's (`Open`, `Protective`, else `DiscretionaryExit`, since a working order records no exit origin), and its only check is check 6. (8) **Surveillance** states figures and flags thresholds, and judges nothing (§9.6: "the platform does not supervise users' trading"). Orders are `submitted − cancels_excluded` (an error if the exclusion is larger), the ratio is `orders ÷ max(fills, 1)` truncated to an integer (round-1 review m4), and the breach uses item 3's comparison. Self-trade flags each instrument with any agent in its set. Close-window activity flags any count above zero, since §4.3 and §9.6 allow no opening there. Concentration is each end-of-day market value as a share of the agent's equity, rounded up, and stated as `1` above equity or when equity is not positive. It is a figure only and never flagged; `SurveillanceBreach::Concentration` is not raised in v1. §3.3 supplies no number, and a threshold the platform chose would be compliance-visible, so a threshold is the founder's (backlog). This answers the PR's second question: no concentration flag. The `Ratio` to `Fraction` step goes through the exact decimal text, because `mandate-num` has no conversion (backlog). (9) **No new `mandate-num` arithmetic.** The brief's `Qty::times_fraction` is the existing `Qty::portion(Fraction, ShareIncrement)`, truncation to the increment, so the PR touches `mandate-risk` only, plus `xtask`'s `BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS`, whose two `mandate-risk` rows the pacing retires. (10) **What still fails closed.** After E6-8 no §9.1 check is owed for a US equity. An ordinary whole-share limit opening is decided; a crypto opening is refused `Unimplemented("evaluate", "E6-10")` for check 2's USD-pairs rule, and an equity opening breaking §5.3 rule 2 or 7 in a way DEC-129 item 27 leaves unregistered (below `min_order_size`, fractional in a whole-share instrument, fractional GTC or fractional bracket) is refused `Unimplemented(.., "DEC-129 item 27")`. E6-6's second slice is an input gap, not a refusal: the gate reads `AgentSnapshot::day_trades` as supplied. (11) **The folded state's contract** (round-1 review M3). "Today" is the risk day, 00:00 to 00:00 America/New_York (mandate §5.3). `ConductState::filled_today` and `orders_today_per_instrument` count only the deciding agent's orders in the account it trades (§9.6: "per agent per instrument per day") and reset daily; a fill counts its order once however many fills it took, so partial fills cannot dilute the ratio, and orders count every purpose, each `client_order_id` once, rejected ones included, less exit-sequence and kill-switch cancels. `last_opposite_fill_at` is the latest fill per instrument and side by any agent in any account of the related-accounts group and is never reset; an older fill stops blocking when the interval passes. `participation_today` is the day's filled quantity on both sides by every agent across the related-accounts group plus their working orders' open quantity, reset daily. `resting_since` is each working order's broker acceptance, which `evaluate` does not read; the executor passes it to `evaluate_cancel`. `AccountSnapshot::related_account_resting` lists every side on which an order rests, per instrument, in any account of the related-accounts group, this account and the deciding agent's own orders included, protective orders too: the gate filters nothing. In this account a non-protective order already denies an opening at check 4, so the set adds resting protective orders there; a bracket opening beside a resting protective sell is therefore denied, the conservative reading, and whether a stop counts is a backlog item. `CancelInput::marketable` is whether the order's limit trades against the current quote, `false` with no usable quote. Each reading counts more or blocks sooner where the spec is silent. (12) **Size exception.** #311 adds 518 non-test source lines to `mandate-risk` at review, 537 after the round-1 fixes, against ES-13's 400. It stays one PR, as DEC-167 item 5 kept a slice over 400 whole: checks 5 and 6, the pacing of an allowed exit, the cancel rule and the surveillance report land together so that no check is half-wired, since the gate refuses to decide an opening while a check is owed and `-D warnings` refuses code only a test reaches. Not split (round-1 review M1). | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79); the round-1 review's rulings on #311 are recorded in items 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 11 and 12. A concentration threshold stays with the founder, and until then the report flags none; item 4's clause that a positive cap truncated to zero slices nothing is superseded by [DEC-445](decisions/DEC-445.md) | Each reading refuses an opening where the spec is silent and never refuses an exit; every boundary has an in-module test and a planted bug it catches | The passive band measured from the opposite side of the quote (wider); a missing volume allowing an opening; denying a zero slice; allowing a zero slice with `qty` 0 (the first draft, overruled as B2); a defer code for a zero slice; an unknown median taking the wider collar; a platform-chosen concentration threshold; a crate-level quote-age constant (rejected by DEC-168 item 5); a new `Qty::times_fraction` |
| DEC-164 | **The pending gate gives one verdict per tree: a property is judged by the minimal failure proptest reports, and its run is isolated** (the coordinator's ruling on [#230](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/230) comment 5860360338; #227's round-2 delta review). Amends [DEC-137](#decisions) item (1) for properties only. (1) `cargo xtask ci pending` reads a failing property by proptest's own report of it, `Test failed: <why>.` over the minimal input or `Test aborted: <why>`, and nothing else in its output: not the panics of the cases shrinking moved past, and not the minimal input's `Debug`. The change only removes evidence the gate accepted: every property that fails away from its stub at its minimal case is now reported, and the three new rows are named one by one and reported for deletion once their tests stop at a stub. It is process tooling, so an agent decision | Pin a case count by environment (overrides only properties that take proptest's defaults, and changes what a property means in `ci test`); run each pending property under several seeds (multiplies the run's time and still reads incidental text); classify by panic source location (a stub's `Err` is unwrapped in the test file, so the location names the test, not the stub); leave the three properties without rows (the gate would fail on main) |

(2) **The shell never invents an input.** A stage stays refusing while its upstream is a stub, or while any of its inputs has no production source. A flatten probe over a synthetic empty input would pass while `plan` could not plan, so it is not written.
