(4) **Stored data is trusted only when `inspect` says so.** It must be the pinned symbol's daily bars, with no partition problem, no duplicate start, no trading day listed without a bar, no true or unclassified gap, and no split inside the span (or corporate actions recorded for only part of it). The closes are returned exactly as stored: the shell adjusts, filters and repairs nothing. `Admitted` gains the pinned `symbol`, and `ModelRef` gains the envelope's `params`. (5) **A one-close outlier is not the shell's to judge.** Doing so is price arithmetic, which DEC-138 item 3 keeps out of the shell. `tests/tracer.rs::outlier_close` stays pending until a market-data trust rule or E6-8's mark-and-collar exists, and this is reported to their owners. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Each keeps DEC-138's rule that the shell holds no trading logic, and each keeps every unfinished stage refusing; none touches live money, spending, legal text or a safety invariant | Write the adapters against invented configuration (a shell decision the brief's stop conditions forbid); probe the flatten path on an empty synthetic input (a probe that passes while `plan` cannot plan); wire the binary to `AlpacaPaperHttp` now (it would read credentials for a run that always refuses before a request) |
| DEC-167 | **How E6-4's `mandate-spec` part is delivered and read** (stream H, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124); [task brief](tasks/M5-F-mandate-spec.md), families R, T, and L). (1) **Slices, one PR at a time, under ES-13.** Slice 1 is T (`risk::risk_day`) plus the three §5 primitives that read no mandate: `Confirmation::update`, `size_factor`, and `reset_lift_order`. It takes the breach-time property live now. Then comes L (`goal::status`). Then the §5 fold in two parts, since together it is about 1,100 lines: R2 covers marks, fills, risk days, clock ticks, universe changes, and the profit stop; R3 covers acknowledgments, allocation changes, floor loosening, goal completion, and retirement. L, R2, and R3 wait for E10-1 slice P, because every one of their tests builds a `ValidatedMandate`, and `ValidatedMandate::new` is slice P's. The harness assertion that a stub is never a pass moved ahead of T in a tests-correction PR ([#239](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/239)). (2) **The risk day** is the America/New_York calendar date of the instant. Its bounds are that date's midnight and the next date's midnight, and `length_s` is the distance between them. New York changes clocks at 02:00, so both midnights always exist, and the boundary instant belongs to the day it opens (§5.4, MC-T01 to MC-T05). (3) **V-040: the scale factors' fractional digits sum to at most 12.** This follows the coordinator's rulings on [#245](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/245) (major 1) and [#251](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/251) (round 1). `drawdown_ladder` allows 5 rungs and `factor` allows 28 places (`^0\.[0-9]{0,27}[1-9]$`), so a schema-valid ladder's size factor could need 140. The size factor has two consumers, and neither may round it (DEC-128 item 4; §8.3 names its only three roundings). The risk state reports it as a `Ratio` of 24 places (§5.5). The order builder multiplies its targets by it as a `SizeFraction` of 12 places (§8.3 step 2), whose bound is load-bearing for the builder's 256-bit budget, so it cannot widen. The tighter of the two binds. Without the rule, one such mandate would make every risk step an error, or every builder proposal empty, against rules 3 and 13. The rule bounds the sum of the digits rather than the digits of the whole product, because a subset can need more places than the whole ladder: 2¹³ × 10⁻¹³ needs 13, while 2¹³ × 10⁻¹³ × 5¹³ × 10⁻¹⁰ = 10⁻¹⁰ needs 10. The sum bounds every subset. It is a V-rule rather than a narrower `factor` pattern, because a pattern would take away precision the schema grants each factor on its own; five 2-place rungs still fit. A `Violation` carries a code and no pointer, and a sum has no single offending rung, so the report points at `/risk/drawdown_ladder`. It is agent-accepted under DEC-79 like the rest of this row: a stricter V-rule only refuses more mandates and weakens no safety rule. The fold's size factor stays out of R2 until V-040 is implemented.  (4) **A goal's reasons, as `reference/mandate/ref.py`'s `goal_status` orders them** (the coordinator's ruling on [#279](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/279), round 1, blocker 1). `end_date` is judged first, then an accumulate goal's target, then its spend (DEC-128 item 26 orders those two). A goal past its date is done for its date, whatever else holds. §3.1 lists the three conditions without ranking them, and `ref.py` is the oracle the cases come from. Only the reason label moves: `then` and `stop_reason` are `ref.py`'s for each goal type. "Below the minimum order" for the quantity is the remainder's notional **at the ask**, as `ref.py` reads it. That ask must come from a quote that passed §5.6's sane-and-fresh filter; the order path's caller in `mandate-risk` (stream G) guarantees it. A remainder is measured exactly: `target_qty` as a `Qty`, or `out_of_range` naming `/goal/target_qty`; `max_spend_usd` as a `Usd`, or `out_of_range` naming `/goal/max_spend_usd`. A position past the target leaves nothing. A zero `qty_increment` and a negative `goal_spent_usd` are `invalid_input` for an accumulate goal, the only type that reads them. An end date whose closing midnight the calendar cannot state (9999-12-30 and -31) is an error, unless an accumulate goal is done for its target or its spend anyway. Both bad inputs are refused before the end date is read, so past its date they are refused alike (#279 review round 2). (5) **The §5 fold ships in four slices, not item 1's R2 and R3.** Written whole, it is about 1,050 non-blank non-test lines, so item 1's two halves would each be well over ES-13's 400. Each slice takes live the tests that pass on it, and everything it does not fold yet is `Unimplemented`, never a silent answer. R1 is the limit conditions of §5.2 and §5.6, which the public `risk::conditions` reads, with #261's minors 1 to 3. R2 is the fold's spine over marks, fills, and clock ticks: the agent sub-ledger, the ladder, breach confirmation with the hard trigger, the daily-loss trigger, the floor, and the effective mode. At about 560 lines it is the one slice over 400. It cannot split further, because `-D warnings` refuses code that only a test reaches, and every one of those pieces runs at every step. R3 adds staleness, universe changes, the risk day (rollover, renewal, and lift), and the profit stop. R4 adds acknowledgments, allocation changes, floor loosening, goal completion, and retirement. **`RiskState` borrows the `SessionClock` it opened with** (`RiskState<'c>`). `step` takes no clock, and every later step counts an equity's staleness and lift delays on it. No caller names the type, so every caller compiles unchanged. The clock is not state, so two states that folded the same inputs are equal whichever clock each holds. **`mandate-num` gains `UsdExact::quotient`**, one rounding of one exact quotient, because §5.1's `X × (E + Δ) ÷ E` and trading spec §8.1's `B × q ÷ Q` both need more places than a `Usd` holds before the division. (6) **R2's readings of the fold.** The dispatch for R2 used item 1's split, which puts the risk day, universe changes, and the profit stop in R2. R2 follows item 5, which is later and binding. (a) **A fill is not a sane quote.** §5.6 arms, latches, and clears a hard breach on a sane quote. A fill quotes nothing: E moves by the fill's price against the last mark, and the mark stays where it was. `ref.py` counts fills, which lets one flash print latch a limit on the next fill, against DEC-63. The crate follows the spec, no reference case changes (checked by regenerating with the fix), and the `ref.py` fix is backlogged. (b) **The risk clock is whole seconds and monotone** (§5.2). An instant between two seconds is `invalid_input`, at opening and at every step, because rounding it either way could end a wait up to a second before §5.6 lets it. A step at the previous instant folds with no time passing: the stub's "at or before" wording would refuse the first step of MC-R05 to MC-R08, MC-R14, MC-R15, and MC-R20. A step before it is `clock_went_backwards`. A refused step leaves the state exactly as it was, because the step runs on a copy. (c) **Staleness is guarded until R3.** A held instrument's mark reaching `mark_max_age_s` with no counted sane mark, or a counted mark failing its checks while held, is `unimplemented`, never a step without `stale_mark`. An expiry is judged by what was held over the interval. A book flat for the whole interval never goes stale. Item 7(e) supersedes the flat-sale reading: a step that leaves the book flat clears `stale_mark`, as `ref.py` and the fold both do. (d) **E = A + cash + qty × mark**, where cash is sale proceeds less purchases, the opening cost included. That is §5.1's A + realized − basis + market value with the basis split cancelled out. `ref.py`'s post-loop lift reset is `lift_s = 0` whenever the rung is not lifting (the #124 handover's items 4 and 5). (e) **Only a `scale_sizes` rung lifts on its lift level** (§5.5). A latched `exits_only` or `flatten_and_pause` rung lifts only on acknowledgment (§5.8), whatever the drawdown does. (f) **A `daily_loss` restriction's mode is the mandate's `daily_loss_action`.** `flatten_and_pause` pauses (§5.4) and fires the agent's kill switch; `Restriction::mode` alone says `exits_only`. (g) **Crypto counts wall seconds** for its lift delay, active time, and staleness, whatever the caller's clock says. Confirmation and the hard wait count wall seconds for both classes (§5.2, §5.6). (h) **Refused at opening:** a negative inherited loss, which would lower the floor (`invalid_input`). H opens at the allocation, which the schema makes positive, and in R2 only rises. **Refused at a step:** a sale of more than the agent holds, which would be a short sale (rule 12). (i) **No MC-R case passes on R2 alone.** Every equity case's base has a `profit_stop` goal, and every crypto case opens with `risk_day_started`, both R3's. With the profit stop stubbed out locally, MC-R01 to MC-R04, MC-R18, and MC-R19 match every expectation. (7) **R3's readings of the fold.** (a) **A daily hard wait carries over the rollover.** `ref.py` pops it into the rollover record and never reads it again, so `hard_breach` could stay applied with nothing to clear it (the #124 handover's first `ref.py` reading). The wait stays under the new day: the next sane quote clears it below the new day's 1.25x level, or latches the daily loss (`hard_trigger`) at that level once the wait has passed. A wait armed before midnight and satisfied after it compares the arming quote against the old day's 1.25x level and the latching quote against the new day's. It still takes two sane quotes at least the hard wait apart, so DEC-63 holds; only the latch can come earlier than a wait restarted at the rollover would let it. A carried breach that latches, or the day's own latch, ends the wait quietly, as confirmation does (§5.6). No reference case reaches it: with `ref.py`'s reading put back, the same 15 MC-R cases pass, and only the fold's own test fails; the `ref.py` fix is backlogged. (b) **A carried breach is not `pending`** (MC-R06 step 3). The new day starts with no confirmation of its own, and the carried breach confirms on its soft level only. The day's own latch drops it. One still undecided at the next `RiskDayStarted` is `invalid_input`: a carried breach that latches does so within `breach_confirm_s` on a tick the executor journals, because the tick emits an event (§5.2), so an undecided one means the stream lost that tick. (c) **A renewal is a new-day breach confirmed by breach time, or at the 1.25x level on one sane quote**, as `ref.py` reads "confirmed" there. It only extends a latch that already holds: it adds no restriction, fires no kill switch, and writes the latch afresh, so the latch is unacknowledged (the handover's second reading). One print can therefore lengthen a latched limit's wait for its lift, which only tightens; it never latches or flattens anything new (DEC-63). (d) **The daily lift** comes at the first input with a new risk day started and at least `daily_breach_min_s` since the breach or the last renewal. A `flatten_and_pause` daily loss also waits for the owner's acknowledgment, which is R4's, so on R3 it never lifts. (e) **Staleness follows `ref.py`.** The mark ages by session seconds while the instrument is held, before the input applies. `stale_mark` is set at exactly `mark_max_age_s`, or by a counted mark that fails its checks while held, and a counted sane mark clears it. A step that leaves the book flat clears it with reason `sane_mark`, the only clearing reason §5.10 names; the gate still wants a fresh quote before any opening (DEC-163 item 1). (f) **Instrument events** come after the profit stop and before the mode (§5.2 names neither), one per restriction that changed, `removed_instrument` before `stale_mark` as `ref.py` sorts them. A removal or re-admission carries its `UniverseChanged` reason. (g) **The state holds one instrument's position**, as `Opening` does, so a `UniverseChanged` applies to that position whatever id it carries. (h) **The profit stop** is read as E − C ≥ `profit_level` × C exactly, confirmed by breach time on wall seconds with no hard level, whatever other restriction holds, and it stops once `goal_complete` is applied. (i) **The scale lift delay credits min(session, wall) seconds** (#324's review, minor 2): a calendar that over-reports its session can never lift a rung early. Active time and staleness take the calendar's count, since an over-report only brings a trim or a stale mark sooner. (j) **`Fold::step` takes `&self` and returns the next fold** (minor 4), so a refused step cannot leave a half-applied fold behind, and `RiskState::step`'s errors tell the caller to fail closed as the gate's caller does (minor 1). (k) **ES-13.** R3 adds 441 non-blank non-test lines (340 of code, 101 of doc comments) and removes 94, so it is 41 added lines over 400. Item 5 fixes R3's contents, and the daily loss over risk days sits in its own module (`risk/fold/daily.rs`, 180 lines), so it can be reviewed on its own. (l) **Fifteen MC-R cases pass on R3:** MC-R01 to MC-R08, MC-R13, MC-R15, MC-R18 to MC-R20, MC-R22, and MC-R24. The other nine stop at an R4 input as `unimplemented`. (8) **R4's readings of the fold.** (a) **Acknowledging the ladder** (§5.8) is `nothing_to_acknowledge` with no rung latched, and `flatten_in_progress` while `drawdown_flatten` holds and the agent is not flat; a latched `exits_only` rung alone needs no flat book, as in `ref.py`. It resets H := E, lifts each latched rung (`owner_acknowledged`), drops the rungs' confirmations, ends a rung's hard wait with `hard_breach_cleared`, and sets every inactive scale rung active (`after_reset`). A refused acknowledgment journals nothing. (b) **Sizes step back from the highest active scale rung.** From the reset until no scale rung is active, only the highest active one counts towards its lift, and the next one's delay starts at the previous one's lift. A rung that triggers again meanwhile is the highest active again and lifts first, which is §5.8's "highest `at` first" and keeps the active rungs the shallowest ones (the ladder-monotone property). `ref.py` holds a re-triggered rung back until the others have lifted, which leaves a deeper rung active with a shallower one lifted. No reference case reaches the difference (all 24 MC-R cases pass either way); the `ref.py` fix is backlogged. (c) **A daily acknowledgment** applies only to a latched `flatten_and_pause` daily loss, once, with the agent flat; it moves that restriction's mode to `exits_only` and journals nothing of its own, since the mode change says it. The lift comes at the first input with a new risk day, `daily_breach_min_s`, and the acknowledgment, so the acknowledgment itself when the other two already hold. A renewal writes the latch afresh (item 7 (c)), so a renewed `flatten_and_pause` daily loss pauses the agent again and waits for a new acknowledgment; `ref.py` also wants the new acknowledgment but leaves the mode at `exits_only`. Pausing only tightens, and reaching it needs a buy while `exits_only`. (d) **An allocation change** is settled by a clock tick at its instant first, always (the #124 handover's reading), and that tick's events come first. It is refused, in this order, when Δ > 0 while any rung, the daily loss, or the floor is latched; when E + Δ is not positive or is below the agent's exposure, qty × mark (the state holds no working orders), both `equity_below_exposure`; or when any rung's, the daily loss's, or the floor's condition, soft or at its 1.25x level, latched or not, would be newly true after scaling. The profit stop is not read: scaling can only make it false. H, E₀, C, and L scale through `UsdExact::quotient`, rounded up at 12 places. A refused change journals its refusal and changes nothing. At an equity that is not positive §5.1's k cannot be formed, so the step is `invalid_input`, refused to decide. (e) **Floor loosening** is `not_loosening` unless f′ > f, latched or not. While the floor is latched, a version without independent approval is `waiting_period` before the end of the first full risk day after the confirmation's (at that instant it applies), and any version while latched is `still_below_new_floor` unless E > C × (1 − f′) + L strictly, compared by the fold's own conditions at f′. Applied, f′ is the floor for every later step, `MandateVersionApplied` carries it, and a latched floor lifts (`version_loosened`) with its confirmation restarted; an unlatched floor is loosened without a lift. (f) **Goal completion** journals the mandate's `on_complete`. `hold_protected` applies `goal_complete`. `disarm_ladder` applies it too and stops evaluating the rungs and the daily loss: latches already held stay, active scale rungs stay active, and the dropped confirmations (all but the floor's, and a breach carried over the rollover) leave nothing pending and nothing for a later rollover to refuse. `release` journals `PositionReleased` with the sub-ledger's quantity and applies `retired`. A `profit_stop` goal completed by the input (its end date, `goal::status`) has no `on_complete`: it journals its one outcome, `discretionary_exit_all_then_retire`, under `goal_complete`, where `ref.py` writes `hold_protected`. (g) **Retirement** journals `AgentStopped` with max(0, N − E) and applies `retired`; a retired agent's profit stop is no longer confirmed, and later inputs still fold, as in `ref.py`. (h) **ES-13.** R4 adds 497 non-blank non-test lines (85 of doc comments) and removes 62, so it is 435 net, 35 over 400; item 5 fixes R4's contents, and the five inputs sit in their own module (`risk/fold/owner.rs`). (i) **All 24 MC-R cases pass on R4**; the status PR marks the last nine (DEC-77 item 3). | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | One slice per family keeps each review to one sitting. The risk-day reading is §5.4's sentence and nothing more: no stored length can disagree with the bounds, and the year-tiling test is the independent oracle. | One PR for all three families: the R fold alone is the crate's largest safety-critical piece. Opening L and R now with their tests still pending: it would add code that no live test covers. |
| DEC-168 | **Stream L's upstream sources for the tracer, each tests first: the instrument snapshot and latest quote in `mandate-alpaca` (E7-8), the agent-stream payload schemas (E7-9), and the control-stream schemas and their `JournaledFact` mapping (E7-10)** (the coordinator's rulings on [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171) of 2026-09-28, items 2 and the scope addition, which assigned this id; claim #171). Items 1 to 8 are E7-8's; E7-9 and E7-10 add theirs in their own PRs. (1) **Two reads, neither a `BrokerRequest`.** `TradingClient::asset` reads `GET /v2/assets/{symbol}` on the paper host (the allowlist's twelfth row, a `GET` and nothing else), the symbol written by `symbol_segment`, the same rule the positions path uses, so a pair is sent as `BTCUSD`. `DataClient::latest_quote` reads the data host. The executor's vocabulary is stream K's and is unchanged. (2) **The data host is kept apart by type.** A `QuoteRequest` has private fields and is built only from an instrument: `/v2/stocks/{symbol}/quotes/latest?feed=iex` for an equity (the paper profile's IEX feed, trading-domain spec §4.2, FR-2.7) and `/v1beta3/crypto/us/latest/quotes?symbols={base}%2F{quote}` for a pair. It goes only through a `DataTransport`, which cannot be handed an `HttpRequest`, and a `TradingTransport` cannot be handed a `QuoteRequest`, so neither host can be sent the other's request. ES-23 compiles in these two hosts and no other. `AlpacaPaperHttp` implements both traits with the same paper credentials. (3) **Numbers.** The data host sends prices and sizes as JSON numbers, so the read takes each number token's own text through `serde_json`'s `raw_value` (as `mandate-marketdata` does, no new dependency), refuses a string, a `null`, an exponent and more than 9 places, and parses with `mandate-num`; never an `f64`. The asset record's order constraints arrive as strings and follow `wire`'s rules, so a JSON number there is `float_number`. (4) **Every failure refuses** (`AGENTS.md` rule 3), as a `ReadError` with a stable code: `absent` (a `404`, a missing or `null` quote, a pair missing from `quotes`), `other_instrument` (the answer names another symbol), `one_sided` (a zero price on either side, which `Price` cannot hold and no mark or collar can use), `stale` (older than the caller's bound), `ahead_of_clock` (stamped after the clock's now), `overloaded` (a `429` or a `5xx`), `unexpected_status` (any other status, such as a `403` for a feed the account may not read), and the wire and transport codes. A crossed or locked quote is kept as sent: whether it is sane is its reader's (spec §4.1, §8.2; the builder's `CrossedQuote`, the gate's `SaneQuote`). A quote is read whole before its age is judged, and nothing is retried inside a read. (5) **Staleness bounds are the caller's.** `latest_quote(instrument, max_age)` and `AssetSnapshot::current(now, max_age)` take them, because the data profile's staleness threshold (§4.2) and §3.1's daily refresh are configuration; the crate holds no threshold of its own. Age is whole nanoseconds against the injected clock, and an age of exactly `max_age` is current. An asset snapshot is stamped with the clock's now when it is read. (6) **The asset fields read** are §3.1's that the gate, the eligibility floor and the executor use: the asset id, the symbol, the class (`us_equity` or `crypto`; any other is `wrong_type`), the exchange (§3.1's five codes, `OTC` and `CRYPTO`; any other is `Other`, which §3.2 item 2 makes ineligible), the status (`active` or `inactive`; any other is `wrong_type`), `tradable`, `fractionable`, the `ipo` and `ptp_no_exception` attributes (an equity must carry the `attributes` array, since its absence cannot say "not an IPO"), and the three order constraints, required for a pair and optional for an equity. The fields §3.1 marks "loaded; unused in v1" are not read, and the exit tier and the liquidity figures are not in the asset record, so they stay with their owners. (7) **Tests first** (DEC-77). The tests PR carries the request layer live (the allowlist row, `symbol_segment`, `QuoteRequest`, `DataTransport`), because it is the host boundary and has no stub to wait on, and the reads as stubs answering `ReadError::Unimplemented` under 21 tests `pending E7-8`. Every refusal case reads a good answer first, so an implementation that always refuses (`absent`, `stale`, `one_sided` or `not_json`) passes none of the 21, and every reading case asserts exact values. The fixtures are hand-built from Alpaca's documented shapes, because no paper credentials were available, and each says so in its README row; the manual paper run records the real ones. (8) **Not journaled here.** These reads are reference data, not broker exchanges on the order path, so this crate records no `BrokerExchangeRecorded` for them; whoever folds a snapshot into the effective-dated `ins` or takes a mark records what it used **E7-9's items** (journal spec v0.6 §9.1 and §11, DEC-177, DEC-252). (9) **Where the rules run.** `mandate-journal`'s `agent` module holds §9.1. `Draft::parse` takes a §9.1 schema by the stream type of `stream_id`, so an agent stream's `StreamOpened` and `KillSwitchActivated` take the agent stream's schema and every other stream keeps its own; on a well-typed payload it then runs rules 1 to 13, and after `artifact_refs` and `pii_refs` the subject rules 14 and 15 and the copy rule 16, in §9.1's order. Rule 10's batch clause is `check_batch`, which `MemoryJournal::append` and `PgJournal::append` both run once every draft of the batch has parsed, so the two backends cannot disagree. §11's `intent_action_mismatch` and `mode_event_mismatch` are `verify_agent_stream(rows, start)`, a range check run after `verify_events` passes, beside `verify_anchor`; wiring it into `mandate journal verify` and the scheduled verification stays the backlog row it is. The other agent-stream events (the approval events, `ModelInvocationRecorded`, `ThesisProposed`, `ThesisRevised`) stay `unknown_schema`. (10) **What changes for writers.** An agent stream's `StreamOpened` with an account subject is now refused as `schema` at its first unlisted member (`payload.account_ref`), where it was `stream_mismatch`, because §9.1 reads the members before rule 14; the two live cases that pinned the old outcomes (`draft_rejections`' agent `StreamOpened`, and the catalogue's `unknown_schema` for the eight types on an agent stream) move to pending tests with the new outcome. The runtime's agent-stream payloads are still the pre-§9.1 shape (the backlog's Stream I row), so they stay refused, now as `schema` rather than `unknown_schema`, and DEC-157 item 7 holds: nothing the runtime writes on the agent stream is newly accepted. (11) **Tests first** (DEC-77). The tests PR adds the harness for the vectors' `agent_stream` section to `mandate-refcases`' journal module (106 cases, pending under `status.toml` until the status change after the implementation), 14 pending tests in `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/agent_stream.rs` (one per §9.1 family, a sweep deleting each of §9.1's 69 members on every chain event of its type and setting each off its type, the members of the lists' items and of `step_up` included (#372 round 1, major 1), the batches, the range checks with the appended chain, that every expectation key is read, rule 10's decimals compared by value, and a `mode_event` before a range's trusted start left to the full-chain run, which no vector has: #340 round 1's backlogged `from_seq` above 1) and 2 in `mandate-journal`'s tests; each family holds an event the journal must accept and one it must refuse, so neither accepting nor refusing everything passes any of them. The stubs answer `InvalidReason::Unimplemented` (`unimplemented`) for every §9.1 draft, which refuses nothing that was accepted (seven of the eight types were `unknown_schema`, and an agent `StreamOpened` was `stream_mismatch` or `schema`), and `AgentStreamCheck::Unimplemented` for every range. The vectors stay version 3; the bump to 4 follows the implementation (DEC-177 item 6) (12) **The implementation lands as one PR.** The coordinator asked for about 400 net non-test lines; this one is about 580, and about 180 of those are the eight schema tables and their lookup, which are data. A split was tried and dropped: the rules without `check_batch` and `verify_agent_stream` would leave `mandate-journal` with no pending test, so the stubs left in it, and the routing that keeps a stub off every other append, would be live code no test judges and the mutation gate would fail. `check_batch` runs on every batch in both backends and is a no-op off the agent stream. The rules are also judged inside `mandate-journal`, by in-module tests that read the generated vectors (each chain event, valid and invalid draft, batch and range case), because the mutation gate runs only the mutated crate's own tests. #288's minor 3 rides here: `TransportError::RefusedPath` now reads as a refused paper trading or market data endpoint, and a connector request that left the process and got no answer reports `broker_outcome_unknown`, apart from `broker_request_not_sent` and `broker_answer_uninterpretable` (13) **§11's agent-stream range checks read the whole range** (#384 review, major 1 and minor 1; a tightening under DEC-176, ruled by the coordinator). `verify_agent_stream` indexes every event of the range before it checks any: an `IntentProposed` whose `causation_id` names a `DecisionMade` later in the range is compared with it and refused on a mismatch, and a `mode_event` that names an event in the range that is not earlier (a later event, or the switch itself) fails, whatever the range's `from_seq`. Only a reference to an event outside the range is left unchecked, and on a full chain (`from_seq` 1) a `mode_event` naming no event still fails. A writer never produces either forward reference, so the reading only refuses tampered or broken chains. In-module tests pin both witnesses; a generated forward-reference vector is a backlog row. Round 2's readings (#384): a present, non-null `mode_event` that is not a string names no event and is refused (`42`, `true`, `false`, `{}`, `[]`), never read as `null`; "earlier" is an event the walk has already passed, so a switch naming an `AgentModeChanged` with reason `kill_switch` later in the range is refused at every start that holds the switch; and the named event's type is checked on its own, so an earlier event with reason `kill_switch` that is not an `AgentModeChanged` is refused. Each clause has an in-module test that deleting the clause fails | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79; the coordinator's rulings on #171) | The coordinator placed both sources in `mandate-alpaca`, and the type split keeps the order path's allowlist exactly as strict as before while adding the one host ES-23 already permits | The quote in `mandate-marketdata` (against the ruling, and a layer-6 crate the shell would read quotes from beside its historical downloads); one transport trait for both hosts (a trading request could be dialled at the data host); a staleness constant in the crate (configuration the spec leaves to the profile); reading the numbers through `f64` (ES-23) |
| DEC-169 | **`ValidationContext` from journaled state: a pure fold with refusing defaults** (E10-1; the coordinator's rulings on [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171) and on [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124), which assigned this id; claim #124). (1) **Shape.** `ValidationContext::from_journal(&Mandate, ContextArgs, facts)` in `mandate-spec`'s `context` module folds a closed `JournaledFact` enum in journal order, with no dependency on `mandate-journal` (DEC-128 item 1 holds). The enum has twelve variants: `AccountSnapshot`, `ConnectionEstablished`, `ConnectionRevoked`, `DisclosureAccepted`, `AgentVersionActive` (`AgentDeployed`, or `MandateVersionApplied` for a deployed agent), `UniverseChanged`, `AgentStopped`, `AgentFlat`, `ModelRegistered`, `ModelWithdrawn`, `MandateVersionCreated` (each recorded path's source), and `MandateConfirmed` (the confirmed paths). The draft's provenance is folded from those last two, as the coordinator ruled on #124 and again on #255, and is not an argument. The draft's agent, its connection, the validation date, the workspace membership, the instrument groups, and the eligibility failures come in `ContextArgs` from their owners. Mapping journal records to facts is stream L's (E7-10, DEC-168), not this stream's. (2) **A missing fact refuses.** No equity snapshot means equity 0 (V-002). No registration means an empty registry (V-007), never `None`, because `None` means "not checked". No membership means zero users (V-024). No `MandateVersionCreated` for the draft's version means every envelope path is unconfirmed (V-020, item 5). A connection with no `ConnectionEstablished`, or one revoked since, is `live` (V-001 on a paper mandate), and a revocation also drops that connection's equity snapshot, so V-002 sees 0 until a new snapshot (#262 round 1). A live mandate on an unknown connection is refused by the platform policy's `environments: [paper]` (§4.3) rather than by a V-rule. The property the coordinator asked for is `with_every_fact_the_base_is_valid_and_without_any_one_it_is_refused_by_its_rule`. It covers the facts that have a refusing default and asserts the rule each absence must fire (equity V-002, environment V-001, registration V-007, disclosure V-005, membership V-024, provenance V-020). A fact that only adds (another agent's allocation, a claim, a retirement) cannot be known to be missing, so its absence is indistinguishable from its not having happened; the payload owner's completeness guarantees are what cover it. (3) **Readings.** Only facts about the draft's connection count toward equity, other allocations, claims, the environment, and the loss carry. The latest snapshot, environment, and version win. The draft's own agent is never "other". An `AgentStopped` retires an agent only when it names the connection of the agent's latest version; a stop naming another connection leaves the allocation and the claims counted, because rule 3 never lets an ambiguous record add V-002 headroom (#255 round 1). A retired agent holds no allocation until a later version redeploys it, and an `AgentFlat` after a stop that did not retire it releases nothing (#255 round 2, n1). Its claims (its latest version's pinned instruments and its working universe, which only `UniverseChanged` changes) stay until an `AgentFlat` after the retirement (trading spec §7.1). An `AgentFlat` for a live agent changes nothing. An agent with `UniverseChanged` facts and no `AgentVersionActive` has no known connection, so its admitted instruments count as claimed on every connection (V-006 fails closed, #262 round 1). The loss carry sums the connection's retirements at most `LOSS_CARRY_DAYS` = 90 days before the validation date, both ends included; a retirement dated after the validation date does not count (mandate spec §5.7, #255 round 1). A negative loss adds nothing rather than lowering the carry. The previous version is the draft agent's latest active version. A sum `Usd` cannot hold is `Err(Num(Overflow))`, the only error. (4) **Tests first** (DEC-77): the stub returns `SpecError::Unimplemented`, and 17 tests in `tests/context.rs` are `pending E10-1`. They include a property against an oracle that decides each quantity by its last deciding step rather than by replaying forward. A no-op fold, a constant context, and a fold that skips the checks each pass none of the 17. A throwaway implementation passes all 17, and each of 27 bugs planted in it fails at least one test. (5) **An envelope path the draft's provenance does not mention is unconfirmed** (the coordinator's ruling on #252, minor 4). `ProvenanceMap::at` reads an unmentioned path as `user_entered` and confirmed, and V-020 and V-022 read only the entries present, so a document with no entries passed both, even with `admission: auto`. `from_journal` therefore takes the draft. It reads the draft's own version's latest `MandateVersionCreated` for each recorded path's source, confirms a path only when the latest `MandateConfirmed` for that version names it or an ancestor of it, and adds a `user_entered`, unconfirmed entry for every part of the document no entry covers. The walk stops at a covered path, descends only where an entry lies below and the node has children, and never adds the system fields; a recorded path below a scalar or an empty array or object names nothing, so that node itself is added unconfirmed, and a path past the end of an array leaves each existing item uncovered, so each is added (#262 round 1, the blocker; an in-module property checks every document leaf ends up covered). V-020 then fires on each added entry, and an `auto` under one fires V-022 (`auto_with_no_provenance_is_v022`). `validate` itself and the MC-V cases, which state their provenance, are unchanged. No production caller may use `validate` or `ValidatedMandate::new` except through `from_journal` until this lands (backlog) | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79; option 1 accepted by the coordinator on #124) | A pure fold keeps the rules below the gate and the crate graph unchanged, and refusing defaults make a missing source a refusal rather than a skipped check (AGENTS.md rule 3) | The constructor in `mandate-shell` (rule-bearing defaults in the shell, against the #171 ruling's item 2); `mandate-spec` parsing journal payloads (reverses DEC-128 item 1 and waits on unowned schemas); an error for every missing fact (callers would learn to supply placeholders); `None` for an unknown registry or environment (skips V-007 or V-001) |

The repository, the crates (`mandate-*`), internal identifiers, and the working documents keep "Mandate", and nothing is renamed. Legal and compliance text naming the product stays a founder decision (DEC-79) | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | The founder bought owlhead.ai as the product's public name and home, and said "no need to rename anything". The codename is load-bearing across every crate, PR, and document in flight, and it does not need to match the brand | Renaming the repository and crates to Owlhead: churn across every open stream for no user-visible gain |
| DEC-172 | **E10-3's readings of §9: change classification as code** (stream F, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124); DEC-128 items 11 and 12, DEC-121, DEC-43). (1) **Pinned instruments compare by whole entry.** An entry the old list does not hold is an added instrument and increasing, including one whose `asset_id` stayed while its symbol or asset class changed; only removals are reducing; the same entries in another order are neutral, a reading that never decides step-up because a pure reorder is unreachable between valid versions (V-009 rejects an unsorted list, MC-V14). The reference implementation compares ids only and would call a changed symbol neutral; no case covers it, and §9.2's row names adding and removing only, so its last row (every other change is increasing) decides. (2) **`pinning_switch` reads the change's own paths.** Its `paths` must be `changed_paths` of the same two documents; any other list, shorter or longer, is `false`, the answer that needs step-up, so no caller can make a change reducing by leaving a path out. (3) **`classify_autonomy` of two identical rule sets is `Neutral`.** `classify` never asks, because nothing under `/autonomy` changed. (4) **The `change` harness arm compares all five members, and `step_up_required` may be absent only from a case that says `invalid`,** where step-up must be `false` (an invalid change is refused, not stepped up); a missing member anywhere else fails its case. `every_change_case_passes_and_fails_on_each_edited_expectation` edits and drops each member of each case. (5) **`null` and absent.** A `null` or absent maximum is unbounded, so a maximum removed is increasing and one set from `null` is reducing; a `null` or absent minimum is no minimum; a ladder `factor` that becomes `null` has risen; `crypto_stop_limit_offset` `null` means unset, so newly set is increasing and cleared is reducing; `end_date` `null` means no end. Decimals and integers compare by value through `SchemaDec`'s order, dates by text, which the schema's `YYYY-MM-DD` pattern makes date order. A value of the wrong type at a numeric path is a `ParseError`, never a guess. (6) **A new rule set that repeats a rule id is increasing.** §9.2's shapes identify a rule by its id, so none can be read off a repeated one; an id repeated only in the old set changes the count of kept ids, which the reorder check already calls increasing. (7) **MI-2 is the risk state's, not classification's.** "An applied allocation change never triggers or lifts a limit" is §5.1's, pinned under E6-4 in `tests/risk.rs`; `classify` reads two documents and no state, so it can trigger or lift nothing. Its counterpart here is a property: an allocation-only change is classified by its direction alone, whatever else the document says. (8) **`Invalid` wins, and needs no step-up.** A changed `environment` or `connection_id` is `Invalid` whatever else changed, including the pinning switch (which cannot contain those paths anyway), and `step_up_required` is `class == RiskIncreasing`, so `false`. (9) **The single-comparison shapes.** "One value changed so it matches less (more) often" is read for `lt`, `lte`, `gt`, and `gte` by the decimal moving, and for `in` and `not_in` by strict set containment; `eq`, `ne`, a changed field or operator, and a set that neither contains nor is contained by the old one are increasing. A rule whose condition is unchanged may only make its `then` stricter; a rule whose condition changed must keep its `then`. (10) **Paths come from the canonical values the version hashes,** walked object by object with arrays compared whole (DEC-128 item 11), so a reported path is exactly one that changes the hash; each path's verdict reads the typed field where the row has one and the canonical text otherwise. (11) **Scope.** The harness interprets all 48 family-C cases and the status PR marks every one that passes: the claim assigns MC-C01 to MC-C48 to E10-3, and the backlog's acceptance clause (MC-C01 to MC-C35) is its floor, not its ceiling. The diff view and the step-up flow itself are later stories. (12) **Readings the implementation adds** (E10-3's implementation PR). An absent member and a `null` one are different canonical values and different hashes, so a change between them is a changed path; every schema member is required, so this arises only between goal types (`accumulate`'s nullable `max_avg_price` against a goal without it), where `/goal/type` already makes the change increasing, so it can add a path to the list but never move the verdict. The reference's `dict.get` reads the two as equal. A same-set reordering of `asset_classes` is neutral, as pinned instruments are under item 1; the reference calls it reducing, and neither asks for step-up. Approvers compare as an ordered list, so reordering them is the "approvers" change §9.2 calls increasing. A mandate whose public fields no longer match the document it was parsed from has no canonical form, so `classify`, `changed_paths`, and `pinning_switch` refuse it (`diverged`) rather than classify fields the version would not hash. (13) **The `not_in` shapes are pinned in-module.** No `tests/change.rs` test and no MC-C case reaches a `not_in` rule (the MI-11 property generates `in` lists only), so reading `not_in` like `in`, or giving it no direction, passed every one of them and survived the mutation gate. `src/change/tests.rs` pins both directions for `auto` and for `ask` and `deny`, in the implementation PR, as #263 closed its survivors with module tests: `tests/` still changes only by deleting pending markers (DEC-77). (13a, 2026-09-30) Since #318's tests correction, the MI-11 property also generates `not_in` lists and `eq`/`ne` value moves, so it reaches both shapes too. The in-module pins stay, because a property's draw is not a pin. | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79) | The spec states each row's direction but not how an implementation reads an absent value, a repeated id, or a caller's path list; each reading here takes the side that asks for step-up when the spec is silent (AGENTS.md rule 3) and agrees with the reference implementation on all 48 cases | Compare pinned instruments by id (a symbol or class change would pass as neutral); trust the caller's path list in `pinning_switch` (a shortened list could make a change reducing); make `Invalid` require step-up (it is refused, so step-up would imply it could be approved); read MI-2 as a classification property (classification holds no state to trigger or lift) |
| DEC-173 | **The M7 spec readings: approval escalation v0 in mandate spec §6.1 and §6.4 and journal spec v0.5** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), DEC-77 stage 2, claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213); extends DEC-155, DEC-156, DEC-158, and DEC-165). (1) **The spec text and the reference model land before the MC-E cases.** `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/mandate_harness.rs` pins the fixture's total (298 cases, 300 tests), so adding MC-E01 to MC-E31 to `mandate.yaml` breaks a passing test, and the fix is a `crates/` change the spec guard keeps out of a spec PR. (2) **The content object has DEC-165 item 3's nine keys and nested names, plus `trigger.rule`**: the owner's confirmed rule `{id, when, then}` exactly as the mandate holds it, or null for `default` and `admission_ceiling`. That settles where DEC-165 item 3 left the rule text; it is owner-written, so the advice-wording scan must tell it apart from the platform's text (backlog, E8-1's follow-up). The `risk_impact` caps are `max_order_usd`, min(`max_position_usd`, `max_position_fraction` × E), min(`max_gross_exposure_usd`, E), null for `bought_today_usd`, the lowest ladder rung's `at`, and `max_daily_loss`. (3) **An assertion id is single-use per workspace, in control-stream order**: any earlier `ApprovalResponseSubmitted`, `OwnerCommandIssued`, or `OwnerAcknowledged` carrying it burns it, whatever that event's outcome. (4) **Malformed step-up evidence counts as missing.** (5) **A refused owner exit loses only the owner-exit privilege** (selling equities outside the regular session at the confirmed bid) and is still routed, in the regular session or at once for crypto, and never dropped (the coordinator's ruling on #275's minor 2). (6) **§6.1 states every owner control's step-up and when it is judged**: pause needs none; resume, Stop, and acknowledge are judged when processed; an owner exit and the kill switch's privileges when committed; the kill switch is never refused (DEC-158 option (c)). This is the spec line PX-4 asked for, which the web kill switch inherits. (7) **Quiet hours suppress push deliveries only**, replacing §6.4's old sentence that requests are not delivered in quiet hours and so time out, which contradicted DEC-156 item 6; product experience X3 is corrected to match. Item 7 rests on DEC-156 item 6 (the merged brief) and DEC-79, not on DEC-176: it makes the spec agree with code that already exists (`crates/mandate-approval/src/quiet.rs`), which DEC-176 keeps for the founder, so it is **flagged for the founder's after-the-fact look**. (8) **Every grant needs step-up, paper included** (`cli_confirm`), replacing "live approvals require step-up": stricter, and what DEC-155 item 4 already built. (9) **The runtime folds the scheduler's `ClockAdvanced` before it steps a tick, and every event a step writes carries that step's clock**, so a tick with nothing to time out still moves the clock that lateness is judged by. (10) **Each control-stream event is copied at most once**: one `ApprovalResponded` per `ApprovalResponseSubmitted`, however often it is re-tailed. (11) **Journal spec v0.5** splits the approval rows, adds `ApprovalRevalidated`, `ApprovalResponseSubmitted`, and `OwnerCommandIssued`, gives `DecisionMade` `ask_suppressed`, and reads a legacy `ApprovalCanceled` reason `rebound` as either `version_applied` or `mode_tightened`. The `mandate-journal` catalogue gains the three new types in the journal stream's own change, and closing the approval events' payload schemas, with their vectors, is DEC-174 item 4's journal spec change, which follows this one. (12) **The reference checks fail loudly**: `fuzz.py` and `check_cases.py` exit nonzero on any failure, which `cargo xtask ci reference` relied on without having it. The fuzz asserts MI-21 to MI-25 against oracles of their own (clock, pending set, grant sets, assertion ledger, scaled-integer drift, a New York day counter, a minute-walk quiet-hours window, and a transition table), and `mutants.py` gains 52 planted escalation bugs (46, item 13's four, and #355's two), every one caught. (13) **Check 7 judges a grant against the stricter of the bound approver requirement and the workspace policy overlay current at the response's effective time** (the coordinator's ruling on the #321 review's major). `independent_approval_required` is a workspace policy key (mandate spec §4.3, DEC-128 item 30(b)), not a mandate field, and a `PolicyChanged` applies to running agents as an overlay where the stricter value wins; reading only the values bound at the request let an admin who turned on maker-checker, or lowered `two_approver_above_usd`, leave a pending approval grantable by the mandate's author alone, a silence that resolved to the looser side (AGENTS.md rule 3). Independence is required if either requires it; the approver count is the larger of the two, so a lowered ceiling can only raise it; an author's earlier `counted` grant stops counting once independence is required; and a policy change never loosens a pending approval. The runtime folds `PolicyChanged` as it folds `ClockAdvanced`, and `ApprovalResponded` records the count and independence applied. The reference model folds `PolicyChanged`, the fuzz oracle keeps its own record of the current policy, and four planted bugs (the bound value alone, the looser of the two, the lowered ceiling ignored, the author's earlier grant counted) are caught. `mandate-approval`'s `admit.rs` still reads only the bound values; the M7 tests correction brings it in line (backlog, E8-3's follow-up). (14) **The M7 tests correction's shape for check 7** (DEC-77 tests first). `AdmissionContext` gains `policy: PolicyOverlay`, the two policy keys (`independent_approval_required`, and `two_approver_above_usd` as an exact `Usd` or none) as the runtime folded them before the step, and `quorum(bound, policy) -> Quorum` names check 7's requirement, the value `ApprovalResponded` records, so the runtime journals the requirement admission judged rather than one it derived again. Checks 1 to 6 and a skip are unaffected, and two live tests say so. The same PR draws the 300 s step-up edge on every seed of `tests/grant_properties.rs` and gives both one-sided drift tests the paired positive, "the unchanged fixture acts". (15) **The quorum property draws only grant sets an admission could have folded.** The bound requirement never changes, so a request that bound independence can never hold its author's grant, and the property's generator draws one only when independence is not bound. On that unreachable state today's `admit` counts the author, where `ref.py` does not; the implementation drops the author from the grants that count whenever check 7 requires independence, whatever the overlay, as `ref.py` does. The tests were shown against a throwaway implementation of `quorum`, not committed: all eight pending tests pass on it over five fresh seeds, and each of five planted bugs (the bound values alone, the looser of the two, the lowered ceiling ignored, an inclusive ceiling, the author's earlier grant counted) fails the property and at least one hand case | Accepted (agent, DEC-79 and DEC-176: items 1 to 6 and 8 to 12 tighten a rule or make the spec agree with accepted decisions; item 13 is a pure tightening under DEC-176 clause (1); items 14 and 15 are the M7 tests correction's readings, which fail closed and change no spec; item 7 rests on DEC-156 item 6 and DEC-79 instead, and is flagged for the founder's after-the-fact look) | Each reading turns an act into a skip or a refusal, or makes a check stricter, except the kill switch, which DEC-158 option (c) already resolved toward the stop and flatten, and item 7, which lets an overnight request be granted from the inbox with the same step-up and re-validation, so the founder may veto it. | DEC-174 | **E7-9 cannot be tested first yet: journal spec v0.4 closes no agent-stream payload schema and its test vectors hold no agent-stream event, so no agent-stream schema is registered until a journal spec change supplies both** (stream L, claim [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171); DEC-168; the acting backend coordinator's dispatch in the coordinator log on [#165](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/165), which assigned this id). (3) **Where the runtime's payloads contradict the spec**, each a ruling for the spec change rather than for `mandate-journal`: empty strings where §4.2 requires `null` (`DecisionMade.reason_code` on an allow, `ApprovalResponded.step_up` on a denial, and `OwnerExitRequested`'s `bid`, `bid_size`, `floor`, `user` and `step_up` when nothing was confirmed); instants written as risk-clock seconds, an integer in `ApprovalRequested.deadline` and decimal text in `ObservationRecorded.at`, `ModelOutputRecorded.as_of` and `expires_at` and `ApprovalResponded.at`, where §4.4 admits integers only for counts and durations and §4.7 and the `FillApplied` vector write a timestamp string; `ObservationRecorded.data` and `ModelOutputRecorded.content` written inline, where §9 names an artifact and a content hash; `IntentProposed` as `instrument`, `side`, `qty`, `limit` and `purpose`, where the `IntentReceived` vector's intent fields are `intent_id`, `agent_id`, `instrument_id`, `side`, `type`, `tif`, `qty`, `limit_price` and `purpose`; key fields §9 lists and the runtime does not write (`DecisionMade`'s outputs used, model weights, clips applied and combined conviction; `ModelOutputRecorded`'s content hash, direction, conviction, confidence, horizon and `ignored` reason; `ApprovalRequested`'s content shown; `ApprovalResponded`'s responder role, step-up evidence parts and separation-of-duties result); and members §9 does not list (`AgentModeChanged.lifecycle`, from DEC-131 item 25(h); `KillSwitchActivated.subject` and `mode_event`; `OwnerExitRequested.bid_size` and `floor`). E7-9's tests PR then follows DEC-77 against those vectors, and any runtime change the rulings need is stream I's. (5) **Found, outside E7-9:** the executor's account-stream drafts fail the registered schemas and vectors too. Every draft carries an integer `risk_clock` (`batch.rs`), where the `FillApplied` vector has a timestamp string and the `IntentReceived`, `GateDecided` and `OrderSubmitted` vectors have none; `IntentReceived` writes `agent`, `kind`, `instrument` and `limit` where its vector has `agent_id`, `instrument_id`, `limit_price`, `type` and `tif`; and `OrderSubmitted` omits its optional members rather than writing `null` (§4.2). | DEC-175 | **An approved pull request merges when the coordinator names its reviewed head and the `merge` workflow finds CI green on that head** (carries out DEC-79; amended 2026-09-30 after the independent review of #313, which found that a label alone was not bound to a commit). On the step-3 PASS of the [ship playbook](../../.cursor/skills/mandate-mode/playbooks/ship.md), from a reviewer on a different model (DEC-79), the coordinating agent writes `Coordinator-approved-head: <full sha>` into the pull request's description and adds the `coordinator-approved` label. The label is the act of approval: GitHub lets only people and apps with triage access or above apply it. The line binds that approval to one commit by convention, not by a GitHub permission, since a pull request's author can edit its description; builders here have no pull request tool and the coordinator writes the descriptions, so a builder's tooling can at worst drop the line or leave a stale sha, and both refuse the merge. The line stands alone inside the description's body, outside any code fence, exactly once; the key is case-sensitive, the sha is all 40 hex digits in either case, and a leading `- ` or `* ` is allowed. `.github/workflows/merge.yml` runs on the label, on a description edit, when a pull request leaves draft, on every completed `ci` or `web` run, and on a half-hourly sweep of every labelled pull request, so that an event GitHub drops never strands an approval. It calls `.github/scripts/merge-approved.sh`, which re-reads everything from GitHub, never from the event, reading again up to three times while GitHub is still computing mergeability, and merges only when all of these hold: the pull request is open, not a draft, targets `main`, and carries the label; its description names exactly one approved sha, and that sha is its current head; GitHub reports it `MERGEABLE`; and the latest `ci` run from a `pull_request` event on that head succeeded, plus the latest `web` run when it touches `web/`, since DEC-112 lets `fast` and `full` skip the web checks. The merge names that head, so a push after the reads makes GitHub refuse it, and a push before them moves the head off the approved sha. The commit message is the description's marked body with any `Co-authored-by` line removed; an empty one refuses the merge rather than fall back to GitHub's default (ship step 5). The title and the description's wording are read at merge time and are not bound by the approval; they change only the commit's wording, never which commit merges. The workflow runs from the default branch and never checks out the pull request's code. Two approved pull requests may merge close together; one left conflicting is skipped as not mergeable, and needs `main` merged in and a new approval. `xtask`'s tests drive the script against a stub `gh` for every refusal. The founder turned off "Require review from Code Owners" on the `main` ruleset, 2026-09-30, so that the workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN` can merge; code owners are still requested for review. Merges by that token start no other workflow, so `nightly` stays the check on `main` itself | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79, on the founder's go-ahead of 2026-09-30 to the coordinator's proposal) | The founder asked how to stop being the merge bottleneck and answered "Yes do everything you need to do" to this plan; the wording and mechanism are the coordinator's, and the founder can amend or revert them. DEC-79 already lets agents merge their own work, but the coordinating cloud session has a read-only `gh` and no merge action, so every approved pull request waited for the founder. It can edit a description and add a label, and the description line binds the approval to one commit without a job that has to revoke it | A label alone, with a job that removes it on push (the first version: GitHub may cancel a queued job, so an approval could outlive the push it should end); a commit status per approved sha (sha-bound, but this session cannot post one); GitHub auto-merge (needs `allow_auto_merge`, uses GitHub's default squash message with the trailers, and is not bound to a reviewed head); a bot account or app token with bypass (a new credential to hold and rotate); the founder keeps merging by hand (the bottleneck this removes) |
| DEC-176 | **Standing delegation: agents accept spec and reference-case changes that only tighten a rule, or that resolve a gap by the reading that adds no risk** (extends DEC-79). An agent may accept, as "Accepted (agent, DEC-176)", a change to `docs/specs/`, a schema, or a reference-case YAML when it (1) makes a rule stricter, or (2) closes a gap or contradiction between the spec and the code, or between two specs, by choosing the reading that adds no risk (AGENTS.md rule 3); and (3) it weakens no safety invariant and no `AGENTS.md` non-negotiable. Such a change goes through the full DEC-79 path: tests first, an independent review on a different model, and green CI; for a reference-case change, the YAML, `cargo xtask refcases --write`, and the reference implementation's checks in the same pull request. Where two readings are open and neither is clearly the safer, the agent takes the more conservative one, records it here as Proposed, and continues (rule 9). Still reserved for the founder (DEC-79): live trading, live credentials, or real money; spending; legal and compliance text; and weakening any safety rule. Making the spec agree with more permissive code is weakening, and stays the founder's. The founder reviews after the fact and can revert | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79, on the founder's go-ahead of 2026-09-30 to the coordinator's proposal) | The founder answered "Yes do everything you need to do" to the coordinator's proposal of this delegation. Most founder-waiting items were spec gaps whose safe reading was not in doubt (DEC-174's journal schemas, E6-8's open questions), and each held a stream for a day. Tightening and reconciling cannot add risk, and the DEC-79 review still checks each one | Every spec change waits for the founder: the bottleneck this removes; agents may change any spec rule: would let an agent weaken a safety rule, which DEC-79 reserves |
| DEC-178 | **How the `mandate-refcases` harness reads families G, F, and L** (DEC-77 stage 4, item 3; stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123), and stream F, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). The YAML header says only "gate: the mandate limits of spec 5.3, in trading spec 9.1 order; every other check passes", "agent_flatten: the agent-scoped kill-switch plan", and "goal: goal completion for the given state"; these are the readings that turn each into a call on merged code. (1) **Where.** `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/risk_gate.rs` interprets `kind: gate` through `mandate_risk::evaluate` and `kind: agent_flatten` through `mandate_risk::agent_flatten`; family L already runs through the suite's `goal` arm and `mandate_spec::goal::status` (E6-4), so it needs only its status rows. `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/mandate_gate_harness.rs` proves the two new arms read every member and compare every expectation. `crates/mandate-risk/tests/refcases.rs`, the crate-local copy whose own doc says it moves here, stays until a follow-up deletes it (backlog). (2) **The full gate, with [DEC-150](#decisions) item 1's `FULL_GATE_ONLY`.** The one entry, MC-G02, also pins the verdict, reason, and unreached `gross` and `gross_limit` values the case states, so an edit to the case fails at the entry instead of passing through it. (3) **"Every other check passes" is an environment in which they do**, so the case's own limits decide. The risk state is healthy at the case's `agent_equity`: high-water mark, day-start equity, and capital base all equal it, the size factor is 1, the mode is normal, and nothing is latched. The account is a margin account, active, in the intraday-margin regime, with equity, maintenance excess, and every buying power at the fixture's `validation_context_defaults.account_equity_usd` (25000). It holds exactly what the agent holds (positions, market values, working orders), so check 7's account 1× bound is evaluated against a real account rather than an empty one. The instrument is a NASDAQ US equity: active, tradable, unhalted, whole-share, plain (not an ETP), status feed current, median dollar volume $90M. Its prior close and its quote (bid = ask) are the proposal's limit price at `now`, with 5-minute volume 1,000,000 and 20-day ADV 10,000,000. The conduct state is empty, and the proposal is a plain day limit order in the regular session with no fee reservation. The gate configuration is the trading-domain `test_default`, plus the five settings it does not carry at `mandate-risk`'s own `test_default_config` values. A case proposing an instrument the base does not pin, or anything but a US equity, fails in the harness, because check 2 still owes crypto's USD-pairs rule to E6-10. (4) **Positions and orders.** The cases state dollars only. Each `positions_mv` entry becomes a one-share position with that market value, since the limits read market values, and a sell's own instrument holds exactly the sell's quantity. Each working opening order is the agent's own: a buy, one share open, opening, not protective, submitted on the New York date of `now`. They take client order ids 1 to n in listed order, and the proposal takes n + 1. Group names become group ids by their rank in text order. (5) **The purpose is assigned, never injected** (DEC-129 item 19). The harness picks the `Origin` and side the purpose implies: open and increase are an order-builder buy, a discretionary exit an order-builder sell, a risk exit a risk-engine sell, an owner exit an owner close, and a protective order a protective leg. The gate's own assignment must then equal the case's `purpose`. (6) **The verdict and its reason are compared together with the checks.** A denial must have exactly one failing check, carrying the stated reason, and an allow must report all eight checks passed, in §9.1's order (item 11). Every `computed` figure the case states must be reported with that value, and a figure the gate did not report fails. A case states only the figures its title is about, so an unstated figure is not compared. (7) **`agent_flatten` names.** The fixture names client order ids and agents with strings, and the crate uses integers. Each order's id is the rank of its name among the case's names, so the plan's ascending ids are the names in text order, and the names must be unique. Agents are numbered by first appearance. (8) **The owner's bid.** `owner_confirmed_bid: true` requires a `confirmed_bid`, and `false` forbids one. `max_exit_offset` is required with a confirmed bid; otherwise it is zero, which no plan reads because the floor exists only for an owner's confirmed bid. No case sets an explicit floor price. The initiator is `risk_limit` or `owner`; any other value fails. (9) **The plan is compared whole.** The mode, the purpose, the cancels as an ordered list, and both endpoint flags are compared, and so is each sell with its pricing, floor, and remainder. An absent `floor_price` means no floor and an absent `remainder` means none, so a plan that adds either fails. A deferred sell's `until` must be `regular_session_open`, the only deferral `DeferredSell` means. (10) **Status.** MC-G01 to MC-G16 and MC-F01 to MC-F04 pass under E6-3, and MC-L01 to MC-L05 under E6-4. MC-G13, an allowed reopen, reaches checks 5 and 6; the harness held it pending while they failed closed (DEC-129 item 29), and it passes since E6-8 ([#311](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/311)) made them whole, so it is swept with the other fifteen, and `the_allowed_reopen_passes_the_conduct_checks_e6_8_made_whole` also denies it `close_window` at check 6 five minutes before the close. The rows land in their own pull request, because the spec guard keeps `status.toml` apart from code (ES-22), as #309 and #310 did for family C. (11) **An allow passes all eight checks.** With E6-8 merged, an allowed US-equity order is `Passed` at every check in §9.1's order, so the arm compares the whole list with that, and an allow cannot rest on a check the gate listed as not reached. MC-G13 is decided that way now, and so would MC-G02 be once restated: as a $1,300 position with no working order it is an allowed opening, an increase, and against that state the full gate allows it and the arm reports its `FULL_GATE_ONLY` entry expired (the backlog's MC-G02 row). The pin holds only for an allowed opening: for an exit the gate lists checks 2 and 5 to 8 as `Passed` without running them, since they apply to openings, so on MC-G08 to MC-G10 and MC-G15 it can catch only checks 1, 3 and 4 or a list of the wrong length or order. A gate that lists check 8 as not reached on an allowed opening fails MC-G13 alone. No fixture edit reaches the pin, so `an_allow_fails_unless_it_reports_every_check_passed_in_order` edits the gate's report instead: each check listed as not reached and as failed, the list cut short, a check listed twice, and two neighbours swapped each fail, naming the list. (12) **The copied gate configuration is checked against the fixture.** `configs.test_default.gate` is in `fixtures/refcases/trading-domain.json`; the mandate fixture has no `configs`. The fixture's `price_floor`, `liquidity_floor_usd`, the five `collar` members, `opposite_fill_interval_s`, `order_to_fill.max` and `.min_orders`, `close_window_minutes`, and `legacy_pdt_equity_threshold` are compared by value with the twelve `GateConfig` fields they fill, after `DecStr` normalises the fixture's text (`"5.00"`, `"0.20"`), which the strict parsers refuse. Its other eight members (`data_profile`, `unexplained_403_threshold`, `crypto_stop_limit_offset`, `stop_watchdog_s`, `protective_replace_buffer_trading_days`, `bracket_partial_fill_timeout_s`, `max_unprotected_s`, `exit_ladder`) belong to other crates and are named so that the fixture's member list must match exactly. The remaining five fields are compared with `mandate-risk`'s `test_default_config` values typed a second time in the test, because that function is in `mandate-risk`'s integration tests (`tests/common`), which another crate cannot call; the second copy catches an edit to the harness's first, not a change to `mandate-risk`'s. `GateConfig` is destructured whole, so a new field does not compile until it is compared. (13) **A failure starts with what it is about.** Every arm message starts with the member it compared (`verdict: `, `computed.gross: `, `sells[1].pricing: `), and the harness tests match a failure by that prefix rather than by substring, so a failure for another reason that mentions the member later (`the full gate's verdict and reason: …`) does not count. Sells and deferred sells are compared count first, then member by member in order, each struct destructured whole. The copied config and the prefixes are the #317 review's minor 1 and nit 2. (14) **The gate's `pacing` is compared, as none.** E6-8 gives an allowed order a `pacing`, and no `gate` case states one. For an opening it is `None` by the gate's own contract, a denial carries none, and each allowed exit (MC-G08 to MC-G10, MC-G15) is proposed at the scene's quote, outside the close window, and far inside both participation caps, so nothing paces it. The arm therefore compares `pacing` with `None` on every `gate` case, and takes `Decision` apart whole, so a member the crate adds does not compile until it is compared. A `pacing` that always sets `marketable_limit_required` fails MC-G08 to MC-G10, MC-G13 and MC-G15 at `pacing: ` (before, every G case stayed green under it), and `an_allowed_exit_the_gate_paces_fails_since_no_case_states_a_pacing` moves MC-G08 into the close window, where the gate paces it, and fails it there. The values of an exit's pacing stay pinned by `mandate-risk`'s own tests (`hand.rs`'s `a_participation_cap_slices_and_never_denies` and `gate.rs`'s in-module collar and slice tests). Group ids are the ranks of the case's own group names in text order, built from those names, so no name lacks a rank and the arm has no error for one | Accepted (agent, reversible engineering, DEC-79) | The header leaves the environment, the names, and the optional members open. Each reading drives the merged production code rather than a copy of `ref.py`, and none can let a case pass while part of it goes unread (DEC-85). Eight bugs planted in `mandate-risk` (concentration at `>=`, orders per day at `>`, a cooldown that ignores groups, a gross limit on account equity, a flatten selling the broker quantity, swapped flatten modes, cancelling every agent's orders, and the cancel-all endpoint) each fail at least one case. After E6-8, six more planted in checks 4 to 6 (the collar's buy ceiling measured down from the ask, the one-working-order rule counting the agent's orders in any instrument, each participation cap compared the wrong way, the close window inverted, and a close window that never denies) each fail MC-G13 or its close-window variant | An empty account, as the crate-local harness has, which leaves check 7's account bound untested; a position the size of the case's dollars at the limit price, which would make a position's quantity depend on an unrelated price; reading `max_exit_offset` as required everywhere, which would fail cases that never price a floor; comparing sells as a set, which would miss a reordered plan |
| DEC-179 | **How the family-N harness decides the three first orders (MC-N01, MC-N14, MC-N26) through `mandate_builder::classify`** (stream H at the coordinator's dispatch, stream J being idle; claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125); DEC-132 item 3, DEC-154). The arm is `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/research.rs`; nothing is re-implemented. (1) **The policy is the patched base through the strict parse, unvalidated**, as family A's is (DEC-162 item 1): the admission cases state no validation context, and `classify` re-checks the rules it reads. (2) **Admission's reported ceiling must equal the parsed policy's `autonomy.admission`.** `FirstOrderFacts.admission_ceiling` is the overlay's effective admission, no family-N case states a policy, and the overlay is `PolicyOverlay::permissive()`, so the two must agree; a mismatch fails the case naming both, rather than one of them being ignored. `classify` reads the ceiling from the policy, so this check is what reads the reported one. (3) **The action is `admission_action` as stated, with `purpose` `open`, and `new_instrument` and `thesis_confidence` from admission's `FirstOrderFacts`**, as `ref.py`'s `admit` composes it (`dict(action, purpose="open", new_instrument=True, thesis_confidence=...)`). The action's `instrument` is taken as stated: in MC-N01 and MC-N26 it names `…0002` while the admitted thesis names `…0005`, which `ref.py` also keeps. No rule in `research_equity` or `research_equity_etp` reads `instrument`, so the outcome cannot turn on it (a fixture observation for the founder, not changed here). (4) **`unusual_input` must be `false`.** V-018 reserves the field until the input-drift detector ships, `ActionContext` has no place for it, and `ref.py` marks it unavailable; a `true` fails the case naming it, rather than being classified as if it were false. (5) **The classification is compared whole, as JSON**, the way this module compares journal rows: `decision` and `by`, plus `approvers_required` and `on_timeout` exactly when §6.4 gives the decision an approval, so an approval member stated or omitted wrongly fails. The decision's spelling is the harness's own. (6) **The oracle.** `every_first_order_fact_reaches_its_own_field` reads the built facts back through `Facts` with admission's facts set to `false` and a confidence no stated member holds, since the fixture's thesis confidence equals its `combined_score`; `an_unusual_first_order_input_fails_naming_it` covers item 4; and the existing doctoring tests now require every doctored member of the three cases to fail, with the pending exemption removed. Four harness plants were each caught: the confidence taken from `combined_score` and `new_instrument` fixed at `true` (by the facts test), the `unusual_input` check dropped (by its test), and the approval members never compared (by the three cases). Five production bugs, each seeded and reverted, were each caught: in `mandate-builder`, step 5 skipped, the ceiling labelled with the rule it overrode, and an ASK with no approval (all three cases each); in `mandate-research`, a renewal reported with `new_instrument` false (MC-N14 alone) and an `auto` ceiling reported (all three, through item 2). (7) **The status rows move in a status-only PR** stacked on the harness, as DEC-162 item 7 does for family A (ES-22) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Harness readings only: no production crate changes, and each reading makes a case fail sooner rather than pass on something unread. Following `ref.py`'s composition keeps the harness an independent reading of the reference rather than of the crate | Validate the policy (tests rules family N does not state inputs for); classify against a policy with admission's ceiling substituted in (composes an overlay step `classify` does not own, DEC-128 item 9); use the thesis's instrument for the action's (diverges from `ref.py` and leaves the stated `instrument` unread); ignore `unusual_input` (an unread member, DEC-85) |
| DEC-16 | **No external durable-execution engine.** Durable timers and long waits (an approval pending for hours, "run until December") are journal-backed: a timer is a journaled event in the Postgres journal, read back behind the `TimerSource` trait, and when it fires it enters the runtime as a journaled input like any other, so the gate and the executor decide from journal state. The journal stays the single source of truth (rule 5; HLD "Durability") | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-27) | State is event-sourced: every change is journaled first and state is the fold of the journal. A durable-execution engine keeps its own log of where each workflow is, which would make a second source of truth that can disagree with the journal after a crash (a timeout the engine fired against an approval the journal recorded), the class of bug the journal exists to prevent. No new infrastructure, no licence question | Restate (a second durable log; the server's licence would need checking); Temporal (a second durable log, a server cluster to run, no mature Rust SDK) |
| DEC-17 | Messaging between the processes of a workspace deployment. **Recommendation (2026-09-26, coordinator, for the start of M5 per ADR-0001 ES-20):** Phase 1 uses Postgres `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` plus journal tailing on the workspace's Postgres, with intents and timers behind the in-process `IntentSink` and `TimerSource` traits; one process per agent deployment (DEC-08) and one Postgres per workspace deployment mean no new service and no message-bus-shaped abstraction in the core. NATS JetStream is reconsidered at M8, when the control plane and many deployments need fan-out. The E6-1 brief (stream I) is built on this recommendation | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-26): Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY plus journal tailing for Phase 1; NATS JetStream reconsidered at M8 | The journal is already the durable log (ES-20); one dependency set on the edge; a single binary; NATS is Apache-2.0 and stays available | NATS JetStream now; Kafka / Redpanda; Redis Streams |
