A flat silhouette keeps DEC-200's flat colour, takes `currentColor`, and still reads at favicon size, where shading turns to mud. Outlines render the same on every device, cost no font request, and keep the font file out of the repository | Use the shaded original in the UI (not flat colour, DEC-200 item 8); keep the typographic "OWLHEAD" (the founder supplied a mark); load P052 as a web font (a font request and a distributed font file); an agent-drawn or redrawn mark (DEC-201 forbids it) |
| DEC-204 | **The web UI is redesigned to a consumer-grade standard, in Ink and Gold, light and dark** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-28, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4, claim [#297](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/297)). (1) **Standard.** The owner-facing screens reach the polish of Robinhood, Public or Wealthfront without their gamification: no confetti, streaks, badges, celebratory motion, or anything that rewards trading. The [product-experience brief](../product/09-product-experience.md) still binds and wins over any design choice; Home's primary action stays "open an agent or an approval". (2) **Structure.** One hero number per screen and one chart as its centre (a scrubbable equity chart on Home and on each agent, with the mandate's levels as labelled price lines), generous space, few boxes (lists are rows on the page; a panel appears only when it carries meaning), and purposeful motion that respects reduced motion. The header keeps Stop as its dominant control; a quiet sidebar on desktop and a tab bar on phones replace the signage bands. **Amended by [DEC-206](#decisions), [DEC-207](#decisions) and [DEC-208](#decisions):** Stop is quiet until something needs the owner, rather than the header's dominant control (DEC-206); the phone's tab bar is Home, Approvals, Agents and More (DEC-207); on desktop a labelled floating dock replaces the quiet sidebar, and the header gains a wide command bar and folding breadcrumbs (DEC-208). (3) **Type.** Mona Sans (OFL-1.1, one variable family, weights up to 600, sentence case, tabular figures) replaces the signage type; there are no capitals-only labels or heavy bands. **Superseded in part by [DEC-209](#decisions):** the one face is Public Sans; the rest of this item stands. (4) **Two densities.** Calm for the retail-facing screens an owner lives in (Home, agents, approvals) and dense for audit, settings and connections; both share every token and keep 44 px touch targets on phones. (5) **Palette: Ink and Gold, in a light and a dark theme.** Light: ink #14161A for type, primary actions and the Stop control, on off-white #FDFCFA (the body and cards) and paper #F8F7F4 (wells); gold #AB7D13 for lines, rails and marks, dark gold #6A4D08 for gold as text, and the gold tint #FFF6E6 for the mandate's field. Dark: the same ramps from the other end, with off-white #F8F7F4 type on ink #14161A and night #0B0D11, gold #D9A948 for lines, #F9D28A for gold text and #3C2E14 for the field. Crimson #9C0C12 is the kill switch alone in both themes (its edge is #FD8C81 in dark). Gold is used only through the gold tokens, never as text lighter than dark gold in light mode, and never as a large block. Every semantic pair and every Kumo role pair meets WCAG 2.2 AA in both themes, and APCA is checked in the development tests only. Gains and losses carry a sign and a word; with colour-blind friendly on, a gain is blue and a loss raspberry in light or orange in dark, each kept apart from gold and crimson under simulated deuteranopia and protanopia. `web/COLOR.md` holds the ramps, the tokens, the gold rules and the contrast and colour-vision tables. **Supersedes [DEC-202](#decisions)** (navy and brass). **Amended by [DEC-205](#decisions):** the accent is ultramarine on a cool white page (Ink and Ultramarine), and the colour-blind gain is teal; the rest of this item stands. (6) **Logo.** The Owlhead mark and wordmark ([DEC-203](#decisions)) are ink on light and off-white on dark, following the theme; the favicon, the app icons and the Open Graph image are the ink mark on an off-white tile; there is still no tagline. **Amends DEC-203** item 3 and its 2026-09-28 amendment (navy on a light surface, always). (7) **What stays.** [DEC-200](#decisions) item 8 (flat colour, no gradients, the Impeccable detector); the routes, fixtures and drill-downs; and every safety behaviour already in `web/` (Stop always visible and never disabled, the Stop sheet's order with step-up and cancel, frozen kill and release records, role gates, equal Approve and Skip with a static deadline, nothing optimistic, crimson only on the kill switch, paper labelling, generic titles and opaque identifiers, and the performance disclosure beside every P&L). (8) **Supersedes** the structure and signage type of the Placard direction in `web/DESIGN.md` (the founder's pick of 2026-09-28); `web/DESIGN.md` records the system that replaces it | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28) | An owner checks on agents that trade for them, and the screens they live in should answer "how am I doing, and does anything need me" at a glance: one number and one chart do that better than signage bands and boxed panels. Polish earns trust in a product that handles money, while gamification would nudge trading, which the brief's No-Nudge rule forbids. The founder previewed Ink and Gold in both themes and approved it: ink and paper keep most of the screen quiet, a single gold accent gives the mandate one place for the eye to go, and a dark theme is expected of a consumer app. Holding gold to lines and dark-gold text keeps it legible and rare, and the contrast and colour-vision tests hold both themes to the same bar DEC-202 set. | Keep Placard (its signage structure reads as an operations console, not a consumer product); a consumer look with gamification (celebrations and streaks nudge trading, against the brief); keep navy and brass (DEC-202; the founder chose Ink and Gold); light only (a consumer app is expected to follow the system theme); gold as body text (gold-500 fails 4.5:1 on paper); one density everywhere (too sparse for audit logs or too tight for Home) |
| DEC-205 | **The web UI's accent is ultramarine, on a cool white page: Ink and Ultramarine** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, chosen from a live preview of seven accents (gold, iris, ultramarine, petrol, jade, plum, graphite) and page colours on the real screens, recorded by the Cursor session; stream W4, following [#299](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/299)). (1) **Values.** Light: ink #14161A type on cool paper #FBFDFE (the body and cards) and #F5F7F9 (wells), secondary type #3F4348; ultramarine #547EEE for lines, rails and marks, deep ultramarine #2F4C9D for ultramarine as text and for the focus ring, the ultramarine tint #F3F7FF for the mandate's field, and #D5E1FE for selected text. Dark: #F5F7F9 type (secondary #D5D8DB) on ink #14161A and night #0B0D11; ultramarine #8EAFFC for lines and marks, #C6D7FE for ultramarine text and the focus ring, #242F4A for the field and #2E406D for selected text. Crimson #9C0C12 is still the kill switch alone. (2) **The ramps.** The accent ramp is ultramarine at OKLCH hue 266 on the shared lightness curve: vivid but not neon at steps 400 to 700 (chroma at most 0.175, clamped to 97% of the sRGB gamut) and a quiet tint at 100, 850 and 900 (chroma 0.05 or less). Paper and ink move to hue 255 at their existing low chroma. Every token keeps its name and meaning, and ultramarine takes gold's place in every rule and test. (3) **Contrast.** Every semantic pair and every Kumo role pair still meets WCAG 2.2 AA in both themes, and APCA in the development tests. The focus ring holds at least 7.4:1 against every surface in light and 9.1:1 in dark, and the marks hold 3.49:1 against the page. (4) **Colour-blind friendly.** The blue gain sat 21 degrees from the new accent and merged with ultramarine text under red-green deficiency, so the gain is now teal (hue 205; #0B5E65 light, #72EDFA dark). The light loss is one step deeper raspberry (#521537) so a teal gain and a raspberry loss stay apart for deuteranopes, and the dark loss stays orange. Tritanopia is now simulated alongside deuteranopia and protanopia, and the accent's marks, gain, loss and crimson stay apart (OKLab ΔE 0.1 or more) under all three. Gain against ultramarine text is required to stay apart under deuteranopia and protanopia in light mode, and is information only in dark mode and under tritanopia, where no gain hue at text lightness separates from it; gains still carry a sign and a word. The warning amber stays off every screen, now because at text lightness it sits beside the colour-blind orange loss. (5) **Brand.** The logo stays ink on light and off-white on dark; the favicon, app icons and Open Graph image are regenerated on the cool off-white tile #FBFDFE. **Supersedes DEC-204's gold accent and warm paper only** (the colour values of its item 5); the rest of [DEC-204](#decisions) stands. `web/COLOR.md` holds the ramps, the tokens, the ultramarine rules and the contrast and colour-vision tables | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); the accent superseded by DEC-214 (volt) | The founder compared the accents side by side on the real screens and chose ultramarine on a cool white page. A cool accent keeps COLOR.md's first principle (no warm, saturated hue pushes the owner), leaving crimson the only warm, saturated colour on a screen, and a page at a neighbouring cool hue keeps the neutrals from reading as tinted against it. Tuning each step's chroma, rather than scaling the preview's gold chroma by 1.55, keeps the marks vivid without turning neon and the fields quiet. The colour-blind change follows from the accent: the tests require the accent's marks, gain, loss and crimson to stay apart for colour-blind owners, and a blue gain beside a blue accent did not | Keep gold (DEC-204; the founder chose ultramarine); iris, petrol, jade, plum or graphite (shown in the same preview, not chosen); keep the warm paper under ultramarine (the founder chose a cool page); take the preview's values unchanged (the scaled chroma ran the mid steps and the dark fields hotter than a quiet tint); keep the blue colour-blind gain (it merges with ultramarine text under deuteranopia, ΔE 0.04); loosen the colour-vision threshold instead of moving the gain (the tests hold the bar DEC-202 set) |
| DEC-206 | **The Stop control is quiet until something needs you** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by a Cursor agent; stream W4). (1) **Quiet by default.** On a calm screen Stop is an outlined pill: a 2 px ink border (paper in dark mode), an ink octagon and "Stop" in ink type, on the header's own colour. From 64rem it is 40 px tall, the height of the command bar ([DEC-208](#decisions)); below that the pill is 44 px. The hit area is 44 px tall at every width. It stays at the right of the header at every width from 320 px. (2) **Loud when there is a risk reason.** It becomes the filled ink pill (paper in dark mode), in the same box, so nothing moves, while `stopAttention` in `web/src/lib/attention.ts` gives a reason taken from workspace state and existing helpers only: an open alert as the Alerts screen lists it, except the owner's own pause or stop (a stale or failing feed, or an agent restricted by its mandate, the account or market data, which covers exits only and an agent halted by a gate); an agent in exits only; an agent near a loss limit; an unreachable deployment; an order in flight. *Near a loss limit* means the agent has used 80% or more of the loss the limit allows (the daily loss budget, a drawdown rung's depth below the high-water mark, or the capital the lifetime floor lets go), measured with the mandate panel's own headroom (`agentLimits`). No threshold existed in the code, `web/DESIGN.md` or the brief, so this decision sets it (`NEAR_LIMIT_USED` in `web/src/lib/limits.ts`). *In flight* means the broker has not confirmed the order: `Intent`, `Submitting`, `PendingCancel`, `PendingReplace` or `Unknown` (trading-domain spec §5.7). An order resting at the broker, partly filled or not, is confirmed; protective stops rest there all day. A pending approval alone never makes Stop loud. While the workspace loads, the last known tone holds, starting quiet. (3) **Unchanged.** Stop is never disabled, hidden or moved, and its accessible name is always "Stop". When loud, `aria-describedby` adds a short reason, such as "Needs attention: 1 alert, Agent 1 is near its daily loss limit". There is no badge, dot or count on the button. The swap is a colour change at `--duration-hover`, instant under reduced motion, with no pulse, glow or other attention animation (the No-Nudge rule), and both tones keep a visible border under forced colours. Nothing else in the header is filled in ink. Crimson stays on the kill-switch choices in the Stop sheet alone, and the Stop sheet is unchanged. **Amends [DEC-204](#decisions) item 2** ("the header keeps Stop as its dominant control") and nothing else. P1 in `web/PRODUCT.md` is unchanged and still met | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | A brake that is always the heaviest thing on the screen signals danger on a calm screen, and it competes with the one hero number DEC-204 gives each screen. P1 asks that Stop be visible on every screen, high contrast and never disabled, with a 44 px target on phones. It does not ask that Stop be the loudest element. A 2 px ink outline is far above 3:1 against the header in both themes, and its label keeps 7:1 (`STOP_CONTRAST`). Turning loud only for a real risk makes Stop louder exactly when it matters, so the change itself carries information. Counting approvals would make it loud on most screens and blur the difference between approving and stopping. | Always quiet (it would never draw the eye when something is wrong); icon only (a bare octagon is easy to misread); moving Stop into the phone tab bar or the sidebar (the header is the only bar on every screen at every width); keeping it dominant (the founder found it too big and prominent) |
| DEC-207 | **The phone is a remote control** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). Below the `lg` breakpoint (64rem) the web UI does four jobs: say whether anything needs the owner, approve or skip, stop, and give a glance at how they are doing. From `lg` up, tablet and desktop are unchanged; between 40rem and 64rem the phone's structure applies with the room used sensibly. (1) **Header.** Three things: the mark, which opens Home; the PAPER badge; and Stop. No menu and no search button, and the glass stays. No status strip: a banner under the header, at the strip's height and in its words, appears only when a feed is stale or failing or the workspace is unreachable. Record screens keep the full strip at every width, as a safety need. (2) **Tab bar.** Home, Approvals (with its count), Agents and More. More is a bottom sheet with the account and workspace switcher at the top, then Search (the command palette), Positions, Audit, Connections, Settings and every other screen the role may open. There is no sidebar sheet. The sheet stops below the header and is not modal, so Stop stays in view, pressable and in the accessibility tree while it is open. Targets are 44 px, and the bar keeps the safe-area inset. (3) **Home.** Needs you comes first: the requests, each a row to its record with the static time it is skipped at, then the alerts, or "All clear. Nothing needs you." with a check; a request appears once on Home. Then the account hero with a 180 px chart and its range picker wholly above the tab bar; one row per agent with its name, its state in a word and its headroom ("$274.45 above its daily loss limit"), with no P&L and no disclosure per row; the last three activity entries and "See all activity". No positions, and the footnotes are condensed. (4) **Agent.** The name, its state and Stop this agent at the top; any waiting request as a row; the equity hero with its disclosure and chart, the level legend behind a Levels button, collapsed; then Headroom, one row per limit with the room left, the limit and a thin ink meter ending at the mandate's deep ultramarine post, in no gain or loss colour. The route tabs give way to a plain list of the sections at the foot of every agent screen, with the counts of positions and orders. Key figures, positions, orders, decisions, activity and the mandate card are a section link away; desktop keeps its tabs and full overview. (5) **Approvals.** The inbox is one hairline row per request. A request is one screen, with Approve and Skip equal in size and weight and pinned on the tab bar to the end of the page. The request states the deadline as an absolute time and the whole minutes left, as the [brief](../product/09-product-experience.md) requires; the rows on Home and in the inbox give the time alone. The frozen content, the step-up and nothing optimistic are unchanged. (6) **What stays.** Every safety behaviour in [DEC-204](#decisions) item 7: Stop on every screen and never disabled, crimson only on the kill switch, the disclosure beside every remaining P&L, paper labelling, generic titles and opaque identifiers, role gates, frozen records and the Stop sheet's order. **Amends DEC-204** item 2's "a tab bar on phones", only to say which tabs | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); item 2's tabs amended by [DEC-479](decisions/DEC-479.md) item 6 | Squeezing the desktop onto a phone made it crowded: two navigation systems (the tab bar and a sidebar sheet), a status strip on every screen, and one request shown three times on Home. An owner reaches for a phone to glance, to approve or skip, and to stop, so those stay on the screen and everything else is one press away under More or in a section link. Headroom is a distance to a limit, not a result, so the agent rows and the Headroom list need no disclosure beside them; P&L on every row would repeat the disclosure on each. A banner that appears only when something is wrong keeps the header quiet on a good day and loud on a bad one. The layout switches by breakpoint classes, not script, so the desktop DOM only gains hidden elements | Keep the desktop structure squeezed (crowded, two navigation systems, the request three times); show P&L on the agent rows with the disclosure repeated on each (noise on the screen an owner glances at most); a five-tab bar (smaller targets at 320 px, and More is still needed); keep the sidebar sheet (a second navigation beside the tab bar) |
| DEC-208 | **The frame, the dock, the agent wire and the hero number** (the founder's decisions of 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-29, made from mockups and the founder's live preview of the built app, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). (1) **Glass on the frame only.** The sticky header and the phone tab bar are frosted glass: the page scrolls under them and shows through, blurred. The founder turned glass down anywhere else, so cards, panels, the More sheet, menus, popovers, sheets and dialogs stay flat. The two bars that take the frame's place on desktop, the agent wire under the header and the dock at the bottom, wear the same glass. The glass is the card colour at 72% (the least that keeps muted header text at AA over anything scrolling under it; the mockup's 58% did not), with a hairline in the type colour at 8%; the dock's is denser, the card at 85% with a 15% edge. It is a flat colour and a blur, so [DEC-200](#decisions) item 8 (flat colour, no gradients) still holds, and it falls back to the solid card where the browser cannot blur, under reduced transparency and in forced colours. (2) **A labelled floating dock replaces the desktop sidebar.** From 64rem the primary navigation is a glass dock floating above the bottom edge, centred: each item an icon over its name (Home, Approvals with its count, Alerts, Agents, Positions, Connections), then the Audit and More menus, so every screen a role may open is on the dock or one menu away. The current section sits on a pill in the type colour at 14%, with a filled icon and a semibold label, and stays current anywhere inside the section; the pill is never an ink fill, which is Stop's, nor ultramarine, which is the mandate's. There are no tooltips, since every item is named. The lockup moves into the header and the content takes the full width. (3) **The agent wire replaces the price ticker.** From 64rem, while every feed answers, the strip under the header is a wire of what the agents are doing: requests waiting for you first, then today's gate decisions and the owner's own pauses and stops, each item a link to where it is read in full. It is ink and muted text only and shows no amount won or lost, so it carries no performance disclosure. Its left end shows the oldest feed's age, and that opens the four feeds' freshness in a popover. It drifts slowly, holds still under the pointer or focus and stands still under reduced motion. A stale or failing feed, a loading, unreachable or empty workspace, a role that cannot see agents, or a frozen record screen falls back to the full status strip at the same height. Below 64rem there is no wire (see [DEC-207](#decisions)). (4) **A wide command bar with an honest prompt.** From 64rem the ⌘K trigger is a 40 px bar in the middle of the header, 380 to 460 px wide. It reads "Jump to an agent or screen…", because the palette jumps to screens and agents and searches nothing else. Stop's desktop pill takes the same 40 px height ([DEC-206](#decisions)). (5) **Folding breadcrumbs.** The current page's crumb never truncates. Where the trail is too narrow, the earlier crumbs fold into an "Earlier pages" menu before it, and in the narrowest header the trail hides. (6) **The hero number.** The equity figure over a hero chart (the account's on Home, an agent's on its page) has its own display size, scaled with the hero's width so a long value fits at 320 px, up to 4.75rem, in Public Sans ([DEC-209](#decisions)) at weight 600 and -0.03em. Its cents are half size, muted and raised to the digits' tops, and screen readers hear it once, whole. It is set in proportional lining figures, the one exception to tabular figures, because tabular digits leave visible gaps at that size. The change sits on its own line on a soft pill toned by its sign, with the word and the percentage, and the disclosure symbol ([DEC-210](#decisions)) beside it, so the figure's width changing as it scrubs moves nothing. The approval's action keeps the hero size. (7) **Section tabs mark one tab.** An agent's section tabs mark exactly one current tab: the exact match, else the deepest section the page sits under, with the underline under its label. **Amends [DEC-204](#decisions) item 2** (the quiet desktop sidebar becomes the dock, and the header gains the command bar and folding breadcrumbs); DEC-200 item 8 and every safety behaviour in DEC-204 item 7 stand | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-29); item 3 superseded by DEC-215 (no agent wire) | The founder chose the frame as the one place for depth: glass on the bars the page passes under says what floats, and flat everywhere else keeps the content calm. A sidebar took width from every screen for navigation an owner uses between screens, not on them; a dock gives the content the full width. Its first, icon-only version left people lost, so it gained labels and a pill on the current section. A price ticker made the product read like a brokerage and put prices, which the owner does not trade by hand, above everything. A wire of what the agents are doing answers the question an owner opens the app with, in the space the ticker took, and names no result, so it needs no disclosure. A search prompt must promise only what the palette does. A current crumb that truncates hides where you are. A larger, quieter figure makes the one hero number DEC-204 asks for read first; proportional figures keep it even at display size, and the pill's own line keeps scrubbing from moving the page. Two current tabs misstate where the owner is | Directions shown with the calm structure and not chosen: Graphite and Signal (black-and-white, no accent), Midnight (dark with an acid-green accent) and Soft (a periwinkle accent). Looks shown and not chosen: Terminal (amber monospace on black), Colour block (bold fills), Industrial (orange on grey with a monospace), Neon noir (violet on midnight, against the palette's no-purple rule) and Press (a serif figure face on pink paper). Surfaces: glass on the dots as well as the frame (the founder rejected glass beyond the frame), neo-brutalism (hard offset shadows), neumorphism (extruded shadows), tactile (textured controls). Frames: floating islands (the header and sidebar as inset glass islands; the dock took the sidebar's place instead) and a status island (the four feed ages collapsed into one pill in the header; the ages stay in the strip and the wire's popover). The price ticker (a brokerage's strip of prices, replaced by the wire); the wire itself was removed under [DEC-215](#decisions) |
| DEC-209 | **The type is Public Sans** (the founder's instruction of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). (1) **The face.** Public Sans (the U.S. Web Design System's grotesque, drawn from Libre Franklin), one variable family (weights 100 to 900) for every role in `web/`: headings, body, labels, figures and the charts' canvas text. It is self-hosted from `@fontsource-variable/public-sans` under the SIL OFL 1.1, which allows bundling and serving it with the app; the Latin file is about 26 KB and the Latin Extended file about 18 KB, and the app loads no font from a third-party host (DEC-200 item 5). (2) **Figures.** Every figure is set with tabular, lining digits (`tnum`; lining is the default): a flagged 1, a straight 7, and a plain oval zero, narrower than the capital O, with no slash or dot; the true minus sign (U+2212) is in the font. Its tabular feature sets the digits only, so commas, points and signs keep their own widths. The one exception is the display hero figure, which is proportional ([DEC-208](#decisions) item 6). (3) **The hero** stays at weight 600, tracked -0.03em (it was -0.035em), where the figures of `$28,478.36` sit close without touching; no type role is tracked tighter than -0.04em, which `web/src/lib/tokens.test.ts` holds. (4) **No figure face.** The hero number is not set in a serif to echo the P052 wordmark; the wordmark stays the one serif in the product. (5) **What stays.** The type scale, the weights (400, 500 and 600, never bolder), sentence case, the 58ch reading measure, and the wordmark as P052 outlines with no font file ([DEC-203](#decisions)). **Supersedes** Mona Sans in [DEC-204](#decisions) item 3, and nothing else in DEC-204 | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | The founder called Inter "an AI smell tell" and asked for the face to be chosen with Impeccable, whose reflex list rejects the defaults (Inter, Geist, Mona Sans, faces that imitate San Francisco, and the fashionable geometrics) and whose product guidance asks for one family unless a second has a role only it can perform. Public Sans comes from the Franklin Gothic line, the grotesque of newspapers and financial pages, so it reads serious and plain rather than like a neutral product default. Rendered in the built app (Home, an agent, the audit timeline, the positions table and a phone), it stayed open and even at 13 to 14 px in dense price tables and steady at the hero size, where the other candidates were either too narrow small or too loud large. Its figures meet every requirement (tabular and lining, distinct 1, 7 and 0, a minus sign), it covers Latin Extended-A (except Ĳ, ŉ and ſ) and Vietnamese, and the whole Playwright suite passed in both themes with no layout change. A serif hero was tried (Literata, Brygada 1918, Alegreya, with raised cents beside the wordmark) and turned down: it splits money across two faces, because the key figures, rows and mandate rails stay sans; it moves toward the warm-paper-and-serif look the product brief lists as an AI cliché; and it competes with the wordmark. | Archivo (narrow and small at 13 to 14 px, loud at the hero size); Golos Text (at UI sizes hard to tell from Inter); Chivo (heavy at 400); Red Hat Text (carries Red Hat's brand); Schibsted Grotesk and Zalando Sans (tabular punctuation and spaces loosen money); Libre Franklin, Albert Sans, Wix Madefor and Commissioner (no tabular figures in the files the packages ship); Hanken Grotesk, Host Grotesk and Rethink Sans (tabular figures only, and generic); Figtree, Onest, Funnel Sans and Reddit Sans (friendly or tied to a brand); Sofia Sans (thin and narrow at 13 px); Instrument Sans, Mona Sans, Inter and Geist (on Impeccable's reflex list); Epilogue and Geologica (display quirks); Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (a slashed zero, turned down under DEC-204); a serif figure face in Literata, Brygada 1918 or Alegreya (see the rationale); P052 as a web font (DEC-203 keeps the font file out of the product) |
| DEC-210 | **The performance disclosure sits behind an info symbol beside each P&L** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor coordinating session; stream W4). (1) **What changes.** Beside every P&L in `web/`, where the dashed `[[DISCLOSURE-PERFORMANCE]]` tag stood, the UI shows an info symbol (muted, 16 px, in a 24 px target that is 44 px on touch) named "Performance disclosure". It opens the disclosure text in a popover on hover, on a click or tap, and on keyboard focus with Enter or Space; Escape closes it. The text is also the symbol's accessible description, so a screen reader hears it without opening anything. (2) **What stays.** The placement still puts the disclosure beside every P&L, in the same row and on the same line as the figure, and the text is one hover or tap away; `web/src/app/disclosure.test.tsx` still fails any route where a P&L has no disclosure in its region. The text is still only the named placeholder (brief §5, rule 9), and `[[LEGEND-HYPOTHETICAL]]` and `[[RETAIL-AUTO-LIVE]]` stay visible tags. (3) **Where it stays inline.** Where nothing can be hovered or opened, the full text shows inline: in print, and on every record screen, because a record screen counts collapsed content as not shown (brief §4.1, PX-1) and its artifact is built from what it renders. No record screen, export or share image shows a P&L today; one that does gets the inline text. (4) **Counsel.** How a disclosure is presented is compliance text, reserved to the founder under [DEC-79](#decisions): **counsel must confirm that a disclosure one hover or tap away meets the "clear and prominent" standard before launch.** If counsel requires it visible, reverting is one line: `PERFORMANCE_INLINE` in `web/src/components/domain/placeholders.tsx`. | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); counsel must confirm before launch | The tag beside every P&L cluttered every screen (five times on Home alone), and the founder asked: "can you put it behind a symbol, opens on click and hover?" A symbol in the tag's place keeps the disclosure attached to each figure, where the reader is looking, instead of moving it away from the numbers. Keeping the text in the page as the symbol's description keeps it available to screen readers and to the checks that find it, and print and record screens keep it inline because nothing opens there. Because counsel has not yet judged prominence, the change is built to be undone in one line | Keep the inline tag beside every P&L (the clutter the founder asked to remove); one footnote per screen (fewer marks, but farther from each figure, and a reader of one row may never reach it); drop the per-row P&L (done on phones separately under DEC-207, and the P&L that remain still need their disclosure) |
| DEC-211 | **Sign-in is Supabase Auth: Google first, passkeys after** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor web session; stream W4). (1) **What it decides.** `web/` signs people in with Supabase Auth (`@supabase/ssr` and `@supabase/supabase-js`, pinned exactly, MIT, `docs/dependencies.md`). Sign-up and the first sign-in are Google over OIDC. After that a passkey signs in: it is offered once a sign-in finds none on the account (`/auth/passkey`, which can be skipped) and is listed, added, renamed and deleted under Settings > Profile. An email magic link is built and hidden behind `NEXT_PUBLIC_OWLHEAD_EMAIL_SIGNIN=1` until there is SMTP; it never creates an account. No message says whether an account or an address exists: a sign-in that did not finish has one sentence whatever the cause, and every email request is answered "If that address can sign in, we've sent a link" (brief O1). (2) **Where it applies.** Sign-in is on only when `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` are both set and the e2e build flag is off (`authEnabled`, `web/src/lib/auth-config.ts`); otherwise the app is exactly as before, with no gate and no call to Supabase. When it is on, `web/src/proxy.ts` refreshes the session on every request and a pure function decides (`authRoute`, `web/src/lib/auth-routes.ts`): a signed-out visitor at `/` sees the welcome page at the same address, a signed-out visitor anywhere else goes to `/login?next=` and the path, a signed-in visitor at `/login` goes on to `next`, and `next` is only ever a same-origin path, so it cannot redirect off the site. Who is signed in comes from `getClaims()`, which verifies the token, never from `getSession()`, and the session lives in cookies, not in browser storage. The public pages (`/welcome`, `/login`, `/auth/*`) have their own frame, the `(site)` route group, with the app's header but no Stop, dock, wire or paper badge; every screen of the app moved into the `(app)` group with no change of address. (3) **Risks.** Supabase passkeys are a beta API, so both packages are pinned and the client opts in. A passkey is bound to its relying-party ID, `localhost` now and `owlhead.ai` at launch; changing it invalidates every passkey made under the old one, so people add theirs again after launch, with Google as the way back in. The fixture roles (PX-11) are not yet tied to signed-in users: the role switch still decides what a screen shows. The step-up before Stop and approvals is still the mock; Supabase reauthentication or a fresh passkey assertion is future work. (4) **Backend handoff.** The Rust services verify the Supabase access token, a JWT, against the project's JWKS at `https://wyyxngmblqqokcvraflx.supabase.co/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`, with issuer `https://wyyxngmblqqokcvraflx.supabase.co/auth/v1` and audience `authenticated`; `sub` is the user's id. **Applies [DEC-18](#decisions)** (OIDC SSO in v1) to the web app's own sign-in | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29) | There is no SMTP yet, so an emailed code or link cannot be the main way in, and Google over OIDC needs no email of ours and is the OIDC DEC-18 already chose. O1 asks for a passkey or OIDC; a passkey after the first visit makes every later sign-in one touch, with no password to leak or reset. Supabase gives both, keeps the session in cookies the server can verify, and publishes keys the Rust services can check without calling it. Building email now and hiding it lets it switch on with SMTP and no new screens | Email and password (a password to store, leak and reset, against O1); an emailed code only (needs SMTP, which does not exist yet); an OIDC provider without Supabase (the app would own sessions, refresh and passkeys itself); Auth0 or Clerk (capable, but another vendor and price for what Supabase already gives the web app and the backend) |
| DEC-212 | **The landing page at owlhead.ai** (the founder's brief of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor landing-page session; stream W4). (1) **Where.** Signed-out visitors to `/` get `/welcome`, which renders `Landing` from `web/src/components/site/landing.tsx`, a server component with no props. The sign-in work owns the route, the site header (wordmark, "Sign in") and `/login`; the page starts below that header and brings its own `<main id="main">` and footer, so the layout around it adds no second `<main>`. (2) **Structure.** A hero: the headline "Let an AI agent trade inside limits you set.", one subhead, "Get started" to `/login`, "How it works" to its section, and the paper badge with "Paper trading only. Live trading isn't available." Then How it works in three steps, each beside a real screen (set the mandate; the agent trades inside it, through the gate; you stay in charge, approving or skipping and stopping); Why it's safe, a ledger of what the agent can't do and what you can always do, then five facts; Who it's for (people with their own brokerage account; businesses, marked "Coming later"); five questions; and a footer. (3) **The product is the hero.** The pictures are the app itself: `npm run site-shots` (`web/scripts/site-shots.mjs`) screenshots the production build at 2x in light and dark into `web/public/site/`, hiding every P&L figure, the hero's change pill, the key figures and the loss-today rail first. `next/image` serves them through a `<picture>` whose dark source follows `prefers-color-scheme`, with width and height set; the hero's screens load eagerly at high fetch priority (Next 16 deprecates `priority`). Frames are hairlines, with one hairline phone frame, and a caption says the screens show example data on a paper account. (4) **Copy rules.** Plain English, sentence case, short sentences and the product's nouns (mandate, gate, Stop, kill switch, journal). No performance, returns, testimonials or figures that read as results, and no "profit", "earn", "guaranteed" or "beat the market". Every claim comes from the docs, and what is not built says so: journal export and the chain check (FR-7.4, FR-7.5) and everything for businesses are marked "Coming later". Paper today, no live trading and no date for it (DEC-98, brief B5); no promise against loss (DEC-145). Compliance text appears only as named placeholders (DEC-79): `[[SITE-DISCLAIMER]]` in the footer, `[[NOT-INVESTMENT-ADVICE]]` as the whole answer to "Is this investment advice?", and `[[PRIVACY-POLICY]]` and `[[TERMS-OF-SERVICE]]` in place of legal pages, which nobody invents. (5) **The same design system.** The app's tokens, Public Sans, the serif wordmark, light and dark with the system, flat surfaces and no gradients (DEC-200 item 8). No cards: hairlines, whitespace and type. Ultramarine marks only the step numbers. One authored motion: from 64rem, with motion allowed, the hero's phone rises 12 px and fades in once. (6) **Indexing.** The landing page is the one indexed page: `landingMetadata` (`web/src/components/site/metadata.ts`) sets the title to "Owlhead" alone, a description, `robots: { index: true, follow: true }`, `/` as the canonical address (`/welcome` serves it there), and the existing share image for Open Graph and Twitter. The description is for search results only; the share card keeps the no-tagline rule (web/PRODUCT.md, amended for this). Every app page stays `noindex`. | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); items 2 to 5 superseded by DEC-213 (the retro text page and the private beta) | What the product does is the best case for it, so the page shows the screens an owner will use rather than describing them, and a trading product must not sell on returns. One design system means the page promises exactly what the owner sees after signing in. Keeping the page in `web/` shares the tokens, the fonts and the components, and ships with the app. | A separate marketing site or framework (a second design system and deploy that drift from the app, and its pictures would still be the app's); a waitlist-only page (says nothing about what the product does, and brief B5 rules out waitlist framing that implies a date); a template (gradients, bento cards, testimonials and metrics, all ruled out by the copy and design rules) |
| DEC-213 | **The landing page is one retro page of text, and it gathers interest in a private beta** (the founder's decisions of 2026-09-29, made on the live page, recorded by the Cursor landing-page session; stream W4). (1) **What changes.** DEC-212's screenshot page gives way to one page of text in the manner of a 1990s home page: a period browser window (title bar, menus and toolbar as `aria-hidden` scenery, the address, guide links, a status bar) on a dithered desktop, the name in ASCII art, and nine numbered sections beside a contents list: what we're building, why, how it works, how it stays in check, the record (a working tamper demo on example data), who it's for, where we are, questions, and ask for a place. The site header is hidden on the landing page (`OffLanding`), which draws its own window. `npm run site-shots`, `web/public/site/` and the `/site/` public path are removed. Its guestbook form posts an email and an optional use (own account, money for others, a desk, an agent of one's own) to `POST /api/beta`, which validates it, drops a filled honeypot silently, and stores it in the Supabase table `beta_requests` (`web/supabase/migrations/20260929180000_beta_requests.sql`; row level security lets the publishable key insert and never read), or in `web/.data/beta-requests.jsonl`, gitignored, until that table exists. A repeat address counts as stored, so the form never reveals who is on the list. (3) **What it may say.** At the founder's direction the page may describe what is not built yet, as long as it is listed as coming: "Working now, on paper" (writing rules and checking every order against them, paper trading on Alpaca, approvals and Stop, the record) and "Coming during the beta" (live trading once it has legal sign-off; Robinhood and Kraken Derivatives US; firm and team limits and self-hosting; the MCP server). No performance, returns, percentages, signed amounts, testimonials, "profit", "guaranteed" or "beat the market"; no promise against loss, and it says a loss can end up larger than a limit (DEC-145). (4) **Compliance wording.** At the founder's direction the landing page's named placeholders (`[[SITE-DISCLAIMER]]`, `[[NOT-INVESTMENT-ADVICE]]`, `[[PRIVACY-POLICY]]`, `[[TERMS-OF-SERVICE]]`) give way to plain words: the footer's "Owlhead is in private beta. It is software, not investment advice. Trading involves risk, and you can lose money." and a plain answer to "Is this investment advice?". This departs from DEC-79 for the landing page only; the product's placeholders stay. Counsel reviews both before launch and supplies a privacy notice for the form before the list is used. (5) **Design.** The app's tokens only, light and dark with no `dark:` classes, flat fills and no gradients (the dither and the construction tape are SVG patterns). Period faces for this page alone, self-hosted from `@fontsource` (DotGothic16, VT323, Pixelify Sans, all SIL OFL) and imported only by `landing.tsx`; the product stays in Public Sans (DEC-209). Two product rules are scoped out for this page, with the reason in the tests: the reserved warning tint marks the construction strip and the edited line of the record, since the page shows no gain or loss for amber to be mistaken for; and its blinking "New" beside the beta notice is not a recommendation badge. The blink is the only motion, and only with motion allowed. (6) **Indexing.** DEC-212 item 6 stands, with the description "A trading agent for your own brokerage account, working inside rules you write. Now in private beta, starting on paper." | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); supersedes DEC-212 items 2 to 5; amends DEC-79 for the landing page's disclaimer; counsel reviews the copy and the wording, and supplies a privacy notice for the form, before launch | The founder wanted one page that says what Owlhead is, why and how, and gathers interest, with a character of its own rather than another financial-product template. Text stays true as the app changes, where screenshots of a moving app go stale, and a list of requests lets places open a few at a time. | DEC-212's screenshot page (said little about why, and gathered no interest); a visual private-beta page with a request form (built first; the founder chose the text page instead); a third-party waitlist service (another vendor holding the addresses) |
| DEC-214 | **The accent is volt: Ink and Volt** (the founder's decision of 2026-09-29, recorded by the Cursor web session; stream W4). (1) **Values.** Light: volt-500 #7C9217 for lines, rails and marks; olive volt-700 #4C5A09 for volt as text and for the focus ring; the volt tint volt-100 #F2FCD7 for the mandate's field; volt-200 #D2F34A for selected text. Dark: volt-400 #A4C025 for lines and marks, volt-200 #D2F34A for volt text and the focus ring, volt-850 #2C350E for the field and volt-800 #3D4814 for selected text. A new token, `highlight`, is neon volt-300 #C8E928 in both themes and always carries ink type (`highlight-foreground`, 13.07:1): the one saturated volt fill, for a call to action or a hovered link. Paper, ink, crimson, gain and loss are unchanged. (3) **Contrast.** Every semantic pair and every Kumo role pair still meets WCAG 2.2 AA in both themes, and APCA in the development tests. (4) **Colour vision.** Every required check passes under simulated deuteranopia, protanopia and tritanopia; the colour-blind teal gain sits 85 degrees from volt. The colour-blind gain against volt text under tritanopia in light mode, and the colour-blind loss against volt text under tritanopia in dark mode, are information only. | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-29); supersedes DEC-205's accent; the rest of DEC-205 stands | The founder's choice, applied across the app and the landing page. (2) **What shows instead.** From 64rem the frame follows the phone's rule ([DEC-207](#decisions)): nothing under the header while every feed answers; the full status strip, at the same 34 px, while a feed is stale or failing or the workspace is loading or unreachable; and the full strip on every record screen at every width. With no strip, the "Fixture data" tag sits at the foot of the page, clear of the dock. Home keeps its own freshness line. | DEC-216 | **A livelier dashboard** (the founder's request of 2026-09-30, recorded by the Cursor web session; stream W4). (1) **Figures roll digit by digit.** A changed dollar figure rolls with Number Flow (`@number-flow/react`): only the digits that change move, up when the value rises and down when it falls, over 520ms on `--ease-out`, each digit clipped to its own glyph. Number Flow's edge mask is set to zero, so it is a hard clip and no fade shows (DEC-200 item 8). A scrubbed figure follows the pointer at once, and nothing rolls under reduced motion. Screen readers still hear one stable copy. (2) **The range pill and tab underline slide.** The current range sits on the neon highlight in highlight type and springs to the next; an agent's section tab underline slides to the next tab. (3) **The hero line.** A new range fades the line up from 20% over 240ms, and while every feed is current the hero line's last point pulses (`LastPriceAnimationMode.Continuous`); it stops when a feed goes stale. (4) **What does not change.** Approve, Skip and Stop never pulse or count (the No-Nudge rule); gains and losses are shown alike, with no celebratory motion. **Amends** the Flat chart rule ("no series animates") and the number-change motion in `web/DESIGN.md` | Accepted (founder, 2026-09-30) | The founder passed on feedback that the dashboard "looks dull and not as good looking and fun as robinhood" and asked for this motion. | Motion the No-Nudge and no-gamification rules exclude: confetti, P&L-coloured lines, pulsing approvals or Stop |
| DEC-177 | **Journal spec v0.6 §9.1 (drafted as v0.5) closes eight agent-stream payload schemas, and the test vectors gain a generated `agent_stream` section** (DEC-174 item 4, first half; stream L, claim [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171); id assigned by the backend coordinator's dispatch). (1) **Scope.** The agent stream's `StreamOpened`, `ObservationRecorded`, `ModelOutputRecorded`, `DecisionMade`, `IntentProposed`, `AgentModeChanged`, the agent stream's `KillSwitchActivated`, and `OwnerExitRequested`. The approval events (`ApprovalRequested`, `ApprovalDelivered`, `ApprovalResponded`, `ApprovalTimedOut`, `ApprovalCanceled`) close together with M7's spec change (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)), which extends their rows, so this change edits neither those rows nor §2. `ModelInvocationRecorded`, `ThesisProposed`, and `ThesisRevised` close with their own stories. (2) **DEC-174 item 3, the spec's side each time.** Empty values are `null` (§4.2): `DecisionMade.reason_code` on an allow, and `OwnerExitRequested`'s `bid`, `bid_size`, `floor`, `user`, and `step_up` when nothing was confirmed. Every instant is a §4.7 timestamp, never risk-clock seconds: `ObservationRecorded.as_of`, `ModelOutputRecorded.as_of` and `expires_at`, and `step_up.authenticated_at`. Observed data and model content are artifact references (`data_ref`, `content_hash`), never inline. `IntentProposed` carries exactly the `IntentReceived` vector's intent fields less `intent_id` (its own `event_id`, §2) and `agent_id` (the stream's): `instrument_id`, `side`, `type`, `tif`, `qty`, `limit_price`, and `purpose`. The key fields §9 lists and the runtime omits become members: `DecisionMade`'s outputs used, model weights, clips applied, and exit and buy convictions; `ModelOutputRecorded`'s content hash, direction, conviction, confidence, horizon, and `ignored` reason. `step_up` is the structured evidence §9 already names for approvals: assertion ID, authentication time, and method. (3) **The runtime's extra members, each kept because another rule needs it.** `AgentModeChanged.lifecycle`: restrictions lift independently (mandate spec §5.9), so an owner's pause taken under a stricter restriction must replay from the agent stream's own events (§1 principle 4). `KillSwitchActivated.subject`: "touches only its scope" (`AGENTS.md` rule 13, trading spec §5.5) is checkable only if the event names the subject, which rule 15 ties to the stream. `KillSwitchActivated.mode_event`: the executor's `AgentModeApplied` copy names it as `causation_id` (§2), which is how the agent stream's fold confirms the switch from its own events. `OwnerExitRequested.bid_size` and `floor`: trading spec §5.5 and mandate spec §6.1 require the confirmed bid size and a floor below which the exit price ladder never prices. (4) **Rules.** Consistency rules 1 to 13 and subject rules 14 and 15, each derived from a rule already stated: long-only purposes; limit orders for opening and increasing (`AGENTS.md` rule 12, trading spec §5.1); §6.2's dry run before classification and built-in AUTO; `defer` for discretionary exits only (trading spec §9.1); §4.1's ordering; mandate spec §5.9's mode order; trading spec §5.5's owner confirmation; and the research agent's thesis and lineage. Each has an invalid draft in the vectors. They only refuse drafts, so none adds risk. (5) **Vectors.** `reference/journal/generate.py` generates the section and checks it. It first reproduces every version-3 vector, then validates the chain events and drafts against its own mirror of the schemas and rules, recomputes `DecisionMade` from the model output, weights, and observed quote (mandate spec §8.3), checks `IntentProposed` against the `IntentReceived` vector, and requires every seeded validator and vector bug to be caught. After round 1 of the review (items 8 to 15) that is 13 chain events, 47 invalid and 5 valid drafts, 4 batches, 2 range-verification cases, 31 validator and verifier mutants, and 21 vector mutants, each vector mutant re-chained so that only the check it names can catch it. A pytest runs it with `--check` in CI, and `mandate journal verify` verifies the exported chain with its artifacts. (6) **The vectors stay version 3**, against the dispatch's "version 4". `journal::version` is a passing harness case that pins 3, the spec guard (ES-22) keeps the harness change out of a spec PR, and the section is additive, so every existing vector keeps its bytes. The bump follows E7-9's tests PR, which makes the harness read `agent_stream`: that code PR, then a one-line spec change. (7) **Not ruled:** what happens to a model output outside §8.2's ranges (conviction in [−1, 1], confidence in [0, 1]); the schema records it as given. The runtime changes these rulings imply are other streams' work, recorded in the backlog. **Round 1 of the independent review** (the coordinator's rulings; each a tightening, accepted under DEC-176): (8) **M2, the Status line names v0.5 first**, as the mandate and trading specs name their current version; M7's spec change (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)) also calls itself v0.5, and whichever merges second renumbers to v0.6. (9) **M3, `AgentModeChanged.lifecycle` has its own three-value type** (`normal`, `paused`, `stopped`), as §9.1's table already said; the generator's mirror had typed it as the four-value mode and accepted `exits_only`. The invalid draft `mode_lifecycle_exits_only` expects `non_canonical` at `payload.lifecycle`, and the mutant `regress.lifecycle_as_mode`, which restores the wider type, is caught only by that draft. (10) **M4, the absent-member rule has vectors and a mutant.** A change in an invalid draft may now be `{path, delete: true}`, which removes the member. `owner_exit_floor_absent` and `decision_reason_code_absent_on_allow` each expect `schema` at the deleted member, and the mutant `record.missing` models a validator that reads an absent member as `null`. It escaped before these drafts and is caught by the second: the first alone cannot catch it, because rule 12 refuses a `null` floor on a confirmed exit with the same reason and path. (11) **B1, `OwnerExitRequested` records who asked and what step-up they gave on every owner exit.** Rule 12 had tied `user` and `step_up` to `confirmed`, but a bid is confirmed only where trading spec §5.5 needs one (an equity sold outside the regular session), so an in-session or crypto close and every owner kill switch would have journaled no owner and no step-up, against mandate spec §5.10 and §6.1 and §9. Now `confirmed` governs only `bid`, `bid_size`, and `floor`; `user` is `text`, never `null`; the new required member `step_up_status` (`valid`, `absent`, `stale`) says which case of DEC-158 option (c) applied; and `step_up` is non-null exactly when `step_up_status` is `valid` (rule 12's second clause). No rule requires a member only valid step-up or a confirmed bid supplies, so no step-up state can hold a reduction (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). The chain now holds an in-session close of a held instrument with valid step-up and no bid, an after-hours close with a confirmed bid whose own limit is the floor, and an owner kill switch with `step_up_status: absent`, with their `IntentProposed` where one is proposed; it gains six invalid drafts and a `valid_drafts` list of three cases that must be accepted, and the mutant `regress.rule_12_ties_evidence_to_the_bid`, which restores the reviewed rule, is caught. The wording of the `OwnerExitRequested` paragraph is flagged for the founder's after-the-fact look, since it describes what DEC-158 option (c) unlocks. (12) **M5, `DecisionMade` gains `ask_suppressed`** (`budget`, `skipped_today`, `recent_timeout`, or `null`), with the name, values, and condition of M7's spec change (claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213), mandate spec §6.4 there): why an `ask` was classified but not asked, so closing `DecisionMade` does not block DEC-156 item 5. Rule 7's second clause makes it non-null only when `autonomy` is `ask`, which rule 7 already confines to openings and increases, so a suppression never skips an exit; that no `ApprovalRequested` names a suppressed decision is checked with the approval events. Two invalid drafts (a non-`ask` decision carrying it, and an unknown reason) and a valid draft (a suppressed `ask` on an opening). (13) **M6, only a §8.3 evaluation's decisions carry its numbers.** Rule 8 had required `exit_conviction`, `buy_conviction`, and `combined_score` on every purpose but `risk_exit`, so a discretionary exit from a goal's completion or a removed instrument, which no §8.3 evaluation produced, could not be journaled and would have been held. `DecisionMade` gains `exit_origin` (`signal`, `goal_completion`, `removed_instrument`, the three origins of mandate spec §6.1), non-null exactly on a `discretionary_exit`. The numbers are required exactly when the purpose is `open` or `increase` or the origin is `signal`; otherwise they are `null` and `outputs_used`, `model_weights`, and `clips_applied` are empty, since §8.3 step 5 names those among the evaluation's results. Every exit's writer holds what the rule needs, so it never holds an exit. The chain gains a `profit_stop` goal completion after the session, deferred (`session_not_allowed`) with nothing proposed and checked against mandate spec §3.1 and §6.2 and trading spec §9.6 by its own oracle; six invalid drafts, a valid removed-instrument exit without numbers, the mutant `regress.rule_8_numbers_on_every_exit_but_risk`, and four vector mutants. (14) **M7, the cross-event facts are checked where they ship.** Rule 10's second clause makes `append` refuse a batch in which an `IntentProposed`'s action members differ from those of the `DecisionMade` it names, decimals compared by value, at the first differing member. §11 gains two per-range checks on an agent stream, `intent_action_mismatch` and `mode_event_mismatch` (a non-null `mode_event` names an earlier `AgentModeChanged` with reason `kill_switch`), so `mandate journal verify` catches both across batches; a reference before a range's trusted start is left to the full-chain run. The vectors gain `valid_batches` (one), `invalid_batches` (three), and `range_verification` (two cases, each a re-chained tampered chain failing one range check at one seq), with the mutants `rule.10.batch`, `verify.intent_action_mismatch`, and `verify.mode_event_mismatch`. The Rust side (`mandate-journal`'s batch check and the verifier's two codes) is a backlog row: the spec guard keeps code out of this change. (15) **The round's minors and nits are backlog rows** under the freeze rule: a copied `AgentModeChanged` naming its `AgentModeApplied`; bounds and a personal-data scan for the model-supplied free text; `KillSwitchActivated` recording the initiator's step-up; the `journal.yaml` header's founder-approval line and DEC-176; `from __future__ import annotations` and ruff for `reference/`; and the stale version in `mandate-refcases`' journal module doc. (16) **Round 2's M1, the deferred goal exit records the defer code.** Item 13's goal-completion exit recorded `session_not_allowed`, the gate's deny code for an equity opening outside the session, where trading spec §9.6 defers a discretionary exit with `discretionary_exit_regular_session_only` (registered `# defer` in the trading-domain reference cases and asserted there at 17:00 ET). The vector now records that code, and `check_goal_exit` reads it from the trading-domain cases' deferred discretionary exits rather than from a constant. The exit is also a limit order, as trading spec §9.4 requires outside the session, priced at the exit ladder's first rung (reference bid 151 × (1 − 0.005), rounded up to the tick, 150.25; §5.6, §2.1), which the oracle recomputes with exact fractions; no other vector changes outcome. Three vector mutants (the deny code, a market order, the limit rounded down) are each caught by that oracle alone. No rule text changes. (17) **Round 2's M2, each clause of `mode_event_mismatch` has its own vector and mutant.** §11's check has three clauses: a non-null `mode_event` names an `AgentModeChanged`, that event's reason is `kill_switch`, and on a full-chain run it exists. Round 1 had one range case, for the first. `range_verification` gains `kill_switch_names_an_owner_pause` (the named `AgentModeChanged` is the owner's pause) and `kill_switch_names_no_event_on_the_full_chain` (`from_seq: 1`, an ID no event has), and a range case may now change more than one member. The generator checks each clause separately, so the mutant `verify.mode_event_mismatch` becomes `.event_type`, `.reason`, and `.unresolved`, each caught only by its own case; without the two new cases the last two escape. Each tampered chain is re-chained and must still hold only valid drafts, so a range case can fail only on its range check. No rule text changes. (18) **Round 2's M3, rule 11's boundary is pinned by accepted drafts.** No accepted draft had `lifecycle` `paused` or `stopped`, or `to` equal to `lifecycle`, so a validator reading rule 11 as strictly stricter would have refused the owner's pause, resume, and Stop, and refusing to journal a pause or a Stop would hold a risk reduction (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). `valid_drafts` gains `mode_owner_pause` (`to: paused`, `lifecycle: paused`), `mode_owner_resume` (`from: paused`, `to: normal`, `lifecycle: normal`), and `mode_owner_stop` (`to: stopped`, `lifecycle: stopped`), each with its owner reason, on the `AgentModeChanged` base. The mutant `boundary.rule_11_strictly_stricter` (`>` for `>=`) is caught by those three drafts and escapes without them. No rule text changes. (19) **Round 2's minors are backlog rows under the freeze rule**, each naming "#320 round 2": both directions of the biconditionals in rules 1, 3, 4, 5, and 13, with per-direction mutants; vectors for `artifact_refs` exactness, `pii_refs` order, and `actor.build`; drafts that assert the report order by breaking two rules; `step_up_status` in mandate spec §5.10's `OwnerExitRequested` row; and §9.1's removed-instrument citation. (20) **How #320 and #321 reconcile, binding on whichever merges second.** (a) It resolves the Status line and the v0.5 change-history bullet and renumbers itself to v0.6, in the §9.1 heading, the `journal.yaml` header, and `generate.py`'s `spec` string, then regenerates with `reference/journal/generate.py --write` and `cargo xtask refcases --write`. (b) It adds `ApprovalRevalidated` as a third allowed cause of `IntentProposed` in §9.1, and records whether `intent_action_mismatch` compares an approved intent against the approval's bound fields. (c) #321's §2 copy rule (`causation_id` pointing to the owner command) needs a §9.1 causation rule, and the vectors regenerated to meet it: chain seqs 6, 9, and 12 (`OwnerExitRequested`) and 13 (`KillSwitchActivated`) have `causation_id: null`. (d) `ask_suppressed` keeps one definition, and #320's hedge "once M7's change lands" goes. (e) §9.1's citations of mandate spec §6.1 are re-checked against the merged text. The backlog row carries the same list. (21) **Item 20 (a): #320 merged second, so its change is journal spec v0.6.** The founder merged #321 (`0a9632f`) and then #320 (`70a3f7f`) by hand, before this reconciliation. The Status line now names v0.5 as approval escalation v0 (DEC-173, amended by DEC-181) and v0.6 as §9.1's closed schemas; the DEC-177 bullet moves above the v0.5 bullets as v0.6; the `journal.yaml` header and `generate.py`'s `spec` string say v0.6, and the vectors stay version 3. DEC-181's "v0.5, amended" bullet (#334) does not contradict §9.1: it names the autonomy source, `delegation_id`, and `requested_by` for `DecisionMade` and says they join §9.1's closed schema, with vectors, in their own change before any writer emits them. §9's `DecisionMade` row lists them, so §9.1 now says in one sentence that they are not members until that change and a draft carrying one is refused as an unlisted member, which is what §9.1 already did. (22) **Item 20 (d) and (e): §9.1 no longer points to #321 as future work, and cites mandate spec §6.1 for what it states.** `ask_suppressed` keeps one definition, mandate spec §6.4's ("Asking is bounded"), which §9.1's row now cites instead of restating the precedence "once M7's change lands"; the two agreed. Rule 7's note, §9.1's list of events not closed, and the v0.6 bullet now say the approval events v0.5 added close in their own change, rather than with M7's. §6.1 now holds #321's owner-controls table, so `OwnerExitRequested`'s "a confirmed bid without valid step-up unlocks nothing" cites it by name, and `exit_origin`'s removed instrument cites §2.3 and §8.6, as §6.1's purpose table and §5.9 do, instead of §2.2 and §2.3 (#320 round 2's citation row). Text only: no rule, member, or vector changes. (23) **Item 20 (b): an approved intent is caused by its `ApprovalRevalidated` and repeats the approval's bound action.** §9's `IntentProposed` row (#321) says that after a grant `causation_id` is the `ApprovalRevalidated`, and mandate spec §6.4 has it follow `ApprovalRevalidated` with result `act` in the same batch. §9.1 now names it as the third allowed cause, beside the `DecisionMade` and an owner's `OwnerExitRequested`. The reading is the conservative one: since re-validation "never re-prices, re-sizes, or changes the order" (§6.4), `intent_action_mismatch` also compares an intent caused by an `ApprovalRevalidated` against the action members bound in its approval's `ApprovalRequested` content object, exactly, and §11 says so. That clause needs chain events for approvals, whose schemas §9.1 does not close yet, so it is checked, with a range case and a mutant, in the change that closes them (a backlog row), and until then no range fails on it. A risk-adding intent that the clause would refuse is one that re-validation changed, which §6.4 forbids, so the check can only stop an approval being acted on in a changed form, never an exit. (24) **Item 20 (c): each copy of an owner command names its `OwnerCommandIssued` (§9.1 rule 16).** §2 (#321) has the agent runtime copy each owner command from the control stream at most once, "with `causation_id` pointing to it", and the chain's `OwnerExitRequested` events (seqs 6, 9, 12) and owner `KillSwitchActivated` (seq 13) broke it with `causation_id: null`. Rule 16 (reason `schema`, path `causation_id`, reported after the subject rules) requires a non-null cause on every `OwnerExitRequested`, on a `KillSwitchActivated` whose `initiator` is `owner`, and on an `AgentModeChanged` with reason `owner_pause`, `owner_resume`, or `owner_stop`. The cause is a control-stream event ID, which §2 allows (as for `OwnerAcknowledged`) and which neither `append` nor §11's per-range checks resolve, since both read one stream, so the rule checks presence only and the cross-stream check is a backlog row. "At most once" is per event type on one agent stream: an owner's kill switch yields its `OwnerExitRequested` and its `KillSwitchActivated`, both naming the one command (seqs 12 and 13). The chain now names control-stream commands listed in the derivation; three invalid drafts drop the cause (an owner close, the owner's switch, an owner pause), a valid draft shows a `risk_limit` switch without a cause is accepted, and the generator's `check_owner_copies` checks each copy's cause, command stream, kind, subject, and submission time and the at-most-once reading; each of those six clauses has a vector mutant registered against it that no other clause of the check catches (item 25). Rule 16 tightens what `append` accepts and never holds a reduction, because the copy is written from its command, and an automated kill switch or an account-stream mode change is not a copy (`AGENTS.md` rule 13); `mandate-journal` does not enforce §9.1 yet and the runtime's payloads do not meet it (the Stream I row), so nothing written today is newly refused. (25) **Round 1 of #340's review: rule 16's clauses each have their own seeded bug, and #339's members are kept.** (a) Main merged DEC-252 (#339) first, which closes `decided_by`, `delegation_id`, `requested_by`, and `client_id` into §9.1's `DecisionMade` and extends rules 5 and 7. The merge keeps those members and clauses and supersedes item 21's sentence that DEC-181's members are not members here, which §9.1 no longer says; the Status line and the v0.5-amended bullet name DEC-252, and the v0.6 bullet stays above them. Rule 16 still reports last: it governs `OwnerExitRequested`, `KillSwitchActivated`, and `AgentModeChanged`, which no clause of rules 5 or 7 reads, so no draft can break both. (b) Planted alone, four bugs had left every mutant reported caught: the at-most-once clause keyed per event rather than per command, that clause deleted, the subject clause disabled, and `owner_resume` dropped from the owner reasons. The at-most-once and subject clauses masked each other, and the duplicate kill switch was caught only by an unrelated range case. Now `rule.16.mode` is one skip key per owner reason, with an invalid draft each for `owner_pause`, `owner_resume`, and `owner_stop`. Every oracle failure names its check (`ORACLE_CHECKS`), each vector mutant is registered against one check, and `run_mutants` counts it caught only when that check catches it and no other check of its family does. The copy mutants are isolated: a command of the same kind for another subject, a command of another kind for the same subject, and a second `OwnerExitRequested` from the owner's kill switch command that matches it in stream, subject, and time. Each of the four planted bugs is now caught, each by its own case. Registering existing mutants moved three, re-seeded so one check catches each: "prev_hash not chained" fixes the body's own bytes so only the link breaks, "kill switch flatten proposed as an intent" appends the flatten intent instead of moving an owner close's, and the `IntentReceived` comparison is its own family (`received.intent`), which "intent differs from its decision" also breaks. The two mutants that mixed the kind, subject, and at-most-once clauses give way to the isolated ones. (c) Minors from the same round are backlog rows under the freeze rule | Accepted (agent, DEC-176) | DEC-174 found every agent-stream draft refused as `unknown_schema`, with no closed field list and no vector to register a schema from. Each ruling takes the spec's side of a contradiction, and each extra member stays only where another rule depends on it, so the rules tighten what `append` accepts and change nothing the runtime may do (DEC-176 (1) and (2)). Keeping version 3 is the one reading that lands the vectors with CI green and without mixing code into a spec change | Take the runtime's payloads as the schema (the journal would accept the contradictions, and correcting them later would need upcasters, §8); drop the extra members (the fold could not replay an owner's pause, confirm a kill switch, or keep an owner exit's floor from its own events); bump to version 4 here (fails the passing `journal::version` case, and the harness change cannot ship with a spec change, ES-22); close the approval events now (conflicts with M7's spec change, which extends them) |
| DEC-250 | **How the shared harness runs family B (the thirty-two `kind: builder` cases) through `mandate_builder::propose`, `mandate_risk::evaluate` and `mandate_builder::decide`** (E6-2's family-B harness, DEC-77 stage 4, DEC-130, DEC-162 item 8; stream H, claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125)). The arm is `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/order_builder.rs`, its own module as `autonomy.rs` is. (1) **Composition, as the case-file header states it:** under `trim_to_target` the risk engine's §5.5 trim first (§6.2 step 1), then `propose`, then the real gate on the order `propose` returned as §6.2 step 2's dry run, then `decide` on that verdict. The case's `gate_dry_run` is compared with the gate's answer and is never an input, so a builder that proposes the wrong order meets the gate's verdict on the wrong order (DEC-130 item 2). (2) **The mandate is validated** against `validation_context_defaults` and projected three ways: `BuilderMandate` (DEC-130 item 5), the document's own `autonomy` block, and the gate's `RiskLimits`. Every B base validates, and the gate is handed a validated mandate by construction, so validating is the tighter choice; DEC-162 item 1's reason for not validating family A (MC-A10's V-024) does not arise. (3) **One clock:** the session and the close window are `mandate_risk::session_at` at the case's `now`, for the builder and the gate alike (DEC-129 item 9). The case's `session` and `in_close_window` are claims about that clock, compared after everything else, so a label that contradicts `now` fails the case naming both. (4) **The gate's other inputs are DEC-178's "every other check passes" environment** (family G's, merged in [#317](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/317)). #317 left `builder` failing as pending on E6-2 in `mandate.rs`; with this arm every kind the fixture holds is interpreted, so the pending-kind table is deleted rather than kept empty (an empty lookup is code no case can drive), and `a_family_another_stream_owns_fails_with_its_story` becomes `a_kind_no_arm_interprets_fails_naming_it`, which requires every kind to reach an arm and a kind none reads to fail naming it. The environment: a healthy risk state at the case's equity and size factor; a margin account at `account_equity_usd`; the case's positions, with other held instruments at one unit at their stated market value; the gate state's working orders as openings with ids 1 to n; a listing on an eligible exchange (none for crypto), tradable, unhalted, liquid on both median dollar volumes, last closed at the bid, fractionable exactly when `qty_increment` is below one, and with `qty_increment` as its minimum order size; the case's quote at `now`, no last trade; empty conduct state; the working universe from `gate_state`. (5) **One exposure:** `gate_state.agent_equity` must equal `agent_equity`; the instrument's `positions_mv` entry must equal `position_qty` × bid, and be absent only when flat; the builder's working cost is the gate state's working openings in the instrument and its gross exposure the gate state's positions plus all working openings. A case may restate `gross_usd` or `working_opening_orders` only as those same figures. (6) **Defaults are the fixture's, never the harness's own:** the case-file header's four (`session` regular, `in_close_window` false, fee rates 0, `has_prior_fill` = position above zero) and `reference/mandate/ref.py`'s (`size_factor` 1; 0 for the drawdown, both P&L fractions, the day's buys, the cost basis and the goal spend). DEC-130 item 19 makes these inputs required in the crate, and the harness supplies the case file's stated value. Every base pins its universe, so `new_instrument` is false and `thesis_confidence` 0 (§6.3); a base that does not pin is refused. An equity fee rate above zero is refused, since the fixture states no equity fee schedule to reserve from, and the gate's `fee_reservation` is 0. (7) **Absent expectations:** `target_value` and `delta` absent mean the builder computed none; `clipped_by` absent means no clip on a hold, is required on a buy and refused on a sell; `order_type` absent means a plain limit; a hold states no `gate_dry_run` and no `autonomy`, and `decide` must refuse it with `nothing_proposed`. `reason`, an order field, `origin` and `trim_withheld` are refused where the action cannot have them. (8) **Pacing:** an allowed opening carries none; an allowed exit's pacing, if any, keeps the proposal's quantity and price, and its `marketable_limit_required` must equal the builder's `order_type`. (9) **A gate `hold` verdict is refused**, since §6.2 step 2 gives `decide` no hold to take. (10) **`skipped` and `deferred` are `Outcome` variants** (DEC-130 item 14), stated with `by: gate_dry_run`; a classified outcome is compared as family A compares one. (11) **The trim cases (MC-B17, MC-B30 to MC-B32) fail at `mandate_risk::trim_proposals` (pending E6-4)**, reached with the scale_sizes rung whose factor is the case's size factor active for `scale_active_s`, and `holding` as `GoalState::Holding`; the trim guards are refused on any other mandate. The three crypto increases (MC-B26 to MC-B28) fail at `mandate_risk::evaluate`'s owed check 2 (pending E6-10), as DEC-162 item 8 said. (12) **The three contradicting clocks are a fixture fix, accepted under DEC-176, in its own reference-case PR.** MC-B22 states `session: after_hours` and MC-B23 `in_close_window: true`, but both put `now` at 2026-09-22T14:00Z, the regular session; MC-B31 has the same `after_hours` label. Under item 3 they fail naming the conflict. The fix moves `now`, and each output's `as_of` and `expires_at` with it, to 21:00Z for MC-B22, 19:55Z for MC-B23, and an after-hours instant for MC-B31. It changes no expectation, and each case then tests the condition its title claims; `the_two_session_cases_pass_once_now_agrees_with_their_labels` shows MC-B22 and MC-B23 pass so moved on unmodified code. It resolves a contradiction inside the case file by the reading that adds no risk, so it is agent-accepted (DEC-176 (2)), and it lands separately (the YAML, `cargo xtask refcases --write`, and the reference checks, DEC-176), because this PR changes no spec or case (backlog, #331 review). **Proposed to the founder:** whether `session` and `in_close_window` stay case-file inputs at all, when the gate derives both from `now` (DEC-129 item 9). Until that is answered the harness keeps reading them as claims about the clock. (13) **What the cases cannot see:** MC-B25's `has_prior_fill` decides nothing, since no base rule reads `first_trade_in_instrument`. Of 26 bugs planted in `mandate-builder` one at a time, the B cases catch 21; `mandate-builder`'s own tests catch four of the other five (the two threshold boundaries, a spend bound blind to `goal_spent_usd`, and the zero-quantity guard of DEC-130 item 21, which `min_order_usd` 1 hides). The fifth, the cap headroom blind to working orders, is unreachable, and so is the whole third clamp it sits in (`cap − current_mv − working`): `Conviction ≤ 1` and `SizeFraction ≤ 1` give T ≤ cap, so Delta = T − current_mv − working never exceeds that clamp, and no input can make it bind. `test_default_gate_config` and `gate_mandate` repeat `risk_gate.rs`'s helpers (#317, merged); sharing them is a backlog row (#331 review). (14) **The status move is its own PR**, listing the 23 passing cases (ES-22). (15) **An ES-13 size exception.** `order_builder.rs` has 1,338 non-test `src` lines by DEC-165 item 17's rule (everything above its `#[cfg(test)]` module; 1,193 of them are neither blank nor doc comments), over ES-13's 800 for a crate outside the safety-critical list; `mandate-refcases` is `layer = "tool"`. It could be split at the builder/gate seam: a holds-only slice of about 793 non-test lines turns the ten hold cases green, and a gate slice of about 480 turns the thirteen order cases green. It is not split, because the split buys no safety in a `tool`-layer crate and costs a seam through the shared scene (the one clock and the one set of exposure figures the builder and the gate both read) and a second review. #317's `risk_gate.rs`, in the same crate, is 1,018 non-test lines, and DEC-178 took no exception for it. Precedents are DEC-140 and DEC-160 (22). (16) **Three autonomy facts are not inputs** (amends item 6; #331 review, nit). `ref.py` reads `drawdown`, `daily_pnl_fraction` and `bought_today_usd` only into §6.3's autonomy facts, and no family-B base has a rule on any of them, so no doctoring could show one read. They leave `INPUT_KEYS` and are given as 0, so a case that states one, even as 0, fails naming it; a key comes back with the first case whose base has a rule on it, and a test that shows it read. (17) **A cash fee rate above zero is refused, for crypto as for equities** (amends item 6; #331 review, minor 5).
