The gate is handed a `fee_reservation` of 0, which trading-domain §7.2 gives only for a fee paid in the asset, so a crypto buy whose cash fee the builder priced in would reach §9.5's buying-power check unreserved. Refusing is the reading that adds no risk; deriving the reservation from the rate waits for a case that states one. A stated 0 is read and passes, and a crypto asset fee rate is still accepted. (18) **Coverage the tests derive rather than count** (#331 review, minor 4, and round-2 nit). `an_unreadable_input_is_refused_naming_it` also sweeps the gate state's `positions_mv` and `last_exit_fill_at`, whole and by entry (a planted entry for the second, which no case states), its `working_universe`, whole and by entry, and each working order's `instrument` and `max_cost`, in the gate state and restated; the harness's errors name each by its path, as `gate_state.working_opening_orders[0].max_cost`. The sibling sweep's hand counts (87 and 11) are gone: an expectation is enum-valued when some family-B case states it as a snake-case word, a null, or a non-empty list of words, and every enum-valued member a swept case states must have a `sibling` arm, except `on_timeout` (one variant) and `action` (it decides which other members a case may state). `origin` and `trim_withheld` qualify, so the trim cases will need arms for them once they pass. **Amendment (2026-09-30, item 12's reference-case PR).** `reference/mandate/generate.py` moves each case's `now` with a helper, `at_now`, that keeps every output's `as_of` 60 s before `now` and its `expires_at` 3,600 s after, so freshness (`as_of ≤ now < expires_at`, `now − as_of` within `max_output_age_s`) is unchanged. The new clocks are MC-B22 at 2026-09-22T21:00Z (17:00 ET, after hours), MC-B23 at 19:55Z (15:55 ET, inside trading spec §9.6's 15:50–16:00 close window on a full day), and MC-B31 at 21:00Z. Their outputs move to 20:59Z and 22:00Z for MC-B22 and MC-B31, and to 19:54Z and 20:55Z for MC-B23. MC-B23's `expires_at` is 20:55Z, not the 21:00Z that `the_two_session_cases_pass_once_now_agrees_with_their_labels` uses; both are fresh. No other input changed, and all 298 `expect` blocks are identical before and after. `reference/mandate/check_cases.py` now requires every `kind: builder` case's `session` and `in_close_window` (header defaults `regular` and false) to equal trading spec §4.3's session and §9.6's close window at its `now`. On the old YAML that check fails exactly MC-B22, MC-B23 and MC-B31, so a label that contradicts its clock cannot come back. Under item 3 MC-B22 and MC-B23 now pass. MC-B31 still fails at `mandate_risk::trim_proposals` (pending E6-4), which it reaches before the labels are compared, so its failure is unchanged. Their status rows ship in a separate status-only PR. The harness's own test is corrected first, in crate code (ES-22): `order_builder.rs`'s `CLOCKED` table judges MC-B22 and MC-B23 by the fixture's consistency. Each must pass when its labels agree with `mandate_risk::session_at` at its `now`, and fail naming the conflict when they do not, so the test is green on the fixture before and after this change, and it catches a harness that passes a contradicting case or fails an agreeing one. Folding the two cases into `PASSING` and dropping the clock-moving test's doctoring wait until this change has merged (backlog). **Amendment (2026-09-30, the fold-back).** After #347 and #346 merged, MC-B22 and MC-B23 moved into `PASSING`, and `CLOCKED`, `labels_agree_with_calendar` and the clock doctoring were removed. The test, renamed `the_two_session_cases_pass_as_stated_and_compare_every_sibling`, runs on the fixture as stated. Item 3's label-against-clock comparison is still proved by `the_builder_and_the_gate_see_one_scene`'s doctored MC-B01. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; every reading tightens or takes the reading that adds no risk, DEC-176); item 15's reading of ES-13's 800-line limit superseded by DEC-253, which keeps the exception; item 12's fixture fix is Accepted (agent, DEC-176) and ships as its own reference-case PR; whether `session` and `in_close_window` stay case-file inputs is Proposed to the founder | Harness readings only: no production crate changes, and each reading makes a case fail sooner rather than pass on something unread. Running the real gate on the real proposal is what makes the dry run a test of the builder rather than a copy of the fixture; deriving the session from the clock is the gate's own rule, so the harness cannot let a label the gate would ignore decide a case | Hand `decide` the case's `gate_dry_run` (the gate would never see the proposal, and a wrong order would pass on the fixture's verdict); take the case's `session` and `in_close_window` as given (the gate derives them and would disagree, and the builder and the gate would see two markets); default an unstated gross exposure or working cost to zero (a case would pass on figures the gate state contradicts); skip the trim step and run the builder alone on `trim_to_target` cases (a risk exit the runtime would place first would go unexamined) |
| DEC-253 | **`safety_critical = true` governs whatever the crate's layer: a safety-critical `tool` crate is mutated on the diff and held to ES-13's safety-critical limit** (ADR-0001 ES-11 and ES-13; the #331 round-2 review's backlog row; the merge coordinator's dispatch). (1) **The mutation gate.** `cargo xtask ci mutants` mutates the changed source of every crate `xtask/layers.toml` marks `safety_critical = true`; `mutated_crates` no longer also requires an integer (product) layer. Today that adds `mandate-refcases`, the one safety-critical `tool` crate (`xtask` is not safety-critical). DEC-137's stub exemption and DEC-139's live-test pre-flight apply to it unchanged. Bugs seeded in the code the harness tests still prove what its checks catch; the gate adds that every harness line a change touches can fail. `the_gate_mutates_every_safety_critical_crate_tool_layer_included` pins it with a fixture workspace holding a flagged and an unflagged crate in each layer. (2) **What the gate asks of a harness change.** A pending case is an ignored test, and `cargo mutants` runs only live tests, so a harness line that only pending cases reach survives: the stub exemption covers `Unimplemented` stub bodies, not harness arms. Such a line needs a live harness test that drives it (a doctored case, as `tests/mandate_gate_harness.rs` and `tests/trading_domain_gate_harness.rs` do). (3) **ES-13.** Its "safety-critical crates" are the crates `layers.toml` flags, `tool` crates included, so a change over 400 non-generated lines in `mandate-refcases` is split. ES-13 is held by review, not by an xtask check, and ADR-0001's ES-11 and ES-13 rows now say so. (4) **Two recorded exceptions, not split:** DEC-178's `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/risk_gate.rs` (#317, 1,018 non-test lines by DEC-165 item 17's rule) and DEC-250's `crates/mandate-refcases/src/mandate/order_builder.rs` (1,338), each merged in one change. DEC-250 item 15's reasons for not splitting stand (a seam through the one shared scene, and a second review); its reading of the 800-line limit is superseded. Earlier harness changes over 400 lines (E3-1's #19, E4-1's #75, E6-4's #156, E5-1, and E6-9's #333) are not reopened: ES-13 is judged at merge, and this reading applies from this change on. (5) **Evidence, at the gate's `--jobs 2` on a 4-core machine.** A throwaway commit touching `parse_status` in `crates/mandate-refcases/src/lib.rs`: `MANDATE_BASE_REF=origin/main cargo xtask ci mutants` tested 3 mutants in 42 s (53 s wall) and failed on one missed, `parse_status` returning an empty map. E6-9's harness diff (#333, three `src` files, unchanged since) is 42 mutants in 5 min 28 s: 35 caught, 7 unviable. Family B's (DEC-250) is 79 mutants in 4 min 59 s: 40 caught, 26 unviable, 13 missed. The `full` job takes 2 to 3 minutes today against its 30-minute timeout. Both sets of survivors are backlog rows; neither is in this change's diff, which touches no crate source. | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 and DEC-176: it only adds checks) | The flag is the repository's one statement of which crates are safety-critical, and CODEOWNERS, the dependency allow-list and the lint header already read it without regard to layer; two checks that read the layer instead let a harness change land ungated while its entry claimed the gate. The harness decides whether a case passes, so a harness line no test can fail is a case that passes while checking nothing (AGENTS.md, "Tests that pass while checking nothing"), and the timing runs found 14 surviving mutants in merged harness code. About five minutes of mutants on a realistic harness change fits the `full` job's budget | Set `mandate-refcases` to `safety_critical = false` (drops the allow-list and the lint header, which weakens a safety rule, so the founder's call under DEC-79); mutate the harness only nightly (a line no test can fail would land, and the nightly run gates no merge); exempt harness lines that only pending cases reach, as stub bodies are (the gate cannot tell such a line from a dead one, and a live doctored-case test is the harness's existing pattern); split `risk_gate.rs` and `order_builder.rs` now (merged and reviewed; the split buys no check and costs two reviews, DEC-250 item 15); add an xtask check for ES-13's limit (new surface beyond the row, and ES-13 counts non-generated lines per change, which review already judges) |
| DEC-255 | **Trading-domain §3.2 item 7's "USD pairs only" gets a reason code of its own, `crypto_pair_not_usd`** ([trading spec §3.2](../specs/trading-domain.md#32-eligibility-floor-dec-31), v0.13; E6-10, stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123); DEC-254 item 3, recorded by E6-10's tests PR [#342](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/342) and not yet on `main`; DEC-129 items 25 and 27; ADR-0001 ES-09). (1) **The code.** `crypto_pair_not_usd` joins the trading-domain `reason_codes` registry under "eligibility and concentration", after `leveraged_etp_not_enabled` so the block keeps §3.2's list order. Its meaning: a crypto opening in a pair not quoted in USD; v1 trades USD pairs only. §3.2 item 7 names it, and the E6-3 brief's check-2 row lists it. (2) **Add only.** No registered code is renamed, removed or re-scoped, so ES-09's stable codes stay stable, and `not_in_working_universe` keeps the meaning mandate spec §5.3 gives it. (3) **Who decides.** This settles DEC-254 item 3, there Proposed (founder), by its own recommendation. The #342 review ruled a new code agent-acceptable under DEC-176 clause (2): the verdict stays deny and only the name of the reason changes, so it adds no risk, weakens no safety invariant, and closes a gap between the spec and the code the gate reports. (4) **What follows in code.** E6-10's later PRs add the `ReasonCode` variant, flip `usd_pairs.rs`'s `PAIR_CODE`, and make the gate report it. Until then the gate reports `not_in_working_universe` for the pair (DEC-254 item 3), `hand::every_reason_code_is_registered_in_the_case_file` still passes because it checks only that every code the gate emits is registered, and every reference case is unchanged | Accepted (agent, DEC-176) | Mandate spec §5.3 defines `not_in_working_universe` as the check that "the instrument is in the working universe". A BTC/USDT pair the research agent admitted is in it, so a denial journaled under that code would record a false reason, and a decision record is only auditable if its reasons are true | Keep `not_in_working_universe` (a false reason in the journal); `ineligible_exchange` (item 2's, scoped to US equities by its own words); `below_liquidity_floor` (would say a liquid pair lacked volume); one code per stablecoin (the gate treats every non-USD quote alike, and the list would need upkeep); leave the question Proposed for the founder (DEC-176 clause 2 covers a gap closed by the reading that adds no risk) |
| DEC-256 | **CI minutes on a private repository: the Playwright suite splits out of the `web` workflow and the nightly turns weekly** (the founder's direction in session, 2026-09-30; DEC-200, DEC-112, DEC-175, DEC-76). The repository goes private when the Actions quota resets on 2026-10-01, with 3,000 billed minutes a month, and the 2026-09-30 runs priced the two heavy checks: the Playwright suite needs 19–34 minutes on a 2-core runner per push that touches `web/`, and the widened mutation nightly (DEC-253), capped at 90, has never completed. (1) **The `web` workflow keeps the cheap checks and its name:** the Impeccable detector, the no-gradients rule, lint, the types, the unit suite, and the production build — about eight minutes — and it stays what the DEC-175 merge gate reads as the `web` check for a pull request that touches `web/`, so a web change still merges only with those green. (2) **The Playwright suite moves to `web-e2e.yml`** (`web/e2e/`'s computed-style checks in Chromium, light and dark, and the signed-out suite, DEC-211), running Sunday 04:17 UTC and on `workflow_dispatch` on any branch. The coordinator dispatches it before merging a web change that moves layout, tokens, or the specs — the judgment DEC-175 already gives the coordinator — and the weekly run catches drift between the specs and the design, which is how #363's sixteen failures went unseen: its `web` run died at the detector and no completed run existed to report them. (3) **The nightly runs Sunday 06:17 UTC** instead of every night, keeping its skip-when-main-is-unchanged guard and its `workflow_dispatch`; the coordinator can still run it before a risky safety-critical merge. (4) **What does not change:** `fast` and `full` still run per pull request, the merge gate's rules are untouched, and both heavy checks remain dispatchable at any time. (5) **A smoke subset is a backlog story, not this change:** a chosen slice of the suite (the volt rules, the landing contracts, Stop) could run per push in a few minutes once the W4 stream picks it | Accepted (founder, in session, 2026-09-30) | Minutes are the binding constraint: per-push e2e plus a nightly mutation run is 2,000–3,000 minutes a month on its own, and #363's lesson is that a check no completed run ever sees protects nothing — a weekly run plus dispatch before risky merges keeps the suite honest at a tenth of the cost | Keep both per push (unaffordable); manual only, no schedule (drift between the specs and the design goes unnoticed — exactly what #363 hid); a self-hosted runner on the founder's machine (self-hosted minutes are free on private repositories, and it can carry the nightly and the e2e later; needs the machine awake, so it is the follow-up, not this change); a per-push smoke subset now (a W4 choice of which tests are the smoke, and the budget already survives without it) |
| DEC-257 | **E8-3's check 7 against the policy overlay: the implementation's readings** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213); DEC-173 items 13 to 15). (1) **`quorum` computes the order value only when `two_approver_above_usd` is set**, and compares it with the ceiling exactly on `mandate-num` decimals, strictly above asking for two. With no ceiling the answer is total, so `PolicyOverlay::NONE` never errs where DEC-173 item 14's interim rule never erred. An order value that overflows under a ceiling is `ApprovalError::Unrepresentable`, which `content_object` already returns when the request is built, so no pending request reaches it; the doc says so (#354 and #355 review, nit 2). (2) **`admit` calls `quorum` for every overlay**, so DEC-173 item 14's fail-closed interim rule ends: no overlay makes a grant that reaches check 7 err. (3) **While independence is required, the author is left out of the grants that count**, at check 7 itself rather than by rewriting the folded grant set, so the set stays what admission recorded and an overlay turned off later counts the author's earlier grant again, never below the bound quorum (DEC-173, the founder's after-the-fact item 2). (4) **The #354 and #355 review's nit 1 (a zero ceiling the policy schema excludes) stays a backlog row**, because DEC-77 lets this implementation PR change `tests/` only by deleting `#[ignore]` lines; nits 2 to 5 are done here. **The runtime's tests PR (M7 tests PR 3 of 4) adds items 5 to 12.** (5) **The control-stream payloads, until their schemas close:** `ApprovalResponseSubmitted` carries `agent`, `approval`, `verdict` (`approved`, `skipped`), `content_hash` (`sha256:` and the hex digest), `submitted_at`, `step_up` (`{assertion_id, authenticated_at, method}` or null), `responder` and `role`; `OwnerCommandIssued` carries `agent`, `command`, `scope` (`agent`, `connection`, `workspace`, `instrument`), `subject`, `bid`, `bid_size` and `floor` (decimals or null), `submitted_at`, `step_up` and `user`. Instants are whole risk-clock seconds, as the runtime's `deadline` already is; when journal spec §9 closes these schemas the reading moves with it. (6) **The runtime's `FoldedEvent` carries the envelope's `actor.kind`**, which check 3 reads (EI-10). The tracer folds only agent and account streams and reads every event as `system`, which admission never admits; mapping the envelope on a control-stream tail is stream L's, with the tail. (7) **An owner's exit of one instrument is handed as an agent-scoped flatten of that instrument** (`FlattenRequest.instrument`), identified by the `OwnerExitRequested` that records it, as a kill switch's flatten is by its `KillSwitchActivated`: the runtime cannot size or price a one-instrument exit itself, which is the planner's (family F). Evidence not valid when the owner committed it is recorded (`step_up_status` `stale` or `absent`, `step_up` null, a confirmed bid as given) and the plan carries no confirmation, so equities wait for the regular session and the exit itself is never withheld (the #281 obligation, rule 13). An `IntentProposed` for an owner exit waits for E7-2's order-shaped owner exit. (8) **`step_up_status` for evidence that is reused or of a method the environment refuses is `absent`**: §9.1 has three values, and mandate spec §6.1 counts evidence that does not count as missing; only an age outside the window is `stale`. (9) **A request's batch is `DecisionMade`, `ApprovalRequested` (the content object inline and its hash), `ApprovalDelivered` (`cli_inbox`, `delivered`), then the deadline timer and the opaque notification**, so the notification never points at an uncommitted request and every request is grantable from creation (DEC-156 item 6). (10) **A resume's and a Stop's step-up is judged at the later of `submitted_at` and the folded clock**, the effective time a response has (DEC-156 items 1 and 8); an owner exit and a kill switch at `submitted_at`. (11) **The runtime tests' event ids are ULID-shaped**, since an approval's reference is built only from one (DEC-165 item 13), and spelled in Crockford letters so no id holds a digit a sentinel scan reads as a quantity. (12) **Out of this tests PR, as backlog rows:** `PolicyChanged`'s fold and the two-approver runtime cases (journal spec §9 gives its payload only as level and diff); a journaled record for a refused resume or Stop (no agent-stream event is specified, so the tests pin only that it does not apply, beside the case where it does); retiring the owner commands that `Input::Command` still carries (stream I's); and the journal catalogue's three entries, which the runtime's implementation needs before a real journal accepts `ApprovalRevalidated`. **The CLI's tests PR (M7 tests PR 4 of 4) adds items 13 to 17.** (13) **Each code is derived on the owner's host and bound to everything it confirms:** `approve`'s is `mandate_approval::confirmation_code` of the request's content hash, which `show` prints; a kill switch's is `kill_switch_code` of its scope and the control stream's head; resume's, Stop's, an owner exit's and an acknowledgment's are bound to the agent, the command, the control stream's head, and for an owner exit the instrument and the confirmed bid, bid size and floor (DEC-66), for an acknowledgment the acknowledged event, so a code goes stale once any other command lands and never confirms another bid. The tests pin the binding, not the derivation, which the implementation chooses within mandate spec §6.1's `cli_confirm`. (14) **Which commands the CLI refuses locally:** resume, Stop and an acknowledgment without their code are refused as `step_up_missing` and commit nothing, so pause stays the brake; a kill switch and an owner exit are **never** refused here, and without their code they are committed with `step_up: null`, which the runtime applies without the privilege (DEC-158 option (c), rule 13, the #281 obligation's CLI half); pause needs no code (PX-4). (15) **A local refusal names the runtime's own reason** (`not_pending`, `late`, `content_mismatch`, `step_up_missing`), commits nothing, and is a convenience: the runtime judges every committed event again (DEC-155 item 5). What a command reports is only what the runtime recorded: `approve`'s outcome reads the `ApprovalResponded` whose `causation_id` is the submitted event, and until one exists it says the default is a skip and never says approved or sent (rule 3). (16) **One invocation commits exactly one control-stream event:** it mints the event id once (the idempotency key, DEC-155 item 2), takes a new writer epoch, and when fenced, behind the head, or left without an answer (`Ambiguous`) retries the same draft with a fresh read, so a retry of an append that did commit is `AlreadyCommitted` and nothing is committed twice. (17) **The CLI's tests use their own in-memory journal (`tests/common`) that runs journal spec §5.1's idempotency, fencing and head check, and injects a fence, a moved head, and a lost answer, but validates no payload**, because `mandate-journal` registers no payload schema for any agent- or control-stream event yet, so a real journal refuses each one as `UnknownSchema`; the payload schemas are a backlog row the runtime's and the CLI's implementations both need. The commands' `clap` wiring and a Postgres-backed `ControlJournal` land with the implementation | Accepted (agent, DEC-79, DEC-176: each item tightens or matches the spec's sentence) | The spec's check 7 sentence decides the values; these readings only place the arithmetic and the author's exclusion so the answer stays total and the record replayable | Compute the order value for every overlay (makes `NONE` fallible for no gain); drop the author from the folded grant set (loses the record a later overlay needs); edit `tests/quorum.rs` here (DEC-77 forbids it) |
| DEC-259 | **How E6-6's second slice folds trading-domain §9.2's `legacy_pdt` day-trade ledger** (stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123); DEC-129 item 6, DEC-150 item 7; [task brief](tasks/E6-3-risk-gate.md)). (1) **Where and what.** `mandate_risk::fold_day_trades` is a pure fold over the **account's** inputs, every agent's, because the budget is the account's (DEC-150 item 7): the day trades on earlier trading days (the broker's report, or an earlier fold's `DayTradeFold::today`), the US-equity quantity per instrument held at the start of today, today's fills in execution order, and the broker's pattern-day-trader flag. It returns the `DayTradeLedger` check 8 reads and today's day trades for the executor to journal; the gate itself still reads the ledger as an input, so `evaluate` is unchanged. (2) **Today** is §2.2's equity trade date of `now` on the committed exchange calendar (DEC-129 item 9): the New York date, the next day from 20:00 ET, then the first trading day on or after it, as `mandate-time`'s `TradingCalendar::equity_trade_date` dates a fill. The window is today plus the four prior trading days of the same calendar; a date outside it is `ConfigOutOfRange`, never a guess. (3) **Counting.** A sell takes shares held overnight first (§9.2), and a sell that takes shares bought today counts once per purchase run: it counts when a purchase came after the last counted day trade, so buy, buy, sell is one day trade and buy, sell, buy, sell is two ("each same-day open-then-close counts once"). A sale of shares held overnight followed by a same-day purchase is **not** a day trade; that reading is the founder's, recorded in DEC-269: sell, buy counts nothing, and a later sell of the repurchased shares closes a same-day open, so sell, buy, sell is one. Fractional day trades count; crypto fills are read for their order only and never count, nor appear in either set. (4) **The sets.** `sold_earlier_today` holds every equity sold today, of overnight shares or today's (the tighter reading of "the same security was sold earlier today"); `open_same_day_positions` holds every equity with shares bought today still held. (5) **Malformed input is refused, never folded to a smaller count:** fills out of execution order or after `now`, an equity fill outside today's trading day, or an earlier day trade dated today or later is `DayTradeLedgerOutOfOrder`, and a sell of more than the account held is the new `DayTradeLedgerInconsistent`, since it would be a short sale and a missed fill is the likelier cause. An error refuses openings only: the ledger is read by check 8 alone, which exits never reach (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). (6) **What stays for the harness half.** Interpreting RC-09's and RC-09B's `regime`, `prior_day_trades`, `last_equity`, `multiplier` and `day_trade_count` in `mandate-refcases` changes RC-09B's pending reason, which `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/trading_domain_gate_harness.rs` pins and an implementation PR may not edit (DEC-77), and both cases carry a second `propose_order` step that DEC-199 item 3 refuses until E7-4 and E7-5; RC-09's third step is also a crypto proposal, which waits for E6-10. (7) **A later proposal in a reference case waits for the earlier one's fills, not for E7-4 and E7-5** (DEC-199 item 3 narrowed; [the coordinator's ruling on #370](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/370#issuecomment-5933118197), item 2). The trading-domain harness decides a later `propose_order` step only once every earlier *allowed* proposal in the case was filled in full, on its instrument and side, by `fill` steps before it. Three conditions bind the reading. **(a) Anything short of a full fill stays refused**, as before: no fill, a partial fill, and overlapping proposals, including two identical buys with no fill between them (#349's protection). A refused proposal leaves no trace. **(b) The conduct figures come from the case's own `fill` steps, never from the proposal:** one order submitted and one filled in the instrument, the filled quantity in today's participation, the last fill's instant as the latest fill on its side, and one order today if it opened or increased. **(c) It is the reference harness's reading only.** It changes nothing in the gate or the executor, and it lapses when E7-4 and E7-5's order path drives these cases. The tests correction #408 removes the one assertion that pinned the old reading (RC-08's denied proposal listed twice); E6-6 slice 2's harness PR implements the reading, with the tests the backlog's E6-6 slice 2 row names | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; item 3 except its overnight-sale reading, and item 4, under DEC-176 as the spec's own text and the reading that adds no risk; the overnight-sale reading is the founder's, DEC-269; item 7, the coordinator's ruling on #370) | Slice 1 (#221) read the ledger as an input the caller folds, so an agent-scoped ledger would have undercounted the account's day trades (#221 review, round 1, minor). Every rule here is §9.2's own; the fold lives beside check 8 because no other crate has a reason to know them (DEC-129 item 6) | Count a sale of overnight shares followed by a purchase as a day trade (§9.2 read literally; the founder took FINRA's reading instead, DEC-269); clamp an oversell to zero (adds risk: it would undercount); fold per agent (undercounts the account's budget) |
| DEC-260 | **Slice 4a's readings where the rulings on the exit price ladder are silent** (stream K, E7-4 slice 4a; trading-domain spec §5.4 and §5.6; `AGENTS.md` rules 3 and 13; DEC-160 items (10), (12) and (23)). Each only tightens or names a case, and none adds risk (DEC-176). (1) **What is laddered.** An exit whose instrument's sequence runs, or will once the gate allows it, is priced by §5.6; every other order keeps its intent's limit, and so does a passive sequence's own exit, which §5.4 places as the new OCO's take-profit at that limit and which needs nothing to price from, so it is never held `exit_unpriced`. An instrument with no exit tier has no ladder: its exit goes at its own limit and is never cancelled to step. (2) **The reference.** §5.6's "last sane bid within the last 5 minutes" is the latest sane quote with a bid, kept past newer quotes that are not (`sane_bids`). It is process-local like the latest quote, so after a restart the ladder prices from the next quote, or falls back or holds as DEC-160 (12) says. (3) **A pause ends the ladder.** While the agent's effective mode is `paused` or `stopped` no rung is cancelled to step, so the working rung keeps resting; a step's cancel the pause overtakes submits no next rung, and its confirmation ends the sequence and re-places protection for the held quantity. Rule 13 lets a pause hold an exit, and rule 3 wants protection back rather than an unprotected position held to the interval's bound, which is where a held exit in a running sequence ends today (DEC-160 (19)). **The pause stop applies only to the purposes the mode may hold** ([#373](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/373) round 1, major 1, the coordinator's ruling): §5.5 exempts kill-switch and mandate-limit flatten exits from the agent's mode ("apply the final mode first (`paused`) → cancel → confirm → close … otherwise the exit price ladder"), so their ladder keeps stepping while paused. Slice 4a cannot reach that case, because the gate's `mode_failure` holds every risk-reducing order at `paused` or stricter; slice 6, which owns the kill switch, narrows the stop and the gate together, under the pending test the backlog binds it to. (4) **No silent drop.** `submit` never drops an allowed exit for want of a price: the gate holds a discretionary exit with nothing to price from before it allows it, so one found unpriced after its allow, which no path reaches today, goes as the fallback does. (5) **The last trade** (amended by [#373](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/373) round 1, blocker 1, the coordinator's ruling; DEC-176, it only tightens). §5.6's "the last trade" is the latest-observed **sane** quote that carried one, kept past newer quotes that carry none (`trades`, process-local like `sane_bids`), and it prices a rung only within the same five minutes as the last sane bid; §8.2's in-session test joins it with slice 5, which folds the session. An insane print or an old one prices nothing: with no sane bid and no fresh, sane trade, the exit takes §5.6 step 4, so risk, protective and owner exits go at their own limit with the `exit_unpriced` alert at once, and a discretionary exit is held. `sane_bids` and `trades` are never replaced by an older observation, and the ladder reads what it prices from in the order it was observed, the latest quote included (round 1, minor 2). (6) **On the tick.** A rung is a sell limit, so §2.1 rounds it up to the tick: an equity's onto the Reg NMS grid (141 × 0.995 = 140.295 goes as 140.30, never as a sub-penny limit the broker refuses), a crypto rung as priced, as the gate's collar does (`mandate-risk`'s `on_grid`). (7) **What an open sell holds back** (rule 13, found by the slice's random-script oracle in code merged with E7-2). §5.3's "the position less the open non-protective sells" counts each open sell's unfilled quantity, as DEC-160 (20) already reads it: a partly filled exit's fills have left the position, so `gate::available` counting its whole quantity again denied `sell_exceeds_available` to an exit that crossed nothing. (8) **One ladder per sequence.** The ladder steps the sequence's own exit; another exit the gate lets go beside a running sequence is priced at the first rung and is ended by the interval's bound, like any exit working there (DEC-160 (19)); its own clock never moves the sequence's. Tests: `a_pause_ends_the_ladder_and_re_protects`, `an_exit_with_no_tier_never_steps`, `a_journaled_passive_sequence_with_no_take_profit_answers_the_stub` (the passive exit's own limit), `the_sequences_exit_prices_from_the_last_sane_bid`, `the_last_trade_outlives_a_newer_quote_without_one`, `an_insane_print_never_prices_a_rung`, `an_old_trade_never_prices_a_rung`, `the_newer_observed_sane_bid_wins_over_a_later_arrival`, `a_rung_is_on_the_equity_tick`, `only_a_crypto_rung_is_left_off_the_equity_tick`, `a_partly_filled_exit_counts_only_what_it_may_still_sell`, `another_exits_submission_leaves_the_ladder_alone`, and the random-script oracle `rule_13_holds_over_random_scripts`. **Slice 4b's readings** (DEC-160 (24)): (9) **The watchdog's clock starts when the executor learns of the breach**, at the later of the quote's own time and the executor's clock: a stale quote cannot claim the mark has been at the stop for `stop_watchdog_s` already, so it never fires the watchdog at once. The clock is process-local, so a restart starts it again from the next breaching mark, which delays the exit and never adds risk while the stop still rests. (10) **The watchdog exit's id** is `md-w-<record>`. A record id is a ULID in production; the `md-w-` prefix keeps the id apart from every intent's whatever the record id's characters, so `ClientOrderId::for_intent` takes letters, digits and inner hyphens after `w-` (the tests' event ids have hyphens) and still refuses an id that reads back as a protective order's. (11) **The watchdog exit's limit**, which it falls back to when nothing prices it (DEC-160 (12)), is the lowest of the latest **sane** quote's mark and bid and the last sane bid's mark and bid ([#385](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/385) round 1, blocker 1, the coordinator's ruling; rule 3): an insane quote never sets or moves it, it is never above a fresh bid and never placed as a passive take-profit, and the ladder prices the exit otherwise. With no sane observation to take it from, the watchdog does not fire: the breach persists, the stop keeps resting and nothing is cancelled, and no alert is due, since §5.6 step 4 alerts an exit at its fallback or held and no exit exists yet; the next sane quote prices it (round 1, minor 1). **One breach fires one watchdog:** the record's time is folded (`watchdogged`), a breach fires only if it began after the instrument's last watchdog, and the next sane breaching mark after a watchdog starts a new breach, no earlier than the second after it. A new breach while the watchdog's own exit still waits fires nothing, which would sell the same shares twice once the exit is released. **A breach never outlives the recovery that ends it** ([#385](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/385) round 2, blocker 2, the coordinator's ruling): any sane mark above the stop ends it, and so does any sane mark while no stop rests, as in the interval the watchdog's own sequence opens. The fold drops the protection once its last leg is cancelled, so a recovery seen then would otherwise be ignored, and the bound's re-placed protection would be cancelled by a second watchdog on a breach no sane quote still supports. Ending a breach only ever delays a watchdog, so it adds no risk. So a stale or bad feed cannot cancel and restore the protection again and again once the bound has ended an exit that did not fill. (12) **What 4b leaves to slice 5:** §5.4's "in a session where the stop can trigger" needs the session slice 5 folds, and a crypto stop-limit's "below its limit price" needs slice 5's stop-limit. Until then the watchdog fires on any breach that lasts, which can only end in an exit, never add risk, and no protected instrument exists outside the tests before slice 5 (DEC-160 (4)). Tests: `a_stale_breach_starts_the_clock_when_it_arrives`, `a_watchdog_exit_is_never_passive`, `an_insane_quote_never_prices_the_watchdog_exit`, `a_watchdog_with_nothing_sane_to_price_from_waits`, `a_breach_that_outlasts_the_first_exit_fires_again`, `a_breach_is_spent_in_the_second_it_fires`, `a_recovery_during_the_interval_ends_the_breach`, `only_a_sane_mark_at_or_below_a_resting_stop_starts_a_breach`, and the two hand tests through `md-w-` ids with hyphens. **Slice 5's session part** (DEC-160 (9); the coordinator's rulings D1 to D3 on [#174](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/174), comment 5926142854, agent decisions under DEC-176): (13) **The session is the calendar's, and a closed market holds the exit** (D1). The executor derives the session an order goes in from the committed US-equities calendar (`mandate_time::ExchangeCalendar`) at the step's clock, as `mandate-risk` does, never from a label; a `ConductBreachDetected` folds as a record (a conduct control never holds an exit, rule 13). **`TradingDayStarted` stays at its stub:** the reference-case harness journals one at every case's first date, so folding it would carry every pending case past its old stub into code paths they cannot pass yet (GTC re-placement, the harness's own startup reconciliation, which holds its openings `startup_reconciliation_pending`), where no stub stops them. The trading day therefore stays with slice 5's trading-day part, and RC-24 with it, even though this part prices RC-24's exits (DEC-160 (9), which amends D3's list). A risk exit, a protective order or a flatten in pre-market or after-hours goes as a limit with `extended_hours = true` (§4.3); a discretionary or owner exit there goes as a regular-session limit, which the broker queues to the session (§5.2). While no v1 session is open (overnight, DEC-30; a weekend; a holiday) an **equity** exit of any purpose is held `session_closed`, rule 13's broker hold, until the calendar's next pre-market open, protection resting and the watchdog unchanged; at the first tick from that open it is released, priced fresh from the quotes then, and its ladder's clock starts at the release. When the calendar can name no next pre-market open (its last days) the exit is held `session_unknown` and alerted, never sent queued or extended-hours. Crypto never takes this hold; an instrument the snapshot does not classify takes the equity sessions, failing closed ([#400](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/400) round 1, minor 3). The overnight session of a trading day is `session_closed` even on the calendar's last day, since that day's 04:00 open ends it (minor 1). **The calendar's range** (the coordinator's ruling 5926945398): an instant after its last date is held `session_unknown` and alerted, never read as the regular session, so the exits do not quietly become broker-queued the day the 2018–2028 calendar runs out; an instant before its first date is read as the regular session, as before this slice, because only the tests' 1970 clocks reach it, until a tests PR moves those clocks onto the calendar and the before-range case holds too. **DEC-77's amendment names `crates/mandate-executor/tests/coverage.rs`** for this one change (same ruling): slice 5 replaces `a_held_intent_past_its_age_is_abandoned_at_a_tick`, which asserted the abandonment (17) reverses, with `a_held_exit_past_its_age_is_never_abandoned`, which also pins that a held opening is still abandoned at `max_intent_age_s`. The closing auction window is §4.3's default, the last 10 minutes, a constant until the gate's configuration carries it. (14) **What else is laddered** (amends (1)). §5.6 ladders an exit that must be marketable where no market order may go, protection resting or not: a risk exit, a protective order or a flatten in extended hours, the closing auction window or a presumed halt, and an owner exit in the latter two (outside the regular session an owner exit needs the confirmed bid, slice 6's). A presumed halt is the latest quote older than `exit_step_s` or not sane (§4.4; the executor reads no status feed yet). A discretionary exit is paced (§9.6) and keeps its own limit, as does an exit priced above a fresh sane bid. Such an exit's rungs fold from their `OrderSubmitted` (`laddered`) into a ladder of its own, which steps as a sequence's does while its agent's mode is below `paused`; a step's cancel that a pause overtakes still submits the next rung, since no protection returns for the remainder and the rung only replaces a working order. (15) **The watchdog and the session** (closes (12)'s first half). A breach is watchdogged only in the regular session, and `stop_watchdog_s` counts from the later of its start and the regular open: a pre-market breach fires no sooner than `stop_watchdog_s` after 09:30, an after-hours or weekend one not at all. The crypto stop-limit's condition stays with slice 5's stop-limit. (16) **§8.2's in-session test** (closes (5)'s): a trade observed in another session than the step's prices no rung. (18) **A ladder that steps into a closed market parks** ([#400](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/400) round 1, blocker 1; the coordinator's ruling 5928646033). A step whose cancel is confirmed while no v1 session is open submits no rung, for the exit ladder and a sequence's alike: the exit is journaled held once, `GateDecided` `session_closed` (`session_unknown`, alerted, where the calendar names no open) with `parked`, which the fold neither gates again nor resends from; a sequence ends and re-places its protection for the night, its ladder kept; at the first tick of the next open the ladder resumes from the next rung, priced fresh from the quotes then, its clock starting there, through a new marketable sequence where protection rests again. A parked ladder waits out a pause. `tests/properties.rs` drops `TradingDayStarted` from `INTERPRETED` (24 → 23), the file the same ruling names under DEC-77's amendment: #392 listed it before any slice folds it; the trading-day part restores it. (17) **A held exit is never abandoned for age** (D2; rule 13, DEC-160 (12); amends DEC-133's `max_intent_age` for exits). An exit of any purpose, held for any reason, waiting on its cancels, or found received at a restart, is never `intent_too_old`: it is priced at its release from the quotes then, so the stale price the age guards against is never acted on. An exit still held past `max_intent_age_s` is journaled once more (`GateDecided`, `held_long`, its hold's reason) and the owner alerted once (`exit_held_long`). An opening keeps the abandonment. Tests: `an_after_hours_risk_exit_is_laddered_and_extended`, `a_presumed_halt_or_the_close_window_ladders_the_exit`, `a_closed_market_holds_the_exit_for_the_next_pre_market_open` (overnight, a Friday evening to Monday, the Friday before Labor Day to Tuesday), `a_closed_market_hold_leaves_the_protection_resting`, `a_closed_market_with_no_next_open_holds_and_alerts`, `a_long_hold_alerts_once_and_the_exit_still_goes` (paused, `exit_unpriced`, `session_closed`), `an_old_exit_still_goes_once_its_cancel_is_confirmed`, `an_old_passive_exit_still_places_its_oco`, `coverage::a_held_exit_past_its_age_is_never_abandoned`, `a_lone_ladder_steps_and_a_pause_stops_only_new_steps`, `a_trade_from_another_session_prices_no_rung`, `the_watchdog_waits_for_the_regular_session`, `session::venue_tests`, `a_closed_market_never_holds_an_exit_the_gate_denies`, `a_sequence_that_runs_into_the_close_re_protects`, `a_step_into_the_close_parks_the_ladder_until_the_open`, `a_sequence_step_into_the_close_re_protects_and_resumes_at_the_open`, `the_last_trading_days_overnight_reads_session_closed`, `a_session_unknown_hold_is_never_abandoned`, `session::venue_tests::an_unclassified_instrument_takes_the_equity_sessions`, and `hand::a_risk_exit_submits_inside_the_close_window`. **Slice 6's readings** (the coordinator's ruling on [#400](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/400) round 2, 5930410998; `AGENTS.md` rules 3, 6 and 13): (19) **A park alerts once, when it parks, and is reported as a long hold from the start.** A ladder that parks (18) alerts the owner once, as it is journaled, for `session_closed` as already for `session_unknown`: the alert is the park's `GateDecided` id and the hold's reason as its key, nothing about the order. **The once-only alert comes from the held-set exclusion:** a parked exit was allowed and stepped, so it is never in the held set that (17)'s `exit_held_long` re-journal walks, and nothing re-alerts it, across a restart included (the journal carries the park). **The mark is what reports the long hold:** the park's `GateDecided` carries `held_long: true`, since a park lasts until the next open and so outlives `max_intent_age_s` by construction, and the fold's `parked` arm reads it into the folded `held_long` set, which is what `exit_held_long` reports (the coordinator's ruling on [#424](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/424) round 1, 5941392985). `a_sequence_parked_overnight_alerts_the_owner_exactly_once` pins both against the journal folded afresh: the parked intent is in `held_long` and not in `held`. An exit **gated** while the market is closed is unchanged: it is held `session_closed` and alerted `exit_held_long` once at `max_intent_age_s`, so it too raises one notification a night. **This amends DEC-160 (2) for slice 6.** The alert and the mark ride on the park's live path, and a stub there (`park` answering `Unimplemented`) fails 4 live tests (`a_step_into_the_close_parks_the_ladder_until_the_open`, `a_sequence_step_into_the_close_re_protects_and_resumes_at_the_open` and both rule-13 tests, measured by #424's review), so DEC-160 (2)'s loud stub is unavailable: the slice's three pending tests fail on the missing alert and mark instead, and are named in `xtask`'s `BEHAVIOUR_ONLY_TESTS` until the implementation PR deletes them (DEC-137, as DEC-140 (2) did for E7-4's first rows). (20) **The rule-13 oracle reaches a step into the close on every run.** `rule_13_holds_for_a_step_into_the_close` scripts #400's composed plant's path from 19:59:50 ET, so the determinism comes from the script; the random oracle keeps drawing that start 3 times in 14. Tests: `hand::a_parked_night_alerts_the_owner_exactly_once`, `hand::a_restart_in_a_parked_night_alerts_nothing_more`, `a_sequence_parked_overnight_alerts_the_owner_exactly_once`, `rule_13_holds_for_a_step_into_the_close` | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79) | Reversible engineering inside one crate; each reading keeps an exit going or re-protects, never denies one, and adds no risk | Keep climbing the ladder while paused (a new order from a paused agent); hold the paused exit to the interval's bound (the position stays unprotected meanwhile); ladder a passive exit (holds a working take-profit for a price it never uses) |
| DEC-261 | **Journal spec v0.7 §9.2 closes the control-stream payload schemas `ValidationContext::from_journal` reads, `AccountSnapshotRecorded`, and `OwnerCommandRefused`, and the test vectors gain a generated `control_stream` section** (E7-10, DEC-168; stream L, claim [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171); the coordinator's ruling there of 2026-10-01 on E7-10's blocker, which reserved this id). (1) **Scope.** The control stream's `StreamOpened`, `ConnectionEstablished`, `ConnectionRevoked`, `DisclosureAccepted`, `ConfigSnapshotRegistered`, `MandateVersionCreated`, `MandateConfirmed`, `AgentDeployed`, and `AgentStopped`. The account stream's `AccountSnapshotRecorded`. `OwnerCommandRefused` on the agent and account streams, whose members DEC-291 item 1 fixed "until journal spec §9.1 closes the schema", so E7-10 registers it with the rest (#411, #413). `ConnectionRevoked` is not in the ruling's list, but `JournaledFact` reads it and the backlog's mapping row names it. **(2) Each member's source**, per the ruling's condition that a member comes from a writer that exists or from a clause that names it:<br>• `AccountSnapshotRecorded`: the executor's writer (`orders::account_fields` and reconciliation step 4 in `reconcile.rs`), all fourteen members. The three cash members are `null` where the writer makes no comparison (the fee step), which today omits them. §4.2 needs them present.<br>• `OwnerCommandRefused`: the runtime's writer (`escalation::commanded`, DEC-291 item 1), with `acknowledge` from §9's account-stream row.<br>• `ConnectionEstablished`: `broker` and `scopes` from §9's row; `connection_id` from mandate spec §3; `environment` from V-001 ("a paper or live account"); the ruling's "opaque id and scopes only". §9's "permission-check result" is not a member. FR-2.2 rejects a connection whose check fails, so every record would carry the same value. `ConnectionRevoked`: `connection_id`.<br>• `DisclosureAccepted`: document, version hash, user, and step-up from §9 and mandate spec §10.<br>• `ConfigSnapshotRegistered`: kind and content hash from §9. The kinds are §9's seven `config_refs` kinds, the row's "calendar" being both calendars and a model `model_version`, since DEC-169 maps a model's registration to this event. `model_id`, `model_version`, and `params` come from V-007 ("id, version, and content hash are registered together; parameters are exactly the model's declared parameters"). `admits_instruments` comes from mandate spec §3's field table (`behavior.signal_models[].admits_instruments`, the field a mandate pins with the model) and §8.4.<br>• `MandateVersionCreated`, `MandateConfirmed`: the ruling's item 2. The members are the version, the provenance per path (mandate spec §2.1's sources), and the confirmed paths. The rest of each §10 row is `record_ref`, a `ref` to the stored record. `mandate_version` is a `ref` too, so the canonical document is stored under its own version and re-hashes (§11 check 6).<br>• `AgentDeployed`: the agent and mandate version from §9 and §10. The rest of §10's row is `record_ref`, the ruling's item 2 applied to the third row §10 defines. The connection, environment, allocation, and pinned instruments that `JournaledFact::AgentVersionActive` needs are read from the stored document, not restated.<br>• `AgentStopped`: agent, reason, and net dollar loss from §9 and mandate spec §5.10. `connection_id` from §9's "the connection's loss carry". `retired_on` from §5.7's "retired on it in the last 90 days", the date that window counts from; the envelope's `event_time` is informational (§3) and cannot drive a fold. The reasons are §9's, with "owner stop" spelled `owner_stop` as `AgentModeChanged` spells it.<br>• Control-stream `StreamOpened`: §2. **(3) Readings that only tighten** (DEC-176 (1)): a `pointer` is never the empty pointer (a record names each path it means); `DisclosureAccepted.step_up` is never `null` (V-005 counts only an acceptance with step-up); rule 22 (a deployment's payload version is its `config_refs` version); rule 24's flag must agree with its own numbers; rule 26 confines `acknowledge` to the account stream and `resume` and `stop` to the agent stream; rule 27 extends rule 16's cause requirement to the account stream's refusal. Each only refuses drafts, and none applies to a risk exit, a protective order, or a kill switch, so none can hold a reduction (`AGENTS.md` rule 13). **(4) No credential or personal data.** Every schema is closed. A key, token, or account number has no member to sit in and is refused as `schema`; the vectors show it for a connection and a snapshot. **(5) Vectors.** `reference/journal/generate.py` keeps the agent stream's section. Its canonical-form and type helpers move unchanged to `reference/journal/common.py`; `--check` reproduces the existing section byte for byte, and all 81 of its mutants are still caught. The new `reference/journal/control.py` builds the `control_stream` section. That section has 10 chain events with 7 artifacts and 4 base drafts on the account and agent streams. It lists the 10 `JournaledFact`s the records map to, with 52 invalid and 8 valid drafts. 28 validator mutants and 19 vector mutants are each caught, each vector mutant by its own registered check only. Round 1 of the review added three drafts and their mutants. A nullable member that is absent (`model_cash`) is `schema`, never read as `null` (`record.missing.nullable`). A date of the wrong length is refused (`types.date_length`). Rule 22 compares the whole hash (`rule.22.prefix`). The mandate document is the mandate reference cases' `btc_accumulator`. Its version is first recomputed from that file's own canonical text, then its model's placeholder content hash is replaced by the stored model content's, since a `ref` must re-hash. The facts are recomputed from the payloads and the stored document by their own path. The vectors stay version 3: the section is additive, as in DEC-177 item 6. **(6) The mapping to `JournaledFact`** is §9.2's table. It is E7-10's to implement, tests first. **(7) Two writers do not conform yet, and each has a backlog row.**<br>• **Stream K, the executor.** Its fee-step `AccountSnapshotRecorded` omits the three cash members, which §9.2 requires present as `null`.<br>• **Stream I / M7, the runtime.** Its `OwnerCommandRefused` writes `effective_at` as integer risk-clock seconds (`escalation.rs`, `payload::seconds`), where §9.2 types it `timestamp`.<br>Per `AGENTS.md` rule 9, §9.2 supersedes DEC-291 item 1's "the same second" for this member. The instant is still the effective time the command was judged at, but it is recorded as a §4.7 timestamp, as §9.1 ruled for every other payload instant. `crates/mandate-runtime/src/escalation/tests.rs`'s assertion that `effective_at` is an integer changes with that writer.<br>Until each writer lands, the drafts it names stay refused at `append`, which adds no risk (rule 3). **E7-10's implementation must not register `AccountSnapshotRecorded` until stream K's fee-step writer conforms.** The fee step pauses every agent and alerts the owner on a fee difference, and refusing its snapshot at `append` must never stop it (rules 3 and 13). E7-10's tests PR carries a pending test that pins this ordering. (Round 1 of the review, B1 and M1, ruled by the coordinator on #425.) **(8)** The connect flow (E7-1) owes `ConnectionEstablished` its connecting user and step-up (HLD §8), as a new `schema_version` with its own vectors (a backlog row). **(9) `PlatformOperatorAction` stays open (Proposed).** Its five actions include three whose members no clause names (the operator stop's subject, the global kill switch's scope, the acceptable-use action) and an "approval" no clause defines. Close it with the operator service's specification (E17-6's later slice). `append` refuses it as `unknown_schema` until then, as today. Its `model_withdrawn` and `research_thesis_halt` members (mandate spec §8.1, DEC-100) are the proposal's starting point. `JournaledFact::ModelWithdrawn` has no registered source until then. **(10) The connection-to-account binding (Proposed).** No clause says which connection an account stream belongs to, so `AccountSnapshotRecorded` cannot name its `JournaledFact`'s connection. The mapping takes it as an argument from its owner, as `ContextArgs` does. Journaling it, for example as `ConnectionEstablished`'s `account_ref`, needs a clause first. (11) Moved to [DEC-304](decisions/DEC-304.md). | Accepted (agent, DEC-176), item 9 Proposed; item 10 closed by [DEC-800](decisions/DEC-800.md) | The coordinator ruled option (a) on #171 with the conditions above. Every member traces to code or a clause, and items 9 and 10 stay open rather than filled with a guess. The rules only refuse drafts, so they tighten what `append` accepts and change nothing a writer may do (DEC-176 (1) and (2)) | Close every §10 member inline (the ruling chose the smaller option); close `PlatformOperatorAction` with guessed members for three actions; carry the deployment's connection and allocation as members no clause names; bump the vectors to version 4 here (ES-22, as in DEC-177 item 6) |
| DEC-262 | **How E6-12 reads mandate spec §6.2 step 5a, the client ceiling, in `mandate-builder`** ([task brief](tasks/E6-12-client-ceiling.md); stream H, claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125); DEC-185, MI-30). (1) **Who asked is a required fact of the classified action.** `ActionContext` gains `requested_by: RequestedBy` (`agent`, `owner`, `client`) with no default, so every path that builds one names its channel; it is not a §6.3 field and no rule reads it. (2) **`propose` is the agent's own request.** It sizes only from signal-model outputs, which step 5a defines as `agent`, so it stamps `agent`; no owner or client request path calls the builder yet (E10-6, the desk), and each must state the channel's requester when it does. (3) **The ceiling is the last step of `classify`,** after the admission ceiling: the stricter of the result so far and `ask`. Like the admission ceiling (DEC-130 item 13) it names itself, `client_ceiling` (DEC-252's `decided_by`), only when it changed the decision, so an ask the rules or the admission ceiling reached keeps its source and a `deny` keeps its own. This is `reference/mandate/ref.py`'s order and labels. (4) **Exits are never narrowed** (`AGENTS.md` rules 2 and 13): step 3 returns before the ceiling for every reducing purpose, and `decide` classifies a sell by its side, so no requester reaches it. (5) **Delegations are not in the Rust order path yet.** `Autonomy` holds no `delegations` until E8-8, so the tests cover every rule, default, admission setting and requester; because the ceiling is last, E8-8's step 4a lands before it, and E8-8's tests PR extends the client-ceiling sweep and property with delegations. (6) **Not in this task:** stamping `requested_by` from the authenticated channel and journaling it in `DecisionMade` (the runtime does not call the builder yet; DEC-252 has the schema), and the per-client ask budget (MI-33's spec change, DEC-251). E6-12 stays open for both. (7) **No reference case moves.** No mandate case states `requested_by`; the family-A and family-N harness modules pass `agent`, and a case that stated the key would fail as an unread member. Cases for step 5a are a reference-case change of their own | Accepted (agent, DEC-176: each reading only tightens or adds no risk) | The ceiling has to follow every step that can loosen a decision, and only `classify` sees all of them; a required field is the only way to keep a later request path from defaulting to `agent` | Carry `requested_by` as a separate `classify` argument (a proposal would not carry it, against step 5a's "the order builder carries it"); label every client ask `client_ceiling` (hides which rule asked, against §6.4's `decided_by`); wait for E8-8 (leaves an `auto` rule able to run a client's order meanwhile) |
| DEC-263 | **E5-6's crate and the manifest's canonical form** ([journal spec §6.2](../specs/journal.md); claim [#374](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/374); [task brief](tasks/E5-6-journal-cold-store.md)). (1) **A new pure safety-critical crate, `mandate-journal-cold`, layer 3** — the lowest layer above `mandate-journal` (2) and `mandate-canon` (0) — `allowed_external = ["thiserror"]`, with its `xtask/layers.toml` entry, CODEOWNERS line and lint header in the same change (AGENTS.md's new-crate rule; normal changes, not Proposed). Every piece of the slice is deterministic arithmetic over bytes and digests: no object-storage client, no clock, no I/O; the Postgres crate stays the hot store and the cold crate **uses** `mandate-journal`'s `verify_events`, `export_segment`, `Anchor`, `merkle_root` and `tsa_imprint` rather than holding a second copy of any check. (2) **The manifest's six fields, with their JSON names pinned**: `stream`, `first_seq`, `last_seq`, `first_prev_hash`, `last_hash`, `file_sha256`, canonicalized by the journal's own canonical rules — the spec names the fields in prose, and the names are this decision's, pinned by a test that builds the object itself. (3) **The manifest hash is the digest of the manifest's own canonical bytes**, not of the file: the file's SHA-256 is already a field, and the `SegmentExported` reference must change when any field changes, which hashing the manifest gives and hashing the file does not. And a `seq` above the canonical integer bound (2^53 − 1) has no canonical bytes and no hash: **`to_canonical_bytes` and `manifest_hash` are fallible and refuse `SeqUnrepresentable`**, as `AnchorLeaf::leaf_hash` refuses the same `seq` — the manifest's fields are `pub`, so the refusal belongs at the byte boundary, where the unrepresentable `seq` first meets canonical JSON, and an out-of-range manifest is never hashed to a digest two different manifests could share ([#391](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/391) round 1, major 4) (4) **`of` derives and refuses**: one contiguous ascending run of one stream, or a typed error — no rows, a skipped `seq`, or a second stream is never a guess (DEC-85). (5) **`parse` accepts only canonical bytes of exactly the six fields**: wrong order, a seventh member, or a bad digest is `MalformedManifest`, never silently re-canonicalized. (6) **The importer splits, it does not judge**: `import_line` reads `{"body": <canonical>, "hash": "<64 hex>"}` back to the event without re-hashing — whether the hash is true is §11 check 4's question, and an importer that rejected the lie would take the check's job and leave the verifier nothing to verify | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79: a new pure crate over existing types, no spec or schema change; the field names are new surface the founder can rename in review) | The cold store's checks are pure functions, so they belong where a fuzzer can drive them, and the hot store's verify walk is already the right walk — reusing it is what keeps one check from drifting into two | Put the manifest in `mandate-journal` (the hot store would grow a cold wing every cold story touches); put the checks in `mandate-journal-pg` (the store is impure and the checks are not); hash the file instead of the manifest (a field change would leave the `SegmentExported` reference stale); let `parse` re-canonicalize (bytes that are not the manifest would read as the manifest); let `import_line` re-hash (check 4 would have nothing to check) |
| DEC-264 | **The segment checks' order and reachability** ([journal spec §11](../specs/journal.md); claim [#374](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/374)). `verify_range` walks a range's segments in order against a trusted start, and the order is: (1) **the manifest must parse** — bytes that are not a manifest are a `segment_manifest_mismatch` at the `seq` the range expected, because §11's code list is closed and a manifest that is not a manifest fails the only check it can; (2) **the manifest must match its file** — the file's SHA-256 and its edges, recomputed from the file's own lines (the first and last lines' `seq`, `prev_hash` and `hash`), else `segment_manifest_mismatch` at the manifest's `first_seq`; (3) **the segments must chain** — each begins where the previous ended, exactly, in both directions: a later segment that begins *before* the `seq` the range expected is as much a gap as one that begins after it, or two inconsistent cold copies of the same events would both stand unchecked, since the walk starts at the expected `seq` ([#391](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/391) round 1, major 3); and the first carries the trusted start, else `segment_gap` **at the `seq` the range expected**, not the `seq` the segment claims: the failure names where the chain broke, which only the range knows; (4) **the events are walked by `mandate_journal::verify_events`** from the trusted start, whose per-event failures surface unchanged as the cold failure's `Event` arm. **Reachability** (§11's own rule): the first segment may begin before the trusted start — a range enters mid-segment, its head is not checked by that range, and the walk starts at `from_seq`, never at the segment's first event; an empty range returns the trusted start as its own verified state | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 and DEC-176: every reading tightens or resolves a gap by the reading that adds no risk — an event before the trusted start is exactly what the spec says is not checked, and reporting the gap at the expected seq is the more informative reading of a closed code list) | The order puts the cheap byte checks before the walk and the manifest's own identity before its file's, so a forged manifest is caught before its file is read; the reachability rule keeps §11's promise that a range checks its range and nothing else | Report the gap at the offending manifest's `first_seq` (names the newcomer, not the break); refuse a first segment that begins before the trusted start (a range that enters mid-segment would fail a chain it was never asked to verify); walk from the segment's first event (the range would check events §11 says are not its business, and a mid-segment start could never verify) |
| DEC-265 | **The TSA check's structural scope and the canonical export's digest** ([journal spec §10, §11, §12](../specs/journal.md); claim [#374](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/374)). (1) **`tsa_token_invalid`'s structural scope**: the token artifact must contain the anchor's imprint — SHA-256 of the 32 raw root bytes (§10, `tsa_imprint`) — so a token that does not even claim this anchor's root fails; containing the root itself is not containing the imprint. The structural check has its own name, `tsa_imprint_matches`, and the check's entry point `verify_tsa` **fails closed** ([#391](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/391) round 1, blocker 2): the structural check runs first — a token without the imprint is `TsaTokenInvalid` — and a token whose imprint matches is refused as the distinct `TsaVerificationIncomplete`, never `Ok`, because the RFC 3161 signature, the certificate chain, and revocation are not done and an `Ok` would overstate what was proven; when that half lands, the incomplete arm goes and the full verification answers. **Full RFC 3161 signature verification, the certificate chain, and revocation are Proposed**: each needs a crypto dependency the crate does not carry, and a later PR adds the `docs/dependencies.md` row with its reason. The artifact's existence and content hash are the per-event artifact checks' business (§11 checks 6 and 7), not the TSA check's. (2) **The inclusion proof lists sibling subtree roots**, from the leaf's level upward, so a verifier rebuilds §10's tree by its own split rule with the leaf's index: for three leaves `a b c`, `a`'s proof is `[leaf_hash(b), root([c])]` and `c`'s is `[root([a b])]`; a stream the anchor does not cover is the named refusal `StreamNotAnchored`, never a silent `None` (DEC-85), and an anchor whose leaves do not produce its root is the named refusal `MalformedAnchor`, because a proof over such leaves would rebuild nothing. (3) **§12's "the verifier's digest" is one length-prefixed digest over the bundle's parts**: each segment's manifest bytes then file bytes, each anchor's root bytes, each token's bytes, in order, every part prefixed by its byte length as a little-endian `u64` — so no two different part lists can concatenate to the same bytes — and **`verifier_digest` is fallible** (the coordinator's ruling on the implementation PR): a part whose byte length cannot be written as the `u64` prefix is the named refusal `PartUnrepresentable`, so the digest is computed from the parts or refused, and the signature leaves no room for a made-up answer such as a plausible `Digest::ZERO`. (4) **The codes are §11's own words** — `segment_manifest_mismatch`, `segment_gap`, `tsa_token_invalid` — pinned by a live in-module test because the mutation gate runs only live tests while the crate holds pending ones (DEC-253 item 2) | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79; item 1's crypto half is Proposed to the founder, the structural half is the reading that adds a check without a dependency, and the entry point's fail-closed refusal only tightens — an `Ok` that overstates what was proven would loosen it) | The imprint is the one thing a token structurally carries that names this anchor's root, so containment is the strongest check that needs no crypto; the entry point's refusal is the fail-closed reading of a half-implemented check, so no caller can read "verified" out of what was only containment; the length prefixes close the concatenation ambiguity a bare `Digest::of_parts` leaves; the sibling-roots shape is the smallest proof a verifier can rebuild with the leaf's index alone | Check containment of the root bytes (the root is not the imprint — SHA-256 of the root's bytes — and a token holding the root without the imprint claims nothing); answer `Ok` when the imprint matches (fails open: the signature, the chain and revocation are still not checked, so nothing is proven); return the leaf hashes as the "proof" (they rebuild nothing without the siblings' subtree roots); walk a lying anchor's leaves (a proof that rebuilds nothing vouches for anything); digest the parts bare (two different part lists can concatenate identically); skip the live code pin (a code arm no live test reads is a mutant nothing catches, DEC-253 item 2) |
| DEC-266 | **E17-5's input-drift detector: what the detector is, and what this slice may not wire** ([mandate spec §8.4, §6.3, §12 item 2, V-018](../specs/mandate.md); DEC-60, DEC-101; RAID R-05; [task brief](tasks/E17-5-input-drift-detector.md); claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), stream J). The spec makes the detector Must in Phase 1 (DEC-101) but leaves its design an open question (§12 item 2), and §8.4's sentence — "escalates unusual inputs before the agent acts on them" — names a mechanism without a shape. (1) **The detector is deterministic pure code in `mandate-research`:** a per-source fold over typed observations (source, class, timestamp, content hash, byte length — never text, so the R-05 injection surface is unrepresentable in the input type), a baseline of the source's first 32 observations and a recent window of its last 8 (overlap allowed; requiring 40 disjoint observations would read "not yet measurable" as "not unusual", the fail-open direction AGENTS.md rule 3 bars), and six measures in a fixed evaluation order whose first crossing is the reported one: `NoBaseline` (under 32 observations: unusual, fail-safe — an unproven source's inputs escalate until its baseline fills), `DegenerateBaseline` (a baseline whose gaps never span fails closed as unusual — the sixth, DEC-267's round-1 tightening), `DuplicateContent` (one content hash at least 4 of the recent 8), `ArrivalRate` (the recent 8's span under a twelfth of the baseline 32's — the factor compares spans, not rates: 8 arrivals at the baseline's own pace already span 7/31 of the baseline's span, so crossing needs roughly a 2.7× rate, not a 12× flood), `GapCollapse` (the recent window's smallest inter-arrival gap under a quarter of the baseline's mean, 31 gaps), `LengthShift` (the recent mean length beyond 4× or under ¼ of the baseline's). **A refused observation quarantines its source** (round 1's tightening reading): a timestamp before the source's last breaks the history's timing integrity, so both the baseline and the recent window read only the observations after the last refusal and the source is unusual (`NoBaseline`) until a full 32-observation baseline has formed after it — the pre-refusal timing is not trusted, and the source escalates while its baseline is incomplete; the alternative (a refusal count on `SourceDrift`, any refusal in the recent window unusual) was not taken because the quarantine also stops the untrusted timing from feeding any measure. The re-baselining is acceptable (round 2's minor 2): every one of the 32 observations that form the new baseline arrives while the source is escalated — the owner sees each `unusual_input` verdict over them — and re-baselining never makes a source quiet sooner than a fresh source, which needs the same 32. All integer arithmetic over nanoseconds and bytes (ES-21); thresholds are code constants pinned by a live test in the implementation PR — moving them into envelope fields, which AGENTS.md rule 11's "thresholds" arguably reaches, is Proposed below. (2) **Per-source isolation is a property:** the unusual set is computed source by source, so a compromised source escalates only theses that cite it. (3) **The §6.3 fact:** `unusual_input` is true for a proposal exactly when a cited source is unusual — and a cited source never observed is unusual (`NoBaseline`: zero is fewer than 32), the fail-safe reading of an unproven citation, so the fact consults the fold's own history for a citation the report's per-source verdicts do not cover (round 1's blocker); making it rule-usable is V-018's lift, stream F's spec change with the founder, not this slice's. (4) **The escalation seam is Proposed, not taken.** Every wiring changes a founder-owned contract: (a) a new §8.5 reason code (ES-09's stable registry), (b) an escalation field on `ThesisProposed` with admission returning an escalated outcome (journal spec §9), (c) V-018's lift alone, owner rules deciding. **Recommendation: (b)** — "escalates" is an escalation (the owner is asked), not a refusal, and (a) would hand an attacker who reaches an allowlisted source a denial-of-service on every thesis citing it while (c) defaults to allowing unless the owner wrote a rule, too weak for the named top risk. The founder picks in the next spec window; the implementation PR wires the choice. Until then the detector computes and reports, and `admit()` is untouched | The design (items 1–3): Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 — new pure behaviour, no spec, schema, or reason code touched, every reading the fail-safe one). Item 4's seam and the threshold question: Proposed (founder) | §12 item 2 asks for this design and DEC-101 dates it; the shape follows what deterministic code can see of the R-05 threat — traffic shape, not content — and the fail-safe readings follow AGENTS.md rule 3 where the spec is silent. The detector is the one §8.4 defense with no implementation at all, while the allowlist, corroboration, the floor, `max_instruments`, and `autonomy.admission` all shipped with the thin slice | Measure content with a model or heuristic text scan (the crate never calls a model, and a text scanner is itself an injection surface); one shared window across sources (a compromised source would escalate every thesis, a denial of service the isolation property bars); treat a missing baseline as quiet (fail-open); refuse on unusual input as the v1 wiring (stricter than "escalates", and the DoS shape of (a)); wire nothing and leave the fact to rules alone ((c), silent until an owner opts in); floats for the measures (ES-21 bars them, and the spans are nanoseconds) |
| DEC-267 | **E17-5's implementation: the fold's readings, the property suite, and the seam still un-wired** ([mandate spec §8.4, §6.3](../specs/mandate.md); DEC-266; [task brief](tasks/E17-5-input-drift-detector.md); tests [#378](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/378); claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), stream J). The tests PR fixed the surface; this records the mechanics DEC-266 left open, as the implementation reads them. (1) **The order check reads the whole history's last, not the trusted suffix's.** A refusal quarantines the source's windows but not its order: a post-refusal stamp before the pre-refusal last is refused too, and re-quarantines, because the history's monotonicity is one property over everything ever folded, not a property of each baseline. (2) **The quarantine is a boundary, not a truncation.** The history keeps every observation ever folded and the windows read the suffix after the last refusal, so the report stays a function of the fold alone and recovery needs 32 fresh observations whatever the pre-refusal history held; a truncating fold would answer the same report but forget the last instant the order check needs. (3) **The unseen-citation fact is the report's own coverage.** The report holds an entry for every source the fold has seen, quarantined ones included, so a cited source with no verdict was never observed and is unusual (`NoBaseline`: zero is fewer than 32); the fact never consults anything the fold did not see. (4) **The arithmetic.** Spans are `i128` nanoseconds — `UtcNanos`'s 1970-to-9999 bound makes every product the measures take exact, and the saturating operations carry unreachable fallbacks (the `mandate-time` `Date::date` pattern); the length means stay as cross-multiplied `u128` totals, so no division's rounding can move a bar (the premature-division plant floors 4001/4 to 1000 and the just-over boundary case catches it); the gap bar is `GAP_FACTOR × 31 × gap` against the baseline span. The thresholds are DEC-266 item 1's six constants plus the 31-gap divisor, pinned live in-module and from both sides by the boundary cases; the envelope-fields question stays Proposed (DEC-266 item 7, DEC-268). (5) **The property suite lands with the implementation** (the tests PR's Not done), in-module so the mutation gate runs it: per-source isolation under any interleaving, each source's verdict equal to its solo fold's; monotone times fold clean, ties included, and a strictly earlier stamp is refused per source; a refusal quarantines until exactly 32 further observations fold, counted by the property's own oracle; and the §6.3 fact is the disjunction over the citations, with a citation of a source whose stream is empty reading unseen — the property's first oracle missed that empty-stream case and the property caught the oracle, which is the evidence it checks something. (6) **A degenerate baseline fails closed** (round 1's major 2 — a DEC-176 tightening taken in this PR, not deferred to DEC-268: without it both timing measures are unreachable for exactly the fastest flood, the R-05 shape, so the detector would go live fail-open on its named threat the moment the seam is wired). A baseline whose 32 observations all arrived in one instant has a zero span, which no fraction of itself can cross, so it reads unusual with its own measure, `DegenerateBaseline`, evaluated after `NoBaseline` and before the window measures — a degenerate baseline is never exonerated by a quiet window. `a_baseline_that_never_spread_is_unusual_whatever_follows` pins the reviewer's witness (32 distinct inputs at one instant, then an 8-input burst a second apart, which the pre-tightening code read quiet), and the pre-tightening plant fails it. (7) **The escalation seam stays un-wired** (DEC-266 item 4): `admit()` is untouched, the detector computes and reports, and the founder's pick wires it in the change that follows | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 — pure implementation of the recorded design; no spec, schema, reason code, or admission behaviour touched.
