Item 6's tightening: DEC-176, it only fails closed) | Items 1 to 3 and 5 are the fail-safe or structural readings of what DEC-266 already records; item 4 keeps every comparison exact where a division could round; item 6 was disclosed in the first push and tightened in round 1, before any wiring could depend on the fail-open corner | Truncate the history on refusal (loses the order check's last); read the order check against the trusted suffix's last (a stamp between the refused one and the pre-refusal last would fold, trusting timing the quarantine refuses — pinned by `a_stamp_between_the_refused_one_and_the_pre_refusal_last_is_refused_too`, round 1's major 1); defer the zero-span tightening to DEC-268 (rejected in round 1: the detector would go live fail-open on its named threat); wire the seam now (the founder has not picked, and every option changes a founder-owned contract) |
| DEC-269 | **A sale of shares held overnight followed by a same-day purchase of the same security is not a `legacy_pdt` day trade** (trading-domain spec §9.2; E6-6 slice 2, stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123); split out of DEC-259 item 3 by the coordinator's ruling on [#370](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/370)'s round-1 review, minor 2; the founder's ruling relayed by the coordinator on #370, 2026-10-01). §9.2 defines a day trade as "purchasing and selling, or selling and purchasing, the same security on the same day", then says "shares held overnight are sold first" and "each same-day open-then-close counts once". Two readings were put to the founder: **(A)** literal, where a purchase after a same-day sale of overnight shares is one day trade, and **(B)** FINRA Rule 4210(f)(8)(B) as brokers apply it, where a long position held overnight and sold before any new purchase is not a day trade and "selling and purchasing" is a short sale and its cover, which v1 never takes (`AGENTS.md` rule 12). **The founder took (B):** sell, buy counts nothing, and a later sell of the repurchased shares closes a same-day open, so sell, buy, sell is one. This matches the broker's own count. Check 8's `required` keeps its +1 for an opening after a same-day sale of the security, so a repurchase still needs the budget on the day, and its compensation test (`gate::tests::the_day_trade_budget_counts_the_account_and_binds_only_openings`) stays. `daytrades::tests::a_repurchase_after_an_overnight_sale_is_not_a_day_trade` pins 0 and 1. §9.2's wording is clarified to match in trading-domain spec v0.14 (#376, a change of its own since ES-22 keeps specs and code apart); no reference-case outcome changes, since no case sells overnight shares and repurchases them | Accepted (founder, 2026-10-01) | Reading (B) counts fewer day trades than §9.2's literal text, so it was the founder's to take (DEC-79, DEC-176); the #370 review found DEC-259's first draft had taken it as an agent reading | (A) the literal reading (not taken: it counts a day trade no broker counts); counting every sell-then-buy and buy-then-sell alternation, including sales of shares bought today (no reading of §9.2 asks for it) |

The change lands in two backlogged PRs owed by stream H2, in order: the reference and reference-case PR (`ref.py`, MC-R17, and any §3.1 or §5.7 wording, kept apart from crates under ES-22), then the `mandate-spec` implementation with a test that release-then-redeploy on one connection starts at the carried L. Only MC-R17's journal moves; no other reference case's outcome does | Accepted (agent, DEC-176: it only tightens MI-14) | A released agent that left no carry would let a redeploy start at L = 0, which loosens MI-14 | Keep `release` carry-free (loosens MI-14); carry only the released position's unrealized loss (not §5.7's net dollar loss) |
| DEC-271 | **The review date's shape, time base, and place in §6.2** (mandate spec §3, §6.2 step 5b, §6.6, MI-32; E6-14, stream H, claim [#125](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/125); DEC-188). (1) `autonomy.review_by` is a **calendar date**, the last risk day on which any `auto` or delegation stands, read like `goal.end_date`: it passes at 00:00 America/New_York after it, so one date is one instant in both daylight-saving states. (2) Only the **risk clock** judges it, never `event_time` or a client's clock; with no risk clock to judge by, it has passed (rule 3). (3) It is **§6.2 step 5b**, after the client ceiling: the stricter of the result so far and `ask`, so it reaches every `auto` (rules, default, a delegation's, the admission setting's) and lowers no `deny`. It names itself `review_ceiling` only when it changed the decision, as DEC-262 item 3 does for step 5a, so an `ask` keeps its source; a delegation's lift it turned back is not usage. (4) Exits are untouched: step 3 returns before it (rules 2 and 13). (5) `DecisionMade`'s `decided_by` gains `review_ceiling` (journal spec §9), and §9.1 rule 7 refuses it unless `autonomy` is `ask`; that clause's vectors follow in their own code change (#380's rulings). (6) **The Rust side** ([task brief](tasks/E6-14-review-date.md)): the risk day is a required fact of the classified action and of the builder's risk context, set by the caller from the journaled risk clock; a review date off the calendar is a parse error, never "no review date"; the family-A and family-N harness arms, which state no time, refuse a mandate with a review date; and until E6-14's implementation the parse's stub refuses a document that sets one and the autonomy row compares it, so nothing can read a changed date as reducing | Accepted (agent, DEC-176: it only tightens) | A date matches how an owner thinks about a review and reuses the risk day the goal's end date already defines; a ceiling after every loosening step is the one place no `auto` can slip past, which is how DEC-185's ceiling was placed | A UTC instant (the owner's "review by" would end at an odd local hour, and nothing else in the envelope is an instant except delegation windows); suspending delegations under §6.5 condition 5 instead (still leaves rule and default `auto`s, needing a second mechanism); flattening or pausing at the date (DEC-188 rejected it: it adds execution risk and holds exits) |
| DEC-272 | **The review date's validation: optional for now, bounded when set or moved, never removed** (mandate spec §4.1 V-046, §7; E6-14, stream H; DEC-188). (1) The field is **optional** and absent means no review date, as before this change, so no existing reference case or base mandate moves and `mandate-spec`'s strict parse keeps passing; §7's platform default (the validation date + 90 days) means the compiler fills it on every mandate it drafts. (2) **V-046:** a date the version sets or moves is in [validation date, validation date + 180 days]; a date carried unchanged is not checked again, so an owner can tighten a limit past a lapsed date without being made to re-arm autonomy; and a version whose previous version set a date sets one too, so the date can never be dropped. | DEC-273 | **Re-confirming is risk-increasing and carries the delegations over** (mandate spec §9.2, V-042, §6.6; E6-14, stream H; DEC-188). DEC-188 calls re-confirming "a neutral version, step-up". So: (1) a **later** or removed date is **risk-increasing**, which gives DEC-188's step-up, waits for a safe point (§2.2), needs maker-checker where policy requires it, and keeps MI-11; an **earlier** date, or one set where there was none, is risk-reducing. (2) **V-042** classifies a version with the delegations removed **and the new version's review date in both**, so a re-confirmation that changes nothing else carries every delegation over and "re-confirming restores them" (E6-14) holds; one that also adds risk elsewhere carries none | Accepted (agent, DEC-176: stricter than DEC-188's literal "neutral", and the V-042 reading only covers a path that did not exist before) | Classifying it neutral would break MI-11 and skip step-up; classifying it risk-increasing with no V-042 reading would drop every delegation at each re-confirmation, against DEC-188's "restores them" | Neutral with a special step-up rule (a second exception to §2.2 and to MI-11); risk-increasing that drops delegations (re-confirming would punish the owner for reviewing) |
| DEC-274 | **The DEC-270 reference side: how `ref.py` retires a released agent, and the cases that show the carry** (mandate spec §3.1, §5.7, MI-14; E6-4 follow-up, stream H2, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). (1) **The release journals its retirement.** A completed goal whose `on_complete` is `release` journals `GoalCompleted`, then `PositionReleased` with the sub-ledger's quantity, then `AgentStopped` with reason `goal_complete` and `loss_carry_usd` = max(0, N − E), and applies `retired`. (2) **E is read before the release.** The released position is valued at the last mark, as at any retirement: the owner takes the holding, and the agent's loss is what it lost while it ran. Reading E after the position left would count the whole position as a loss. (3) **Only MC-R17 moves**, by that one event (its carry is 0, since it releases at its cost). Regenerating with fixes (b) and (c) of DEC-275 alone leaves the fixture byte-identical. (4) **Three cases show what MC-R17 cannot.** MC-R25 releases at a loss, with the carry, 150, recomputed by `check_cases.py` from the case's own figures. MC-V68 redeploys on the same connection with that carry, computed by the risk state rather than typed, against a 150 floor budget: V-032 refuses it. MC-R26 opens the redeploy at L = 150 and latches the floor at E = 9150, which a fresh connection's floor (9000) would not; the ladder latches a step earlier, since a 10% floor sits past the 8% flatten rung. `generate.py` now asserts that every risk case's mandate passes every V-rule, as the crate's harness requires (a first draft of MC-R26 lowered f below `max_drawdown`, V-014). §11 grows to MC-V01 to MC-V68 and MC-R01 to MC-R26. (5) **Status.** MC-R17 is `pending` in `status.toml` until the `mandate-spec` side lands, the one flip DEC-277 allows (this PR changes MC-R17's fixture entry), and a backlog row tracks its return; and MC-R25, MC-R26, and MC-V68 start unlisted, so pending. MC-R26 and MC-V68 pass on today's code. The four are marked `passing` by a status PR under DEC-77 item 3 once the implementation merges. (6) **A fuzz invariant.** Every retirement, a release included, journals `AgentStopped` once; a redeploy on its carry is refused by V-032 exactly when the carry reaches the floor budget, and otherwise opens with its floor exactly the carry above C × (1 − f), both against the fuzz's own N and E. The fuzz now draws `on_complete` for its crypto mandates, and asserts it exercised a release. `mutants.py` seeds a release with no `AgentStopped` and one with a zero carry; the fuzz catches both | Accepted (agent, DEC-176: it only tightens MI-14) | DEC-270 decided the rule; this records how the reference states it and which cases show it | Value the position after the release (overstates the carry); show the redeploy only in the crate's test (no reference case for V-032 on a released carry) |
| DEC-275 | **Two reference fixes from E6-4's slice R4: the stepped lift and a `profit_stop` goal's completion** (mandate spec §3.1, §5.8; DEC-167 item 8 (b) and (f); stream H2, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). (1) **A re-triggered scale rung goes back in its place.** During the stepped lift after an acknowledgment, `ref.py` removed a rung that triggered again from `reset_queue`, so it could lift only once the others had, and a shallower rung could lift while a deeper one stayed active. (2) **A completed `profit_stop` goal journals its one outcome.** A `goal_complete` input on a `profit_stop` goal (its end date) wrote `on_complete: hold_protected`, which a `profit_stop` goal has no field for. It now writes `GoalCompleted` with `then: discretionary_exit_all_then_retire` and applies `goal_complete`, as the profit stop's own completion and the fold do. (3) **Neither moves a case**: no reference case reaches either path, and regenerating leaves `fixtures/refcases/mandate.json` unchanged. (4) **The fuzz now states both.** A targeted stepped-lift walk (latch, acknowledge, then wander across the scale rungs' trigger and lift levels) asserts that the active scale rungs are always the shallowest, and every run asserts a `profit_stop` goal completes only with its §3.1 outcome. `mutants.py` seeds the old queue removal and the old `hold_protected`; the fuzz catches both | Accepted (agent, DEC-176: both follow the spec where `ref.py` was silent or looser, and move no case) | The fold already reads §5.8 and §3.1 this way (DEC-167 item 8); the reference was the oracle out of step | Lift only the highest active rung while stepping (same outcome, a larger rewrite of the queue) |
| DEC-276 | **A reference PR can move or add a mandate case without turning `main` red: the shared harness reads family sizes from spec §11 and sweeps only the MC-R cases `status.toml` marks passing** (ADR-0001 ES-22, DEC-77 item 1; the DEC-270 follow-ups, stream H2, claim [#124](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/124)). `crates/mandate-refcases/tests/mandate_harness.rs` pinned each owned family's size (S 31, V 67, R 24, ...), their total (202), the expectation counts (178 top-level, 111 in steps), and the MC-R sweep's edit total (2,097), and it required every MC-R case to pass whatever `status.toml` said. (1) **Family sizes come from spec §11**, whose `<prefix>01 to <prefix>NN` ranges the fixture must match; the spec and the fixture move together in one protected PR, so a case the fixture gains or drops that §11 does not list still fails, naming the family. (2) **The MC-R pass-and-edit sweep runs over the cases `status.toml` marks passing**, and checks that its set is exactly those; a pending case is left to `refcases.rs`, which runs it with `--include-ignored`, as DEC-77 item 1 already says. The key, member, and field sweeps still cover every owned case, pending or not. (3) **Totals are recounted from the fixture**: the edit total by its own walk of each swept case's steps (one per member, one per journal-event member, a name set's names plus two), the expectation counts by family and step count. On today's fixture the recounts are exactly 202, (178, 111), and 2,097, the literals they replace. (4) **What it gives up, and what replaces it.** Sweeping every MC-R case whatever `status.toml` said incidentally enforced ES-11's "a passing case never regresses" for family R, and nothing else checked it for any family (the #381 review, round 1). DEC-277's guard in the spec guard now checks it for every family, so this decision weakens nothing | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79: a test-infrastructure change that checks the same properties and adds the sweep's set check; ES-11's guard is DEC-277) | The only order that keeps `main` green under ES-22: the harness first, then the reference PR, then the code, then the status PR | One PR with the fixture and the harness (the spec guard forbids it); bump the literals after the reference PR merges (a red window on `main`); keep MC-R17 unchanged and add the carry only in a new case (DEC-270 moves MC-R17, and the literals still pin the counts) |
| DEC-277 | **A passing reference case may go back to pending only when the same PR changes what it asserts; a guard enforces ES-11** (ADR-0001 ES-11, `crates/mandate-refcases/status.toml`; the #381 review, round 1, major; the coordinator's ruling there, relaying the founder's). ES-11 said a passing case never regresses, and the founder-owned `status.toml` said a passing case never returns to pending, but nothing checked either, and DEC-270 needs MC-R17's expected journal to move ahead of the code that meets it. (1) **The rule (option A).** A reference case may go `passing` → `pending` only when the same PR changes that case's expected output in `fixtures/refcases/`, a spec or reference change to what the case asserts. No other `passing` → `pending` flip, and no `passing` → absent flip, is allowed. The implementation PR that follows must bring the case back to `passing`, and a backlog row names the case and the story until it does. (2) **The guard.** The spec guard (`cargo xtask ci spec-guard`, in `ci fast`) compares `status.toml` at the merge base with the working tree, in every suite: it refuses `passing` → absent always, and `passing` → `pending` unless the case's entry (the object whose `id` is the case, in `fixtures/refcases/<suite>.json`) differs; a case no fixture names by `id` counts as unchanged, so its flip is refused. The lookup matches the `mandate` suite's keys, but not the `journal` keys or `trading_domain`'s compound `RC-NN::sub_case` and version keys, so for now the exception is effectively `mandate`-only there; every flip in those suites is refused, the safe direction, and a backlog row extends the lookup (the #381 review, round 2, minor 1). Two xtask tests over fixture repositories plant each flip: pending with the entry unchanged, pending while only another case's entry changed, absent with the entry changed, and the deleted file, all refused; pending with its own entry changed (in two suites) and pending → passing, allowed. (3) **Where the text changes.** ES-11 in ADR-0001 now states the rule; the `status.toml` header changes in the next reference PR, because the spec guard keeps protected paths and code apart | Accepted (founder, 2026-10-01) | It keeps "a passing case never regresses" for every change except a reviewed one to what the case asserts, and puts it on the Checked rung | No exception (a reference change that moves a passing case could never land without a red `main`); exempt `status.toml` from the spec guard (DEC-77 rejected it) |
| DEC-278 | **E8-3 in the runtime: the readings where DEC-155 to DEC-158 and DEC-257 are silent** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213); M7's runtime implementation PR). (1) **An `ask` for a purpose that adds no risk is proposed as `auto` is.** An exit is never asked (mandate spec §6.2, `AGENTS.md` rule 2) and `mandate_approval::AskablePurpose` has no exit variant, so a classifier that answers `ask` for one cannot hold the reduction (rule 13); before, the runtime asked for it and the approval held nothing. (2) **A request is asked only when every part of it binds:** a non-empty `DecidedBy` label, which the content's `trigger` shows and check 10 compares (DEC-156 item 4); a combined score that is a decimal; and a content object `mandate_approval::content_object` can represent. Otherwise `DecisionMade` records the `ask` and nothing is requested, which is the default the owner would get from silence. The tracer's stage classifier names no label until E7-7 wires the builder's, so its in-module test `ask_journals_the_decision_and_sends_nothing` pins that. (3) **Check 7 runs at its loosest until the overlay is folded (DEC-257 item 12):** the request binds one approver without independence and admission reads `PolicyOverlay::NONE`, which is a loosening against mandate spec §6.4's `approvers` row, not a tightening, and is unreachable today because nothing folds or writes `PolicyChanged` (the #395 review, minor 1). The content's `risk_impact` is empty until the gate exposes the §6.3 figures, `action.order_usd` standing, and its `evidence` cites each unexpired `ModelOutputRecorded` on the instrument, labelled "Output of software you selected". (4) **The deadline is the risk clock plus the view's `autonomy.approval.timeout_s`**, which retires the 300 s constant the runtime used before `MandateView` carried it. (5) **A cancellation's reason is the brief's:** `version_applied` and `mode_tightened`, beside `owner_pause`, `owner_stop`, and `kill_switch`; the placeholder `rebound` is gone. (6) **What cannot be read refuses rather than acts:** an answer to an id that is not an `ApprovalRequested` ULID, or to none the fold holds, is copied as `refused`, `not_pending`; a stated hash that is not a content hash is `content_mismatch` at check 5; step-up evidence with a missing member or a method v0 does not know is no evidence; an unknown `command` or `verdict` refuses the step (DEC-85); and a `backtest` connection reads as `live`, so `cli_confirm` is refused there (DEC-155 item 4). (7) **An assertion is used once any other control-stream event carried it first**, an `ApprovalResponseSubmitted` or an `OwnerCommandIssued`, in the control stream's fold order; a re-tail of the first carrier does not reuse it. (8) **A control event for another agent is inert here**; a kill switch is judged by its scope and subject, as every kill switch is (trading-domain spec §5.5). (9) **An owner exit is handed whatever the mode**, because it is the owner's instruction and only reduces risk; `paused` and `stopped` may hold an exit (rule 13) but nothing requires it. A restart hands again every owner exit no `IntentReceived` shows the executor took, in every mode a flatten is re-handed in, with only that instrument's working orders (DEC-257 item 7). (10) **Causation:** `ApprovalDelivered` names its request and `ApprovalRevalidated` its `ApprovalResponded`, beside journal spec §9.1's `IntentProposed` after `act`; every copy of an owner command names its `OwnerCommandIssued` (rule 16). (11) **`FOLD_VERSION` stays 1 in this PR**: `tests/hand.rs` pins it and DEC-77 lets this PR change `tests/` only by deleting `#[ignore]` lines, and the golden journal's expected output does not move (a `MarkUpdated` now also folds a mark, which the pinned output does not read). The bump with a golden journal that holds approval events is a backlog tests correction. (12) **A refused owner resume, Stop, or acknowledgment is not journaled** until the catalogue has an event for it: the command stays on the control stream and the agent stream records nothing, a known gap against the brief's "refused and journaled" (the #395 review, major 1). The M7 spec PR adds `OwnerCommandRefused`, and the next M7 implementation PR journals it. (13) **Not in this PR, as backlog rows:** DEC-156 item 5's ask budget and suppressions with `DecisionMade.ask_suppressed`, which only remove asks; and the `mandate-journal` schemas DEC-257 item 17 names, without which a real journal refuses the new events, so the runtime's code is complete in the core while the shell still cannot append them | Accepted (agent, DEC-79, DEC-176: each reading only skips, refuses, or keeps a reduction moving, and none weakens a safety invariant) | The pending tests fix the outcomes; these readings fill the gaps between them, each toward nothing happening or a reduction proceeding | Ask for an exit (an approval that could hold nothing and that rule 2 says is never needed); bind a placeholder `DecidedBy` label (check 10 would compare it with itself and pass); judge reuse against every assertion including the event's own (a re-tail would refuse itself) |
| DEC-279 | **E8-3 in the CLI: the readings where DEC-155, DEC-158 and DEC-257 items 13 to 17 are silent** ([task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md), claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213); M7's CLI implementation PR). (1) **An owner command's code is the first eight hex digits of the SHA-256 of a canonical object** naming what it confirms (`resume`, `stop`, `owner_exit`, `acknowledge`), the agent, the control stream's head as decimal text, and the instrument, the confirmed bid, bid size and floor, and the acknowledged event, each `null` where the command has none; the same shape as `mandate_approval::kill_switch_code`, which a kill switch's code is, so no code confirms another command, another bid, or a later head (DEC-257 item 13). (2) **`show` refuses a request whose stated hash is not the hash of its content object** as `content_mismatch`, so the code printed is always the hash of what the owner reads (P8, EI-14). (3) **`approve` checks in the runtime's order:** `not_pending`, then `late` (the owner's second at or after the deadline), then the code as `content_mismatch`; `skip` checks the first two. (4) **The event id and the step-up assertion are minted only after every local check passes**, so a refused command spends neither, and each gesture draws one fresh assertion. (5) **The append loop:** every attempt takes a new writer epoch, reads the head afresh, and appends the same bytes; `Fenced`, `HeadMismatch`, `Ambiguous` and `Unavailable` are retried, at most eight attempts, and `IdempotencyConflict` and `Invalid` are reported. A report after the last attempt says the command was committed at most once, which every attempt carrying one id guarantees (DEC-257 item 16). (6) **A kill switch of a connection or a workspace carries `agent: null`**, and a workspace's `subject` is the owner's workspace id, which the runtime compares (trading-domain spec §5.5). (7) **`OwnerAcknowledged` carries `agent`, `event`, `risk_clock` and `submitted_at` (both the owner's second), `step_up` and `user`**, until journal spec §9 closes it. (8) **The inbox:** an admitted grant not yet re-validated, a `counted` grant, and a `refused` answer all leave a request pending; resolved requests list after the pending ones, each group by deadline, with `remaining_s` 0. (9) **The draft's envelope** is written as `actor.kind: user` with the owner's opaque id, `clock_source: local`, and the owner's instant as `event_time`. (10) **Not in this PR, as backlog rows:** the commands' `clap` wiring and a `mandate-journal-pg` `ControlJournal`, because `mandate-journal` registers no schema for these events (DEC-257 item 17), so a real journal would refuse every command they could send; and Stop's `--release` choice and warning, which `Command::Stop` cannot express yet | Accepted (agent, DEC-79, DEC-176: each reading only refuses locally or binds a code more tightly, and the runtime judges every committed event again) | The tests pin the bindings and the outcomes; these readings place the derivation and the order of the local checks | A code without the head (a code typed before another command lands would still confirm); minting the id per attempt (a lost answer would commit twice) |
| DEC-280 | **The MC-E reference cases: their shape and the readings their generation needed** (mandate spec §6.1, §6.4, §11; [task brief](tasks/M7-escalation-v0.md) "Reference cases harnessed"; DEC-173 item 1; claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)). (1) **One kind, `escalation`, with an `op`:** `lifecycle` scripts the runtime's steps from `start` under a `context` and expects each input's drafts; `ask_permit` judges queries against a journaled ask ledger; `deliver_now` judges a channel under quiet hours. A kind of its own keeps every other family's count and arm untouched (the #343 review). (2) **A `fold` input** folds one event and steps nothing, which is how the folded clock reaches a deadline without a tick expiring the approval, so MC-E05 shows lateness by the clock alone (DEC-156 item 1). (3) **Every expectation is the reference model's own output** (`escalation_step`, `ask_permit`, `deliver_now`), and `check_cases.py` asserts each title's claim from the drafts, never by calling the model again. (4) **`ask_permit` reads its whole ledger**, so a budget case cannot query a moment before one of its entries: MC-E25 pairs the eleventh ask with the next risk day instead. (5) **MC-E26's DST case** puts ten asks at 23:00 New York on 31 October 2026 and queries one second before and at 00:00 New York on 1 November, both on one UTC day, so a budget reset at UTC midnight fails it. (6) **No rule changes**, so no existing case moves; the cases have no `status.toml` row and are pending until a harness reads them (DEC-77 item 1). (7) **`OwnerCommandRefused` records a refused resume or Stop on the agent stream, and a refused acknowledgment on the account stream** (journal spec §9, mandate spec §6.1), naming the control-stream event as `causation_id`, with the command, the reason, and the effective time: mandate spec §6.1 already said such a refusal is journaled and no event carried it (the coordinator's ruling on the #395 review, major 1). An owner exit and a kill switch are never refused as commands, so they need none. It only adds a record and changes no outcome | Accepted (agent, DEC-79, DEC-176: cases that only restate the accepted §6.4 rules) | The cases make the spec's escalation rules checkable by any implementation without changing them | Three kinds, one per `op` (three new arms for one family); a tick that does not expire (a step the spec does not have) |
| DEC-287 | **E5-6a's property suite: the oracle and the generators** ([journal spec §6.2, §10, §11, §12](../specs/journal.md); claim [#374](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/374); [task brief](tasks/E5-6a-cold-store-properties.md); the property-based invariant tests #404's review owed, backlog row E5-6a, the coordinator's ruling). (1) **The oracle builds everything the crate reads and computes every expectation from the scenario it built**, never from the crate's walk (AGENTS.md, independent oracles): the parts from `to_canonical` of its own six-field object and `export_segment` of the rows; the verified state by advancing the trusted start over the walked rows; the failing check and its `seq` by DEC-264's order over the tamper it knows. (2) **A scenario is a random run** of one stream (1 to 12 rows, real journal hashes), **cut into segments at random boundaries**, entered at the genesis, inside the first segment, or exactly at any later segment's first seq with the earlier segments omitted — a range may be entered at any supplied segment's first seq, not only at the genesis or inside the first segment (round 2's major: the boundary entry is the natural case of reading a range that starts partway through the cold store, and the one shape in which a stale copy ending before the entry is refused at the start itself) — with `prev_hash` the hash of the event before `from_seq`. (3) **Every tamper names its owner**: a flipped file byte or a lying manifest field fails `segment_manifest_mismatch` at the manifest's own claimed `first_seq`; bytes that are not a manifest — or the six fields serialized in a non-canonical member order, which only `parse`'s canonical-bytes guard (DEC-263 item 5) can refuse — fail it at the seq the range expected; a dropped segment or a stale overlapping copy — built from the run's own first rows, so manifest and file agree with each other — fails `segment_gap` at the expected seq, and the copy may also be prepended in front of the trusted start, where one ending before the entry is refused at the start itself, by the first segment's upper carries bound alone: the copy is walked as empty and the run's own first supplied segment then begins exactly at the entry, so nothing else reports the gap (round 2's plants: deleting `expected <= manifest.last_seq`, or the oracle's matching `last < start` half, each fails the failure property); a row whose hash is a lie, with the parts rebuilt from the lied row so every segment check passes, fails the per-event check 4 and surfaces as the `Event` arm unchanged; and the tamper's indices are drawn against the very scenario they corrupt, so the failure property's expectation is always an `Err` while the untampered property pins the `Ok` direction — together, no constant answer passes (#407 round 1's majors: the canonical-form and per-event tamples close the two gaps its review found). (4) **Reachability as a property**: the first two rows' hashes may be swapped and a range entered at 3 or later still verifies, the parts rebuilt from the swapped rows so the export stays self-consistent. (5) **The token's two answers as a property**: `verify_tsa` is never `Ok` — `TsaVerificationIncomplete` exactly when the token contains the imprint, by the suite's own containment search, and `TsaTokenInvalid` otherwise. (5a) **The proof's root as a property** (#407 round 1's minor 1): an anchor's inclusion proof, folded with the oracle's own §10 walk — the leaf's side at each level read from the split rule root to leaf, then the siblings combined leaf-upward on the far side — rebuilds the anchor's root, so a proof whose siblings are swapped or wrong-length fails it. (5b) **The oracle's chain rule is derived from DEC-264's wording, not from the implementation's expression** (#407 round 1's minor 2): "each begins where the previous ended, exactly, in both directions", and the first "carries the trusted start" — read as the start's `from_seq` not sitting outside the first segment's span. (6) **The properties are live, not pending** (the coordinator's ruling): the implementation is #404's and merged, so DEC-77's stage-1 markers have nothing to pin — the PR is tests only, and the do-nothing check carries the vacuity burden instead: with every entry point's body replaced by a refusal or a constant, the codes and the accessor included, 0 of the 7 properties pass for all 37 flavours; the one test an identity `verify_range` answers is `cold::an_empty_range_returns_the_trusted_start` (#391's test of the degenerate case, whose subject is exactly that answer), so the exact claim over the 39 is 0 of 39 passing for every refusal flavour and 1 of 39 — that test alone — for the identity flavour: no test this PR adds passes under any flavour (round 2's minor 2) | Accepted (agent, DEC-79: a tests-only PR over a merged implementation; each reading checks an invariant DEC-263 to DEC-265 already pin, and the oracle's independence is the only new surface) | The invariants AGENTS.md asks for are the hand cases' own, generalized: the oracle is the accumulator the spec implies and the generator is the adversary the review planted by hand | Compute the expectation from the crate's answer (the oracle would agree with any bug); offer tamples whose outcome the oracle cannot decide (the failure property's expectation would sometimes be `Ok`); mark the properties pending (nothing is stubbed; the do-nothing check, not the pending gate, carries vacuity) |

| DEC-281 | **E17-8's forward-paper evaluator: the pure scoring half's shape** (mandate spec §8.6, §8.2; DEC-99, DEC-122, DEC-111, DEC-103; [task brief](tasks/E17-8-forward-paper-evaluation.md); claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), stream J). DEC-99 fixes the evidence rule and DEC-122 the window, the metric, and the threshold; this records the mechanics. (1) **The evaluator is deterministic pure code in `mandate-research`'s `score` module** — a closed thesis, a close series, the pre-registered decision, and a modeled round-trip cost in; a scorecard out — and it **scores ideas, not executions**: the input carries no admission outcome and no predecessor's score, so a refused thesis and an admitted one score the same (DEC-99's "every thesis") and a revision starts from zero by construction (MI-18), its row showing the lineage's revision count. (2) **The pre-registered decision is a typed input**: the calendar window a scored horizon must close inside, the minimum count of *scoreable* closed theses, and the bound's `z` as an exact decimal (1.645 for DEC-122's 95%). The evaluator refuses an early run (`WindowNotClosed`) and refuses a thesis whose horizon closes outside the registered window (`ThesisOutsideWindow`, the edges inclusive: a horizon closing exactly at `from` or `to` is inside): the caller selects, the evaluator re-checks, nothing is silently included or dropped — a report before the window closes would be the choose-after-the-fact that pre-registration exists to prevent. The registered figures themselves — DEC-122's three months and 100 closed theses — are the **shell's** to confirm when the decision is recorded: the evaluator applies the minimum as registered, so guarding DEC-122's own figures happens at registration, not in the scoring (round 1). (3) **The boundary rule is no-lookahead at both edges**: the entry price is the first close *strictly after* `as_of` (a close at the same instant is the model's own, not a price it could act on); the exit is the last close *at or before* the horizon end. An edge with no close on its side makes the thesis **unscoreable** — excluded, named with its reason, never counting toward the mean or the minimum, so a data gap can never help an evaluation pass. (4) **The per-thesis score is its direction's window return net of the modeled round-trip cost** — `(exit − entry) ÷ entry` at 12 places half-even (DEC-127 item 4's scale), the cost an exact fraction the caller computes from the same §6.4 fee model the backtest charges, so the evaluator never duplicates the schedule; `Direction::Other` is unscoreable (DEC-32's ignored output, carried into scoring). (5) **Excess is against each baseline over the same window**: the basket baseline is the equal-weighted mean of the members' own window returns, the index baseline the ETF's own. (6) **The margin is `z × σ ÷ √n`, exact where the arithmetic allows and never understated**: `root_ceiling(squared_quotient(z × σ, n))`, with the product exact through one `mandate-num` addition (`Ratio::checked_mul`, the shared-crate pattern of #136 and #175, live with live tests); with fewer than two theses the variance is absent and the margin is zero, the bound the mean itself. **Pass is the bound above zero against both baselines** (DEC-122's "against each"). (7) **The scorecard is the report and recomputes from its own fields** (the E4-2 discipline, DEC-127 item 5's precedent that the report carries what a reader needs to recompute it — round 1: the report echoes the decision's `z` and each row's round-trip cost), rows ordered by thesis id, pure inputs giving an identical scorecard — reproducible from the journal alone. The live half — the internal paper workspaces over DEC-122's window — is the founder's go, as E17-0's is | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 — new pure behaviour, no spec, schema, or reason-code registry outside `ResearchError`'s add-only codes, no trading-path change) | §8.6 already binds scoring to the forward-paper evaluator and DEC-122 to the window, metric, and threshold; the fail-safe readings (unscoreable never helps pass, refuse the early run, the ceilinged margin) follow AGENTS.md rule 3 where the mechanics were open | Score realized executions (the evaluator would measure the executor, not the ideas, and a refused thesis would vanish from the evidence); the entry at the last close at or before `as_of` (lookahead — the model may have been trained on it, R-27's whole point); the cost inside the evaluator (duplicates the §6.4 schedule the backtest already owns); a `z` constant in code (the confidence level is the founder's to pre-register); report early with a caveat (the pre-registration exists to prevent exactly that); float statistics (ES-21, and the figures must recompute exactly) |
| DEC-284 | **How the trading-domain harness reads §9.2's regime and its figures** (E6-6 slice 2's harness; stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123); DEC-199, DEC-259; trading-domain spec §7.2, §9.2). (1) **The regime** is `initial.account.regime`. The alpaca profile's margin account defaults to `intraday_margin`, as the case file's header and §7.2 say. A generic margin account must state one, and a proposal in one that does not is refused, never given a regime. A cash account states none, and a stated one is refused; it is handed `legacy_pdt`, which check 8 never reads for a cash account (as before). (2) **`last_equity`** is `AccountSnapshot::prior_close_equity`, the figure check 8 compares with the threshold. With none stated it is the account's equity, as DEC-199 had it. (3) **`multiplier`** must be 1 (§7.2's 1× requirement). Any other value is refused when the case is read, since §7.2 pauses the agents then and the harness does not model a pause. (4) **`prior_day_trades`** are `DayTradeInput::earlier`. A `broker_account_update` that changes the regime or any of these figures stays refused, naming E6-6, as `an_account_update_refuses_what_it_does_not_read` pins; no case sends one, and the backlog row on the real order path carries it. (5) **The fold.** A `legacy_pdt` margin account's ledger, and every `day_trade_count`, is `mandate_risk::fold_day_trades` at the step's instant over every `fill` step so far, with the pattern-day-trader flag false (no case states one). The shares held at the start of today are each US-equity position less today's fills in it. An equity fill on an earlier trading day is refused, because its day trades would need that day's fold, which the harness does not run; that refusal never yields a smaller count. An `intraday_margin` or cash proposal is handed an empty ledger, since check 8 never reads one for it. (6) **The static note.** While another pending item keeps a case from running, `a second propose_order step in one case` is listed only when a proposal follows one that the `fill` steps between them, on its instrument and side, do not fill in full. Otherwise the run decides, and a refusal under DEC-259 item 7 reads the same text, with no step label. That keeps the pinned pending reasons of RC-15, RC-17, RC-22 and RC-25 byte-identical | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79: readings of the reference harness only, no gate, spec or schema change; each refuses what it does not read rather than guessing, DEC-176) | RC-09 and RC-09B state these members and the harness refused them until E6-6 (DEC-199); each reading is the case file's header or §7.2's own text, and every gap is a refusal rather than a figure | Fold every earlier day in a chain (more harness for no case that needs it); treat an unstated generic regime as `legacy_pdt` (stricter, but the case file says a generic account must state it); read `multiplier` as a cap on buying power (§7.2 pauses instead) |
| DEC-285 | **How the reference harnesses read a crypto pair** (E6-10's harness; stream G, claim [#123](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/123); DEC-254 item 7, DEC-199 item 6, DEC-85; trading-domain spec §2.3, §3.2 item 7, §5.3). (1) **The quote currency is the instrument's `symbol`, written `BASE/QUOTE`** (§2.3), in both harnesses that build a crypto listing: the trading-domain gate driver and family B's `listing`. `USD` is `QuoteCurrency::Usd`. Any other quote, a stablecoin or a lower-case `usd` included, is `Other`. A symbol without exactly one `/` between two non-empty parts, `BTCUSD` among them, states none. The gate never reads `Other` or none as USD (DEC-254 item 1), so the reading can only deny. (2) **The trading-domain crypto listing** has no exchange, which check 2 does not read for crypto. Each instrument may state only its own class's members (DEC-85): a pair that states an `exchange` or `fractionable` is refused, and so is an equity that states a `symbol` or `min_trade_increment`, since the driver gives an equity a one-share minimum of its own. Its 30-day median is the same 90,000,000 the equity listing has as its 20-day one, because the case file's header says absent crypto volume fields pass. Its minimum order is `min_trade_increment`, or the smallest `Qty` when the case states none, so the harness never refuses a quantity the case sizes to. (3) **A crypto proposal with no `tif` is `gtc`**, the simple crypto limit order of §5.1's table; `day` is for equities. Nothing in the gate reads a crypto order's `tif` today, so this reading cannot be observed yet; a backlog row adds its test when E6-8 or the executor first reads it. (4) **A crypto buy reserves no cash for its fee**, because §6.4 takes a crypto buy's fee from the asset it receives. The harness still fills the proposal on a copy of the account, as a taker, because the accounting needs a liquidity for a crypto fill, and the cash fee it finds is zero. (5) **`crypto_status` stays unread.** No crypto-proposing case states it, the header defaults it to `ACTIVE`, and `trading_domain_gate_harness.rs` pins it as pending E6-10, initial and in an update, so reading it needs a tests correction first (DEC-77). The driver hands the gate a crypto-active account. (6) **What it earns:** MC-B26 to MC-B28 (family B) and RC-09. | DEC-282 | **E17-8's forward-paper evaluator: the implementation's readings** (mandate spec §8.6; DEC-281; tests [#405](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/405); [task brief](tasks/E17-8-forward-paper-evaluation.md); claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), stream J). The tests PR froze the surface; this records the mechanics DEC-281 left open, as the implementation reads them. (1) **The window return rides the E4-2 arithmetic**: a unit share's value at each boundary price through `SignedQty::value_at` and `Usd::ratio_to` at 12 places half-even, so no new `Price` operation exists here to drift from the report's own. (2) **The report's ordering is the report's**: the loop walks the input order and both lists — the scored rows and the unscoreable — sort by thesis id at the end, so no caller's ordering reaches the report. (3) **Refusal precedence is input integrity first**: every thesis's horizon is validated (`ThesisOutsideWindow`) before any scoring and before the `WindowNotClosed` count, and each unscoreable thesis's reason is the first failing one in the order the input presents it — the direction (part of the thesis value itself), then the series (the instruments map), then the entry and exit edges (the series) — pinned live by `the_first_failing_unscoreable_reason_is_reported` (round 1 added `a_thesis_outside_the_window_is_refused_before_the_minimum`, the `ThesisOutsideWindow`-before-the-count half with both refusals live; under item 8's entry bound the edge order is the exit before the entry). (4) **Pass is strictly above zero**: a bound of exactly zero is not a pass (DEC-122's "above zero"), pinned live by `a_bound_of_exactly_zero_does_not_pass`. (5) **The zero-dispersion arm states zero, not `z × 0`**: below two theses the variance is absent and the margin is `Ratio::ZERO`, so a single thesis's bound is its own mean whatever `z` was registered. (6) **The aggregate rides `Ratio`'s Decimal-backed statistics, whose 96-bit mantissa bounds the sums of squares**: an evaluation whose excesses are so large — a window return far past ±100% — that their squares leave the bound refuses with `Num(Overflow)` rather than rounding, the fail-loud reading (the same refusal answers a thesis count above the u32 the decision's minimum carries). (7) **The property suite** holds the report's identities over generated scenarios — a first thesis that always scores, up to five more that may be non-Long or lack their series, one to three basket members, an index, and a `z` from the registrable range, with every window return within ±100% (the realistic domain, and the suite's own bound): the counts agree, both lists are ordered by thesis id, every aggregate figure recomputes from the report's own fields, each row's net return recomputes from its own entry, exit, and echoed cost, the same inputs give an identical scorecard, and neither the theses' nor the basket's input order changes it. (8) **The entry close rides inside the thesis's own window** (#410 round 1's blocker, the coordinator's ruling): the scoring path's entry is the first close strictly after `as_of` **and at or before `horizon_end`**, so a close past the horizon is never read at the entry either — the unbounded entry DEC-281 item 3's frozen signature left open scored a thesis from a close past its horizon against a close before its `as_of`, a backwards window. A thesis whose closes all lie outside its window, and the degenerate window whose `horizon_end` closes at or before its own `as_of` (whose entry interval is empty), are unscoreable with `NoEntryClose`: excluded, named, never counted toward the minimum, never a figure. The bound lives in the scoring path (`buy_and_hold` and `evaluate`), not in `entry_close`'s frozen signature, and it amends item 3's edge order: **the exit edge is read before the entry edge**, so a thesis whose closes all lie past its horizon keeps the frozen case's `NoExitClose` (its exit edge has no price) while a thesis missing the window on the entry's side — before `as_of`, around it, or a degenerate horizon — is `NoEntryClose`; a close exactly at `horizon_end` is inside (the entry when it is the first close after `as_of`, and the exit besides), pinned live by `a_thesis_whose_closes_all_lie_outside_its_window_is_unscoreable`, `a_degenerate_window_is_unscoreable_never_a_figure`, and `a_close_exactly_at_the_horizon_is_the_entry_and_the_exit`. (9) **An empty scoreable set against a registered minimum of zero refuses fail-loud, nameless** (#410 round 1, minor 3): `count < minimum_scoreable` is false at zero, so the aggregate reaches `Ratio::mean` of an empty set and the run refuses with `Num(DivisionByZero)` — loud, so it adds no risk, but the code names nothing a caller could read; a named refusal is a backlog row and the frozen surface stays | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 — the readings implement DEC-281's frozen surface; no spec, schema, or registry change; the live tests are the tests PR's own, their `#[ignore]` lines deleted alone; items 8 and 9 are #410 round 1's, under DEC-176 — the entry bound only tightens DEC-281 item 3, and the nameless refusal fails loud) | The tests PR's 24 cases and 23 planted bugs already pin the boundary rules, the aggregate, and the exclusions; what they left open was precedence, strictness, the zero-dispersion arm, and the arithmetic's bounds, each of which a live pin or the property suite now answers; round 1 closed the one boundary the frozen cases left unpinned — the entry's own upper edge — with a bound that only excludes | Sorting before the loop (the caller's order would reach the report through the unscoreable list's construction); `z × 0` as the single-thesis margin (a rounding where an exact zero is statable); pass at zero (DEC-122 says above); rounding the sums of squares into the bound (a quiet wrong figure where a refusal is honest); a new `Price` ratio operation (duplicating the report arithmetic the crate already rides); the unbounded entry (a close past the horizon read as the entry price, scoring a backwards window against a close before `as_of` — the #410 round-1 blocker); the degenerate horizon scored as a figure (an inverted window's −0.5); naming the empty-set refusal in this PR (the frozen surface stays; the backlog row owes the name) |
| DEC-290 | **The #397 review's CLI follow-ups: closed payloads, Stop's release, `status`'s restrictions, derived event ids, and backoff** (the coordinator's ruling on [#397](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/pull/397); the M7 brief's "The CLI control surface"; journal spec §9; DEC-136, DEC-155, DEC-279; claim [#213](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/213)). (1) **Closed payloads until the catalogue closes them:** `OwnerCommandIssued` carries exactly `agent`, `command`, `scope`, `subject`, `release`, `warning_shown`, `bid`, `bid_size`, `floor`, `submitted_at`, `step_up` and `user`; `ApprovalResponseSubmitted` exactly `agent`, `approval`, `verdict`, `content_hash`, `submitted_at`, `step_up`, `responder` and `role`; `OwnerAcknowledged` DEC-279 item 7's six; non-null step-up evidence exactly `assertion_id`, `authenticated_at` and `method`. (2) **Stop's release choice (DEC-136):** `release` is `true` or `false` on a Stop and `null` on every other command; `warning_shown` is `sha256:` and the hex digest of the exact warning text the CLI shows (`RELEASE_WARNING`) on a Stop with release, and `null` otherwise, so the journal names which warning the owner saw. A Stop's code binds the release choice. The flat-or-release precondition stays the runtime's. (3) **`status`'s restrictions** are read from the agent's account stream (`acct:{workspace}:{account}`, the account given to `status`): each agent restriction with the mode its latest `AgentModeApplied` naming it records, gone once that mode is `normal`, and each instrument restriction whose latest `InstrumentRestrictionChanged` is active; agent restrictions first, each group in name order. (4) **The event id is derived, not minted:** the first 128 bits of the SHA-256 of a canonical object naming the control stream, the event type, the owner's choice (the payload less `submitted_at`, `risk_clock` and `step_up`), whether the command is stepped up, and the head it was decided at, written as a ULID (journal spec §3: its time component carries no meaning). The same choice confirmed the same way at the same head is one event across retries and invocations. (5) **A re-run finds what it committed:** when the control stream's last event is the same command, decided at any head before that event, and no agent-stream event names it as `causation_id` yet, the command reports that event and commits nothing, spending no step-up. Any earlier head counts, because another writer's event that landed between the command's read of the head and its append leaves the command last but decided further back. Only the last event is read, so a command repeated after any other is committed again; a code that confirms at the head now is a new gesture, never a re-run; and once the runtime has recorded the earlier event (for example a refused answer), the same command is committed again. **A kill switch of any scope is always committed** and never reported as an earlier one (rule 13; under rule 3, the reading that adds no risk; the #409 review, minor 5). An acknowledgment has no agent-stream record the CLI can read, so the same acknowledgment again right after it reports the first. (6) **Backoff:** each retry waits 100 ms doubling to at most 2 s, plus a jitter below that base drawn from the event id, so racing invocations stop fencing each other; eight attempts at most. The report after the last names the event id, which a re-run finds if nothing else has been committed since. (7) **A refusal's message** says whether the deadline has passed at the owner's clock: before it, that the owner may answer again; at or after it, that the deadline has passed and the action is skipped; `Outcome::Refused` carries the request's deadline for this | Accepted (agent, DEC-79, DEC-176: each item only closes a payload, binds a code more tightly, or commits less often, and the runtime judges every committed event again) | The #397 review found a `typed_code` member passes every test, Stop's release absent, `status` without restrictions, at-most-once only per invocation with no backoff, and a refusal message offering another answer after the deadline | Minting the id per invocation and recording why cross-invocation idempotency cannot hold (the owner's re-run after an exhausted report would commit twice); scanning the whole control stream for the same command (a pause after a resume would be swallowed); fixed delays without jitter |

(1) **Members until journal spec §9.1 closes the schema:** exactly `command` (`resume` or `stop`), `reason` (`step_up_missing`, `step_up_stale`, `step_up_reused`, `step_up_method`), and `effective_at`, the effective time the command was judged at: the later of its `submitted_at` and the folded clock, the same second `ApprovalResponded`'s `effective_at` names. `causation_id` is the `OwnerCommandIssued`. (2) **It is the command's one copy:** the step that refuses a resume or Stop writes it and nothing else, and the fold reads it as a copy, so a re-tailed command after a restart writes nothing (EI-3). (3) **The catalogue entry** admits it on the account and agent streams with no required `config_refs`, as journal spec §9's two rows give it (—). The slice that adds the copy also writes `OwnerCommandRefused` for an acknowledgment whose step-up does not count, tests first, with a test that a refused acknowledgment leaves exactly that account-stream event and lifts no restriction | Accepted (agent, DEC-79, DEC-176: it records a refusal the spec already requires and changes no outcome; the executor's half is sequenced, not dropped) | The #395 review found that a refused resume, Stop or acknowledgment journals nothing; the M7 spec PR (#401) added the event | Journaling the refusal as an `ApprovalResponded`-like record on the control stream (a second writer of that stream); writing the executor's half now against a copy that does not exist yet |

| DEC-293 | **E17-6's correlated-flow controls: the pure monitoring and halt half's shape** (mandate spec §2.3, §8.4 "Correlated flow", §8.5 check 9; journal spec §9's `UniverseChanged` and `PlatformOperatorAction` rows; DEC-100, DEC-123, DEC-05, DEC-09, DEC-67; [task brief](tasks/E17-6-operator-flow-monitor.md); claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), stream J). The admission half is already merged (check 9, MC-N10); this records the other two controls. (1) **Both halves are deterministic pure code in `mandate-research`'s new `flow` module** — the operator *service* DEC-100 places "in the workspace deployment, outside the trade path" is wiring outside the crate, and "writes to no workspace, and sends nothing to the global control plane" is the function's signature: no entry point accepts a journal, stream, or workspace handle to write through, so the boundary is representable rather than promised. The monitor never halts by itself (DEC-123): its output is a report value nothing consumes. (2) **The monitor sums per agent, grouped by workspace**: `AgentFlow { workspace, pinned, exposure }`, one agent's research exposure per instrument in dollars — for a dynamic mandate every working-universe instrument is research-admitted, so the agent's exposure over its universe is its research flow, valued by the caller (facts produced elsewhere, the crate's house pattern). **A pinned agent contributes nothing** — bring-your-own-strategy keeps per-account controls only — and an instrument only pinned agents hold has no row at all. (3) **DEC-123's threshold, strictly above**: the lower of 1% of the instrument's 20-day average daily dollar volume and 1,000,000 USD per instrument per deployment; at the bar quiet, one cent above alerting, both arms and the `min` pinned by cases that discriminate against `>=` and against `max`. (4) **The share is reported** (one rounding of one quotient at 12 places), and **a missing ADV is undecidable, not quiet**: the row alerts with the share `None`, even below the dollar arm, because the 1% arm may bind lower (AGENTS.md rule 3); the rest of the report lives on, because one missing figure blinding the monitor to every other instrument is its own failure. A zero ADV decides — the 1% arm is zero, so any exposure alerts — with the share still `None`, because nothing divides by zero. (5) **Negative exposure is refused** (`ResearchError::NegativeExposure`, an ES-09 add-only code): v1 is long-only (rule 12), and a negative row under-states the aggregate — the fail-open direction. (6) **The halt resolves per workspace**: `halted_instruments(halts, admitting_model)` — an unscoped halt matches every workspace, a hash-scoped halt only one whose admitting model's content hash is the named one (DEC-67; a pinned mandate, having no admitting model, is matched by none of them). The hash arrives as an argument, a fact read from the mandate, so the crate's narrow envelope view carries no model triple and no shared type ripples across streams F and G. (7) **The halt's application removes, and only removes**: `apply_operator_halts(halted, entries, universe)` errors on an unread universe (a silently dropped halt is fail-open, the same reading admission takes), returns a pinned universe unchanged with an empty journal (MI-20's mirror: a pinned mandate has no research flow to stop), and on a dynamic universe removes every halted instrument the entries hold — journaled `UniverseChanged` (removed, `operator_halt`) citing the entry's thesis and lineage (journal spec §9's row requires both ids for every reason), `universe_size_after` sinking by one per removal, in instrument order, the entries read once each exactly as `expire_theses` reads them (duplicates refused), the restriction the exits-only `RemovedInstrument` (§2.2, §5.9), and a halted instrument the entries do not hold removing and journaling nothing (check 9 already holds its admissions). "Never permits anything a workspace's own limits deny" is structural: the application produces removals only. (8) **Isolation is representable and pinned at this crate's seams** (DEC-09): no entry point takes two workspaces' state except the monitor, whose report no admission, fold, or halt input accepts; a halt crosses workspace boundaries only as each workspace's own resolved halt set, derived from its own journaled `PlatformOperatorAction`. The gate-level two-workspace run the acceptance clause names lands with the deployment wiring story — the gate is stream G's under an open claim and structurally single-workspace — and the stagger is already two-workspace (MC-N23: `ws_a`/`th-1` = 317 against `ws_b`/`th-1` = 682). (9) **No spec, schema, or reference case moves**: journal spec v0.4 already carries `research_thesis_halt`; family N covers check 9 and the stagger; the monitor and the removal have no case family, like E17-5's detector and E17-8's evaluator | Accepted (agent, under DEC-79 — new pure behaviour, no spec, schema, or reason-code registry outside `ResearchError`'s add-only codes, no trading-path change; the halt's removal rides §2.2's exits-only semantics and §5.9's existing restriction, and the gate's `not_in_working_universe` denial already stops openings in a removed instrument) | DEC-100 and DEC-123 fix the controls and the thresholds; the fail-safe readings (missing ADV alerts, the unread universe errors, the pinned universe untouched, removal-only) follow AGENTS.md rule 3; DEC-09's isolation is made structural by the signatures rather than asserted | Counting every workspace's gross exposure (puts a cross-workspace control on agents the bring-your-own-strategy sentence holds out of it); refusing the whole report on a missing ADV (one gap would blind the monitor); the monitor halting by itself (DEC-123 bars it: one tenant's state would change another's decisions); the halt set read from another workspace's state (DEC-09); touching the gate or the validator (stream G's and stream F's open claims); the halt's lifetime decided here (the operator service's, outside this crate) |
| DEC-294 | **E17-6's correlated-flow controls as code: the readings the implementation takes where DEC-293 and the frozen tests leave a choice open** (DEC-293's design, DEC-77 stage 2; [task brief](tasks/E17-6-operator-flow-monitor.md); claim [#132](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/132), stream J). (1) **The sums, the arms, and the share are `mandate-num`'s own arithmetic, nothing new.** The 1% arm is one exact product `adv × 0.01` through `Usd::times_fraction` — a factor, never a rounded quotient — the dollar arm is the canonical constant `1000000` (DEC-123 fixes both; making them fields is DEC-295's founder question), the threshold is their `min` compared strictly above, and the share is `Usd::ratio_to(exposure, adv, 12, half_even)`, the same one-rounding-of-one-quotient call the `score` module's window return rides (DEC-282 item 4's precedent). The mode is unpinned by any case — `1 ÷ 3` rounds down at its 13th digit under every mode — so half-even is the house default taken, not a case's answer. (2) **A negative exposure row is refused wherever it appears, a pinned agent's rows included:** the row never enters the aggregate, but a caller bug the monitor swallows is the fail-open direction, and v1 is long-only whatever the mandate's kind (rule 12). A negative **ADV** is caller garbage the monitor neither refuses nor reads as quiet: its 1% arm is negative, so every exposure alerts above it and the row shows its own share, rather than hiding behind a second error code DEC-293 scoped away. (3) **The contributing workspaces are collected in id order**, each once (a `BTreeSet` drained into the row): "in order" is the id order every ordered collection in this crate uses (ES-21), not the agents' arrival order, which no fixture distinguishes. (5) **`universe_size_after` sinks from the input universe's own size**, one per removal in instrument order, so the last removal's size is exactly the outcome universe's size and the journal replays exactly. (6) **Duplicate entries are refused before the pinned branch:** a malformed list is a caller bug whatever the mandate's kind, and the pinned path's "returned unchanged" answers a well-formed list only. | DEC-302 | **Journal spec v0.7 §9.2's `AccountSnapshotRecorded` gains `risk_clock`** (a DEC-261 follow-up; E7-10, stream L, claim [#171](https://github.com/kunwarshivam/mandate/issues/171); DEC-302 to DEC-304 are stream L's per its dispatch). (3) **Stream K's writer row grows.** The executor writes `risk_clock` as integer seconds (`payload::clock`) on every event, so the fee-step fix also writes the timestamp form. `AccountSnapshotRecorded` stays unregistered until that lands (DEC-261 item 7). |---|---|---|---|
| ~~OD-01~~ | ~~First exchange~~ | — | **Resolved by DEC-23** (Alpaca first, Kraken Derivatives US second) |
| OD-02 | First fast decision model: Laya (self-hosted) or Jev (hosted, early access) | Phase 3 (P1 in v1) | Bake-off on labeled financial decisions: accuracy, calibration, latency, cost |
| OD-03 | Adopt NautilusTrader connectors later, or keep our own | Phase 3 | LGPL review; connector coverage |
| OD-04 | Minimum paper-trading duration before live | M9 | Design-partner feedback; risk appetite |
| OD-05 | Mobile access to on-site approval services: customer VPN, end-to-end encrypted relay, or both | M10 | Design-partner security requirements |
| OD-06 | Minimum identity data held by the global control plane in hybrid mode | M8 | Privacy review; billing needs |
| OD-07 | Whether Kraken's self-service demo environment covers the US (Bitnomial-listed) perpetual contracts, or a UAT account is needed | Phase 3 | Kraken documentation and support |
| ~~OD-08~~ | ~~Alpaca day-trading regime; cash accounts~~ | — | **Resolved:** Alpaca applies the intraday margin rule since June 4, 2026; all Alpaca accounts are margin accounts (1× below 2,000 USD equity or by setting) |
| ~~OD-09~~ | ~~Fee rates and rounding~~ | — | **Resolved in approach:** SEC (sells), TAF (sells; cap per execution by default, per order configurable pending spec §15 Q6), CAT (buys and sells), accrued per fill and charged as a daily total rounded up; rates transcribed from Alpaca's fee schedule into effective-dated configuration at M3 |
| ~~OD-10~~ | ~~Close-window deferral~~ | — | **Resolved by DEC-70** |
| ~~OD-11~~ | ~~Ban code comments~~ | — | **Resolved by DEC-80** |
| OD-12 | Robinhood Agentic Trading: whether one platform may act for many customers, rate limits, and the MCP tool contract. **Resolved in part (2026-09-26, from Robinhood's support article):** access is self-serve, with no waitlist or developer registration; a customer with a primary individual investing account in good standing connects any MCP client to `https://agent.robinhood.com/mcp/trading` on a desktop, authenticates with their Robinhood login, and is prompted to open the agentic account (one of at most 10 individual accounts). The agent then reads **every** Robinhood account of the customer, including account numbers, positions, balances, transactions, and watchlists, and trades stocks in the agentic account and crypto through a matching Robinhood Crypto account (not in New York; no transfer, staking, or lending). Robinhood states the customer is responsible for the agent's trades. Open: rate limits, the tool contract, and platform-scale terms. Also open: whether the MCP tools accept a client order ID, whether retries with the same ID are idempotent, and whether an order can be queried by that ID; these decide whether journal-before-acting and crash reconciliation (E7-2, E7-3) are possible over MCP. Robinhood has no paper environment, so the go-live path for Robinhood users (E10-4 requires a paper run) needs a founder decision: the paper stage on the simulated broker with Robinhood's rules, on Alpaca paper, or both ([design questions](09-mandate-rewrite-questions.md) 8) | Before the Robinhood connector story (M8) | Robinhood support article "Agentic Trading overview"; compliance question 32 |

## Reserved identifiers

**No new `DEC-` rows** ([DEC-344](decisions/DEC-344.md)): a decision number is reserved by adding
`decisions/DEC-<n>.md`. The table stays the registry for `OD-`, ADR, epic and story identifiers,
and the record of the `DEC-` reservations made before DEC-344.

For `OD-`, ADR, epic and story identifiers, this is the single registry for identifiers minted in
unmerged work
([coordination playbook](../../.cursor/skills/mandate-mode/playbooks/coordination.md) §3). Take the
next integer for the prefix after the highest here or in the documents on `main`, whichever is
larger. A released reservation keeps its row and its number is never reused.

Two notes on the `DEC-` rows recorded here before DEC-344: DEC-200 to DEC-249 were reserved ahead
of the sequence for the web UI, so the other numbers ran on below that block and jumped past it;
and DEC-153 was skipped in the sequence: no surviving row, commit, or claim names what
it reserved, so the number is retired rather than reused (recorded 2026-09-30 during the docs
sync; the gap was found by counting).
